P·L LIBER NUMERI 113

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 113 ·

LIBER NUMERI

One book of the Glossa ordinaria — the whole gloss, book by book.

PL 113, coll. 379–446  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  New English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Anselm of Laon and his school

Anselmus Laudunensis et schola

twelfth century

The masters of the cathedral school of Laon, gathered around Anselm (c. 1050–1117), to whom the making of the Glossa ordinaria on the Bible is traditionally credited. The gloss is a school's work rather than one man's: it draws its comment from the Fathers — Rabanus, Isidore, Bede, Jerome, Gregory — and names them as it goes, and it grew by accretion over generations. Migne prints it under the name of Walafrid Strabo, an ascription now known to be mistaken.

Hebraice VAIEDABBER ( וידבר ), id est : ET LOCUTUS EST.

In Hebrew VAIEDABBER ( וידבר ), that is: AND HE SPOKE.

PRAEFATIO.

  • 379B

Liber iste, et ordine historiae et mysterio, quarto loco ponitur in lege. Ordine historiae, quia in Levitico, qui tertius est, agitur de utensilibus tabernaculi, et de ordine Levitarum et sacrificii: hic autem de dedicatione tabernaculi agitur, quid scilicet de Levitis, quid de utensilibus tabernaculi portare debeant: mysterio hoc ostenditur. Excubare circa tabernaculum depositum, ut dicitur, ternis tribubus in singulis quartis: per quod signantur duodecim apostoli per quadripertitum orbem fidem sanctae Trinitatis docuisse. Sed etiam inter figuras quadratura nihil firmius est, quaternarii quoque operis perfectio commendatur. Quarto die facta sunt luminaria (Gen. I), quarta aetas sub David fuit robustissima, 379Cquarta etiam aetas hominis fortissima, quatuor tantum recepti sunt qui scripserunt Evangelia. Inter dona Spiritus sancti fortitudo, et inter poenitentiales psalmos, Miserere mei, Deus, quartus est, in quo de humilitate (per quam sumus fortes) agitur. Inter nomina Domini quartum est, fortis, praecedunt admirabilis, consiliarius, Deus; sequitur Pater futuri saeculi, princeps pacis (Isa. IX), et similia. In hoc quoque libro eorum qui numerantur fortitudo ostenditur, dum hi qui aetate et virtute bello sunt apti, numerari jubentur.

Dicitur liber Numeri, quia in eo numerantur Israelitae; tum inde, tum quia mansiones a Ramesse usque ad Jordanem. Sed quia omnia in figura contingebant illis (I Cor. X), et lex erat umbra futurorum, 379Dita legenda est historia ut non amittatur allegoria. Numerati sunt Israelitae soli, non proselyti et alienigenae qui erant inter illos: non tamen omnes, quia nec pueri nec feminae, sed tantum viri qui apti bello erant. Etenim alieni a Deo, gentilium spurcitia; vel crudelitate Judaica, vel etiam Christiani puerilem levitatem vel mollitiem femineam habentes, inter filios Dei non numerantur. Non sunt numerati in Aegypto: sed postquam transierunt mare Rubrum, post columnam ignis in mansione juxta montem Sina: quia manentes in tenebris peccatorum non sunt Dei, sed mundati baptismo et armati manus impositione. 380BMansiones merito duodecim computantur, a prima quae fuit in Ramesse usque ad Jordanem, ut per hoc signetur nos per tot gradus virtutum ascendere in coelum, quot generationibus Christus ab Abraham descendit ad Mariam.

Intentio Moysi in hoc opere est Christianum populum et ejus profectum, et ascensum a terra in coelum per Israel, et per duodecim mansiones designare. Per Israel enim Christi populum, per progressus mansionum profectus virtutum significatur.

Ordo libri talis est: primo duces populi, et populum circa tabernaculum numeratos per quadrum collocat. Levitas, a numero exceptos, ad ministerium tabernaculi destinat: deinde tabernaculum dedicatur, et modus oblationum, et catalogus mansionum 380Ccomputatur. Deinde prophetia Balaam a rege Balac ad maledicendum populum Dei inducitur. In fine sex civitates ordinantur, tres ultra, tres citra Jordanem, ad quas confugeret qui nolens sanguinem proximi effunderet. Levitae inter tabernaculum et caeteros filios Israel excubantes, divisi per Amramitas ab oriente, per Caathitas ab austro, Gersonitas ab occidente, et Meraritas ab aquilone, significant praelatos Ecclesiae, qui sunt medii inter ministros tabernaculi, quibus in temporalibus provident: et inter laicos, quibus in spiritualibus praesunt. Amramitae, id est, Moses et Aaron et horum filii, inspiciunt et involvunt tabernaculi secretiora; introducentes Caathitas, dant eis propriis humeris portanda involuta, ut videntes non moriantur. Non sunt enim propalanda 380DEcclesiae mysteria, nisi summatibus viris, id est, Mosi et Aaron, quia infirmi inde scandalizantur. Unde et liber Evangelii presbytero et diacono portatur apertus, aliis vero clausus. Gersonitae autem et Meraritae, qui sunt indigniores Caathitis, non propriis humeris, sed bobus et plaustris sua portant onera. Quia qui vult venire post me, me sequatur, quasi per mortem (Luc. IX). Et quanto magis quis vult, tanto major persecutio.

Benedicetis filiis Israel et dicetis, etc. Ab hoc loco sumpta est benedictio quam facit episcopus. Duo digiti qui elevantur juncti legem significant et sacerdotium, 381Aquae per Mosen et Aaron intelliguntur. Tres versus qui sequuntur continent illam trinam petitionem quae fit in verbis episcopi. Petitur gratia Spiritus sancti, vera salus, pax aeterna. Deinde sequitur distinctio personarum semper his verbis: Quod ipse praestare dignetur qui cum Patre et Spiritu sancto vivit, etc. In tabernaculi dedicatione oblata sunt munera a primatibus in usum ejusdem et sustentationem Levitarum, quod hodie fit cum dedicatur ecclesia aliqua. Sicut et laicorum consuetudine, mulieri cum desponsatur viro, multa dona offeruntur: et Dominus filiis Israel in Aegypto, die quo educeret, concessit argentea vasa. Et post mare Rubrum non duxit eos statim recta via contra hostes, ne territi retro abirent, sed per circumitum; deinde praecepit ne 381Btemporis re. p. t. Caute omnia provisa. Introeuntibus enim in Ecclesiam primo decor ejus ostendendus est, ut appetatur; non miseria, ut timeatur.

(ORIG., hom. I in Num.) Divinis numeris non omnes digni sunt, etc., usque ad capillos capitis appellavit.

(ID.) Illud quoque consideremus, etc., usque ad et tunc ad numerum jussu Dei populus adducitur.

Vide ergo quanta tibi transeunda, quanta toleranda, quot profectibus, quot tentationibus, quot praeliis scilicet pugnandum, quot vincendum, ut pertineas ad divinum numerum, et inter sanctas tribus numereris, et possis visitari, et per Dei sacerdotes Aaron et Mosen numerorum censibus ascribi. Suscipienda est tibi primo lex Dei, lex Spiritus ejus, offerenda 381Csacrificia, explendae purificationes, peragenda omnia quae spiritus docet.

(ORIG., ibid.) Ratio quoque tribuum, distinctio ordinum, societas familiarum, etc., usque ad et erunt hujusmodi ordines in resurrectione mortuorum, qui in hoc libro figurantur.

Illud quoque, quod quadam connexione tribuum castrorum positio et metationis ordo describitur, significat aliquem statum in resurrectione mortuorum. Quod tres ad orientem collocantur, tres ad occidentem, tres ad meridiem, et tres ultimae ad aquilonem, qui durus dicitur ventus (Eccli. XLIII); quod etiam tribus Juda, quae regalis est (Eccli. XLIV), ad orientem statuitur, ex qua Dominus ortus est, non puto otiosum: quod et Issachar huic jungitur et Zabulon, 381Det quod in quatuor partes trinus iste numerus ordinatur. Quae quamvis positionum suarum diversas habeant qualitates, omnes tamen intra Trinitatis numerum continentur: et quod per totas has quatuor partes in unum numerum colligitur, eadem Trinitas semper, quia scilicet sub uno nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus sancti censentur omnes qui, ex quatuor partibus orbis venientes, invocant nomen Domini, recumbentes cum Abraham, Isaac et Jacob in regno Dei (Matth. VIII). Haec generaliter diximus, volentes hujus libri continentiam mysticam breviter comprehendere: et suscepta occasione unusquisque in reliquis similem, aut si cui plus Deus revelat, superiorem prosequatur imaginem. Ego enim 382Avere me imparem judico ad enarranda mysteria libri Numerorum, multo autem inferiorem ad illa quae concludit Deuteronomii volumen.

(RAB. in Num. ) Numerorum libri multiplici mysteriorum obscuritate involutum Patres ostendunt. Ibi enim numerus filiorum Israel mystice describitur, et singularum tribuum castra circa tabernaculum testimonii ad formam ecclesiae locantur. Ibi Levitarum in ministerium distributio, et oblatio principum secundum convenientiam sacramentorum. Tandem omnium mansionum ab Aegypto usque ad terram promissionis catalogus numeratur, ut monstretur qualiter de Aegypto spiritali egressi, per mare Rubrum et eremum praesentis saeculi properemus ad promissam patriam in coelis.

382B(ID.) Juxta finem Exodi scriptum est, etc., usque ad quia legem dat ad instructionem credentium, id est spiritualium Israelitarum.

(ISID. in Num., tom. V.) Liber Numeri appellatur, etc., usque ad quae pleno gurgite fluens spiritus sancti gratia redundavit.

(ID.) Prima mansio est, etc., usque ad et baculos in manibus tenentes.

(ID.) Secunda mansio, etc., usque ad et ad sanctam terram viventium accelerandum.

(ID.) Tertia mansio, etc., usque ad ut ducibus ad terram promissionis perveniamus.

(ID.) Quarta mansio, etc., usque ad Mariam praecinentem in tympanis.

(ID.) Quinta est, etc., usque ad iterum suavitas 382Ccompensetur.

(ID.) Sexta est mansio, etc., usque ad duces ovium, doctores gentium.

(ID.) Septima mansio est, etc., usque ad et praeterita discrimina poni ante oculos.

(ID.) Octava, etc., usque ad grande odium meremur inimici.

(ID.) Nona, etc., usque ad incipimus in sacramenta pulsare.

(ID.) Decima, etc., usque ad angelorum panis manducatur.

(ID.) Undecima, etc., usque ad Caeterae in suis locis convenientius exponentur.

PREFACE.

  • 379B

This book, both by the order of the history and by its mystery, is set in the fourth place in the law. By the order of the history, because in Leviticus, which is the third, there is treated of the furnishings of the tabernacle, and of the order of the Levites and of the sacrifice: but here there is treated of the dedication of the tabernacle, namely what the Levites, and what of the furnishings of the tabernacle, they ought to carry: by mystery this is shown. To keep watch about the tabernacle set down, as is said, by three tribes on each of the four sides: by which it is signified that the twelve apostles taught the faith of the holy Trinity throughout the fourfold world. But among figures too nothing is firmer than the square, and the perfection of a work of four is likewise commended. On the fourth day the lights were made (Gen. I), the fourth age under David was the most robust, 379Cthe fourth age of man too is the strongest, only four were received who wrote the Gospels. Among the gifts of the Holy Spirit, fortitude; and among the penitential psalms, Have mercy upon me, O God, is the fourth, in which there is treated of humility (through which we are strong). Among the names of the Lord the fourth is strong; there go before wonderful, counsellor, God; there follows Father of the world to come, prince of peace (Isa. IX), and the like. In this book too the fortitude of those who are numbered is shown, since those who by age and by strength are fit for war are commanded to be numbered.

It is called the book of Numbers, because in it the Israelites are numbered; partly from that, and partly because of the stations from Rameses as far as the Jordan. But because all these things happened to them in figure (I Cor. X), and the law was a shadow of things to come, 379Dthe history is so to be read that the allegory be not lost. The Israelites alone were numbered, not the proselytes and the strangers who were among them: yet not all of them, because neither boys nor women, but only the men who were fit for war. For indeed those estranged from God by the filthiness of the Gentiles, or by Jewish cruelty, or even Christians having a boyish levity or a womanish softness, are not numbered among the sons of God. They were not numbered in Egypt, but after they had passed the Red Sea, after the pillar of fire, in the station beside Mount Sinai: because those remaining in the darkness of sins are not God's, but those cleansed by baptism and armed by the imposition of the hand. 380BThe stations are with reason reckoned twelve, from the first, which was at Rameses, as far as the Jordan, that by this it may be signified that we ascend into heaven by as many steps of the virtues as the generations by which Christ came down from Abraham to Mary.

The intention of Moses in this work is to designate the Christian people and its advance, and its ascent from earth into heaven, by Israel and by the twelve stations. For by Israel the people of Christ is signified, by the progress of the stations the advance of the virtues.

The order of the book is this: first he places the leaders of the people, and the people numbered about the tabernacle, in a square. The Levites, exempted from the numbering, he appoints to the ministry of the tabernacle: then the tabernacle is dedicated, and the manner of the oblations, and the catalogue of the stations, 380Cis reckoned. Then the prophecy of Balaam is brought in by king Balac to curse the people of God. At the end six cities are appointed, three beyond and three on this side of the Jordan, to which he might flee who unwillingly shed the blood of his neighbour. The Levites, keeping watch between the tabernacle and the rest of the sons of Israel, divided into Amramites on the east, Caathites on the south, Gersonites on the west, and Merarites on the north, signify the prelates of the Church, who are in the middle between the ministers of the tabernacle, for whom they provide in temporal things, and the laity, over whom they preside in spiritual things. The Amramites, that is, Moses and Aaron and their sons, look upon and wrap up the more secret things of the tabernacle; and bringing in the Caathites, they give them the wrapped things to be carried on their own shoulders, that they may not die by seeing them. For the mysteries 380Dof the Church are not to be published abroad save to the chief men, that is, to Moses and Aaron, because the weak are scandalized thereby. Whence also the book of the Gospel is carried open for the priest and the deacon, but closed for the rest. But the Gersonites and the Merarites, who are less worthy than the Caathites, carry their burdens not on their own shoulders, but with oxen and waggons. Because he who will come after me, let him follow me, as it were through death (Luc. IX). And the more a man wills it, the greater the persecution.

You shall bless the children of Israel, and shall say, etc. From this place is taken the blessing which the bishop makes. The two fingers which are raised joined together signify the law and the priesthood, 381Awhich are understood by Moses and Aaron. The three verses which follow contain that threefold petition which is made in the words of the bishop. There is asked the grace of the Holy Spirit, true salvation, eternal peace. Then follows the distinction of the persons, always in these words: Which may he vouchsafe to grant who liveth with the Father and the Holy Spirit, etc. At the dedication of the tabernacle gifts were offered by the chief men for the use of the same and for the support of the Levites, which is done today when any church is dedicated. Just as also by the custom of laymen, when a woman is betrothed to a man, many gifts are offered: and the Lord granted vessels of silver to the children of Israel in Egypt on the day when he brought them out. And after the Red Sea he did not lead them at once by the straight way against their enemies, lest being terrified they should go back, but round about; then he commanded ne 381Btemporis re. p. t. All things were cautiously provided for. For to those entering into the Church her comeliness must first be shown, that she may be desired; not her wretchedness, that she may be feared.

(ORIG., hom. I in Num.) Not all are worthy of the divine numbers, etc., as far as he called the hairs of the head.

(ID.) Let us consider this also, etc., as far as and then at the command of God the people is brought to the number.

See therefore how much thou must pass through, how much thou must endure, with how many advances, how many temptations, how many battles thou must fight, how much thou must conquer, that thou mayest belong to the divine number, and be numbered among the holy tribes, and be able to be visited, and be enrolled in the censuses of the numbers by God's priests Aaron and Moses. Thou must first take up the law of God, the law of his Spirit; there must be offered 381Csacrifices, purifications must be fulfilled, all things must be performed which the spirit teaches.

(ORIG., ibid.) The reckoning also of the tribes, the distinction of the orders, the fellowship of the families, etc., as far as and there will be orders of this kind in the resurrection of the dead, which are figured in this book.

This also, that by a certain connection of the tribes the position of the camps and the order of the measuring out is described, signifies some state in the resurrection of the dead. That three are placed to the east, three to the west, three to the south, and the last three to the north, which is called a harsh wind (Eccli. XLIII); that also the tribe of Juda, which is the royal one (Eccli. XLIV), is set to the east, from which the Lord arose, I do not think idle: nor that Issachar is joined to it, and Zabulon, 381Dnor that this threefold number is arranged into four parts. Which, although they have diverse qualities of their positions, are yet all contained within the number of the Trinity: and what is gathered through all these four parts into one number is always the same Trinity, because, namely, under the one name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit are reckoned all who, coming from the four parts of the world, call upon the name of the Lord, sitting down with Abraham, Isaac and Jacob in the kingdom of God (Matth. VIII). These things we have said in general, wishing briefly to comprehend the mystical content of this book: and, the occasion once taken up, let each one pursue in the rest a like image, or, if God reveal more to any, a higher one. For I 382Atruly judge myself unequal to the setting forth of the mysteries of the book of Numbers, and much more inferior to those which the volume of Deuteronomy concludes.

(RAB. in Num. ) The Fathers show the book of Numbers wrapped in a manifold obscurity of mysteries. For there the number of the children of Israel is mystically described, and the camps of the several tribes are placed about the tabernacle of the testimony after the form of the Church. There is the distribution of the Levites unto the ministry, and the oblation of the princes according to the fitness of the sacraments. At last the catalogue of all the stations from Egypt as far as the land of promise is numbered, that it may be shown how we, having gone forth from a spiritual Egypt, hasten through the Red Sea and the desert of the present world to the fatherland promised in the heavens.

382B(ID.) Near the end of Exodus it is written, etc., as far as because he gives the law for the instruction of believers, that is, of spiritual Israelites.

(ISID. in Num., tom. V.) It is called the book of Numbers, etc., as far as which the grace of the Holy Spirit, flowing with a full flood, has overflowed.

(ID.) The first station is, etc., as far as and holding staves in their hands.

(ID.) The second station, etc., as far as and that we must hasten to the holy land of the living.

(ID.) The third station, etc., as far as that with [such] leaders we may come to the land of promise.

(ID.) The fourth station, etc., as far as Mary leading the song upon timbrels.

(ID.) The fifth is, etc., as far as that sweetness may again 382Cbe made good.

(ID.) The sixth is a station, etc., as far as leaders of the sheep, teachers of the Gentiles.

(ID.) The seventh station is, etc., as far as and that past perils be set before the eyes.

(ID.) The eighth, etc., as far as we earn the great hatred of the enemy.

(ID.) The ninth, etc., as far as we begin to knock at the sacraments.

(ID.) The tenth, etc., as far as the bread of angels is eaten.

(ID.) The eleventh, etc., as far as The rest will be more fitly expounded in their own places.

CAPUT PRIMUM.

  • 382D

VERS. 3.-- Omnium virorum. (ORIG., hom. 1 in Num.) Alias LXX: Omnis qui procedit in virtute Israel, etc. Qui ergo procedit, etc., usque ad per fidem evangelicam et apostolicam traditur.

VERS. 4.-- Principes tribuum, etc. (AUG., quaest. 1 in Num.) Quid est quod singulos de singulis tribubus eligi jubet principes, etc., usque ad ut vocentur χιλίαρχοι. .

VERS. 5.-- De tribu Ruben. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Moraliter Ruben primogenitus Israelis, etc., usque ad cor unum et anima una.

(RAB., ibid.) Princeps tribus Ruben eos generaliter significat, etc., usque ad continentium, virginum, et utriusque sexus curam gerens.

383AVERS. 6.-- De tribu Simeon. (RAB.) Simeon generaliter omnes obedientes, etc., usque ad dicentes: Ad te levavi oculos meos, qui habitas in coelis (Psal. CXXII).

VERS. 7.-- De tribu Juda. (ID.) Possunt significari in Juda omnes qui recte invocant nomen Domini, nec sensu nec verbo dissentientes a norma fidei: quorum merito Naasson princeps est, qui scilicet non solum alios praedicando instruit, sed etiam virulenta vitiorum jacula corpus suum castigando in semetipso interficit, secundum illud: Castigo corpus meum et in servitutem redigo, etc. (I Cor. IX).

Allegorice, Juda, de quo reges, qui interpretatur confessio vel laudatio, hic populum significat Ecclesiae, cujus caput est leo de tribu Juda, id est, Christus. 383BVere enim fidei laudatio vel confessio, id est, recta praedicatio, tantum in Ecclesia est. Naasson ergo, id est serpentinus, vel augurium, filius Aminadab, scilicet spontanei populi, Salvator intelligitur de patriarchis, Deo devotis famulis, genitus, qui per mortem suam destruxit diabolum. In serpente enim, unde primum mortis causa, id est peccatum, processit, mors figuratur. Unde: Sicut exaltavit Moyses serpentem in deserto, sic exaltari oportet filium hominis, etc. (Joan. III).

VERS. 8.-- De tribu Issachar. (ID.) Issachar monachos non incongrue exprimit, qui, omnibus mundi negotiis spretis, dono Dei, id est Spiritu sancto protegente, praemium aeternum districta vita, voluptate mortificata, quaerunt, et pompam mundi 383Ccalcantes, humilia amplectentes, soli Deo placere cupiunt.

Nathanael filius Suar. Qui pie exercent se ut mercedem in coelis accipiant, eorum princeps est Nathanael, id est Deus meus vel donum Dei, Filius Suar, id est pusilli Christi, scilicet qui inter homines pusillus apparuit: ipse enim dux et ipse merces.

VERS. 9.-- De tribu Zabulon. (ID.) Zabulon patientes figurat, etc., usque ad contra haereticos scilicet et schismaticos.

VERS. 10.-- Joseph. Idem Joseph, pater duarum tribuum, Christum significat, Patrem populorum duorum et lapidem angularem. Ephraim minor, Manasse primogenito praelatus, gentilem populum 383DJudaico praelatum. Unde: Amen dico vobis, non inveni tantam fidem in Israel (Matth. VIII).

Elisama. Elisama Christum significat, de quo dicitur: Factus est obediens Patri usque ad mortem, etc. (Philip. II). Qui de se ait: Quae audivi ab eo haec loquor in mundo (Joan. VIII). Hic est filius Ammiud, id est populi inclyti, de stirpe scilicet regum, patriarcharum et prophetarum.

(RAB.) Elisama princeps in tribu Ephraim, significat eos qui attente verbum Dei audiunt corde bono et optimo, et fructum afferunt in patientia.

Manasses, etc. Obliviosus, ea scilicet quae retro sunt obliviscens et in anteriora se extendens: ut accipiat coronam vitae, quam retribuet Deus diligentibus se (Philip. III; Jac. I).

384AGamaliel. Gamaliel, reddidit mihi Deus, filius Phadassur, id est redemptionis validae, princeps est in tribu Manasse, qui interpretatur oblitus, vel obliviosus: cum Christus (quem ad vocandum populum Judaicum Pater in mundum misit) in regno suo gentem obliviosam invenit. Oblitus est enim Israel creatoris sui. Non tamen omnes perierunt, sed cum plenitudo gentium intraverit, omnis Israel salvus fiet (Rom. XI).

VERS. 11.-- De tribu Benjamin. Benjamin Jerusalem significat, quae est in tribu sua: cujus populus, secundum prophetiam Rachel, Bennoni, id est filius doloris fuit, quia gravi dolore matrem affecit. Sanguinem enim prophetarum fudit, et in Christi necem conspirans ait: Sanguis ejus super nos et 384Bsuper filios nostros (Matth. XXVII). In credentibus vero, juxta patris prophetiam, filius dextrae. Benjamin quoque gentilem populum potest significare, qui filius doloris fuit Christianos persequendo. Filius etiam dextrae potest dici, quia in judicio ad dexteram Dei erit, vel quia dextera, id est bona opera, facit. Hujus populi Abidan, id est pater meus, judex, filius Gedeonis, qui interpretatur tentatio iniquitatis vel humilitatis; princeps est: quia Christus (quem humilem Judaeus sprevit et diabolus inique tentavit) caput est credentium, et judex venturus vivorum et mortuorum. Moraliter, Benjamin, filius dextrae, significat eos qui fortiter pugnant contra inimicos, et fortiter premunt tentationis stimulos, nec femineum aliquid vel molle in se dominari permittunt.

384CVERS. 12.-- De tribu Dan. Dan judicium, vel judicans. Hi sunt qui omnia discrete agunt, qui Abiezer, id est fratrem adjutorem, sibi principem constituunt: quia secundum Patrum regulam omnia faciunt, vel omnia bona sua Deo tribuunt.

(RAB., ubi supra.) Dan, etc., usque ad ad societatem spiritalis gratiae hortantur.

VERS. 13.-- De tribu Aser. (ID.) Aser, etc., usque ad retribuetur enim eis in resurrectione justorum.

VERS. 14.-- De tribu Gad. (ID.) Allegorice, etc., usque ad unde: Gallus succinctus lumbos et aries, nec est rex qui resistat ei (Prov. XXX).

VERS. 15.-- De tribu Nephthali. Nephthali, etc., usque ad unde: Jam non dicam vos servos, sed amieos, 384Detc. (Joan. XV).

VERS. 16.-- Per cognationes. (AUG., quaest. 2 in Num.) Merito quaeritur quare, etc., usque ad et novum quatuor Evangeliis.

VERS. 18.-- A vicesimo. (ISID.) A vicesimo anno numerantur, qui ad bellum eliguntur, quia ab hac aetate contra unumquemque vitiorum bella nascuntur. Ideoque ad pugnam eliguntur, ut pugnent contra libidinem, ne luxuria superentur.

VERS. 37.-- Triginta quinque millia. Boni conjugati, quia ad significandum tricenarium index et pollex blando osculo continguntur. Unde aliud tricesimum, aliud sexagesimum, aliud vero centesimum.

VERS. 39.-- Septingenti. Spiritalibus donis provecti, et ad dexteram Dei locandi. Centenarius enim 385Anumerus primus in dexteram computatur, pollice et indice in coronam flexis; unde: Jam dextera computat annos.

(RAB. in Num., tom. II.) In tribu Levi, etc., usque ad quasi humeris laboris et patientiae Ecclesiam portant.

VERS. 51.-- Cumque proficiscendum, etc. Id est, sermone et exemplo disponent, quomodo quisque humiliatus in profectu virtutum gradiatur.

Quisquis externorum, etc. (AUG., quaest. 3.) Alienigena qui accesserit, moriatur. Hic alienigena intelligendus est etiam de illis filiis Israel, etc., usque ad ut allophyli appellentur quasi aliarum tribuum homines.

385BAllegorice. Qui ad sacrum ministerium indignus accedit, poenam praesumptionis non evadit: Nemo enim debet sibi sumere honorem, nisi qui vocatur a Deo, tanquam Aaron (Hebr. V). Unde Ozias, qui sacerdotium usurpavit, lepra percussus est.

VERS. 52.-- Unusquisque per turmas, etc. (ORIG., hom. 2 in Num.) Unusquisque secundum signa sua, etc., usque ad si nihil inquietum, nihil inordinatum, nihil inhonestum inveniatur in nobis.

CHAPTER ONE.

  • 382D

VERS. 3.-- Of all the men. (ORIG., hom. 1 in Num.) Otherwise the LXX: Every one who goeth forth in the strength of Israel, etc. He therefore who goeth forth, etc., as far as it is handed down by the evangelical and apostolic faith.

VERS. 4.-- The princes of the tribes, etc. (AUG., quaest. 1 in Num.) What is it that he commands single princes to be chosen out of the single tribes, etc., as far as that they be called χιλίαρχοι.

VERS. 5.-- Of the tribe of Ruben. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Morally, Ruben the firstborn of Israel, etc., as far as one heart and one soul.

(RAB., ibid.) The prince of the tribe of Ruben signifies them generally, etc., as far as bearing the care of the continent, of virgins, and of either sex.

383AVERS. 6.-- Of the tribe of Simeon. (RAB.) Simeon generally [signifies] all the obedient, etc., as far as saying: Unto thee have I lifted up mine eyes, who dwellest in the heavens (Psal. CXXII).

VERS. 7.-- Of the tribe of Juda. (ID.) There can be signified in Juda all who rightly call upon the name of the Lord, dissenting neither in sense nor in word from the rule of faith: of whom Naasson is with reason the prince, who, namely, not only instructs others by preaching, but also slays in himself the venomous darts of the vices by chastising his own body, according to that: I chastise my body and bring it into subjection, etc. (I Cor. IX).

Allegorically, Juda, from whom the kings come, who is interpreted confession or praise, here signifies the people of the Church, whose head is the lion of the tribe of Juda, that is, Christ. 383BFor truly the praise or confession of faith, that is, right preaching, is only in the Church. Naasson therefore, that is serpentine, or augury, the son of Aminadab, namely of a willing people, is understood as the Saviour, begotten of the patriarchs, servants devoted to God, who by his own death destroyed the devil. For in the serpent, whence first the cause of death, that is sin, proceeded, death is figured. Whence: As Moses lifted up the serpent in the desert, so must the Son of man be lifted up, etc. (Joan. III).

VERS. 8.-- Of the tribe of Issachar. (ID.) Issachar not unfittingly expresses the monks, who, having despised all the business of the world, by the gift of God, that is, with the Holy Spirit protecting them, seek the eternal reward by a strict life, pleasure being mortified, and trampling the pomp of the world 383Cunder foot, embracing lowly things, they desire to please God alone.

Nathanael the son of Suar. Of those who piously exercise themselves that they may receive a reward in the heavens, the prince is Nathanael, that is my God or the gift of God, the son of Suar, that is, of the little Christ, namely who appeared little among men: for he himself is both leader and reward.

VERS. 9.-- Of the tribe of Zabulon. (ID.) Zabulon figures the patient, etc., as far as namely against heretics and schismatics.

VERS. 10.-- Joseph. The same Joseph, the father of two tribes, signifies Christ, the Father of two peoples and the corner stone. Ephraim the younger, preferred to Manasse the firstborn, [signifies] the Gentile people 383Dpreferred to the Jewish. Whence: Amen I say to you, I have not found so great faith in Israel (Matth. VIII).

Elisama. Elisama signifies Christ, of whom it is said: He was made obedient to the Father even unto death, etc. (Philip. II) [Vulg. factus obediens usque ad mortem; the received text has no "to the Father"]. Who says of himself: The things which I have heard from him, these I speak in the world (Joan. VIII). This is the son of Ammiud, that is of a renowned people, namely of the stock of kings, of patriarchs and of prophets.

(RAB.) Elisama, the prince in the tribe of Ephraim, signifies those who attentively hear the word of God with a good and best heart, and bring forth fruit in patience.

Manasses, etc. Forgetful, namely those which are behind forgetting, and stretching himself forth unto the things before: that he may receive the crown of life, which God will repay to them that love him (Philip. III; Jac. I). [Vulg. quam repromisit Deus diligentibus se, "which God hath promised"]

384AGamaliel. Gamaliel, God hath rendered to me, the son of Phadassur, that is of a strong redemption, is the prince in the tribe of Manasse, which is interpreted forgotten, or forgetful: since Christ (whom the Father sent into the world to call the Jewish people) found in his kingdom a forgetful nation. For Israel has forgotten his creator. Yet not all have perished, but when the fulness of the Gentiles shall have come in, all Israel shall be saved (Rom. XI).

VERS. 11.-- Of the tribe of Benjamin. Benjamin signifies Jerusalem, which is in his own tribe: whose people, according to the prophecy of Rachel, was Bennoni, that is the son of sorrow, because it afflicted its mother with a heavy sorrow. For it shed the blood of the prophets, and conspiring in the slaying of Christ said: His blood be upon us and 384Bupon our children (Matth. XXVII). But in those that believe, according to the prophecy of his father, the son of the right hand. Benjamin also can signify the Gentile people, which was a son of sorrow by persecuting Christians. It can also be called the son of the right hand, because in the judgment it will be at the right hand of God, or because it does right-hand things, that is, good works. Of this people Abidan, that is my father, a judge, the son of Gedeon, who is interpreted the temptation of iniquity or of humility; is the prince: because Christ (whom the Jew despised as lowly and the devil wickedly tempted) is the head of believers, and the judge to come of the living and the dead. Morally, Benjamin, the son of the right hand, signifies those who fight bravely against their enemies, and bravely press down the goads of temptation, and permit nothing womanish or soft to have dominion in themselves.

384CVERS. 12.-- Of the tribe of Dan. Dan, judgment, or judging. These are they who do all things with discretion, who appoint for themselves as prince Abiezer, that is a helping brother: because they do all things according to the rule of the Fathers, or ascribe all their good things to God.

(RAB., ubi supra.) Dan, etc., as far as they are exhorted to the fellowship of spiritual grace.

VERS. 13.-- Of the tribe of Aser. (ID.) Aser, etc., as far as for it shall be repaid to them in the resurrection of the just.

VERS. 14.-- Of the tribe of Gad. (ID.) Allegorically, etc., as far as whence: A cock girded about the loins, and a ram, and there is no king that resisteth him (Prov. XXX).

VERS. 15.-- Of the tribe of Nephthali. Nephthali, etc., as far as whence: Now I will not call you servants, but friends, 384Detc. (Joan. XV).

VERS. 16.-- By their kindreds. (AUG., quaest. 2 in Num.) With reason it is asked why, etc., as far as and the new by the four Gospels.

VERS. 18.-- From the twentieth. (ISID.) From the twentieth year are they numbered who are chosen for war, because from this age the wars of the vices are born against every man. And therefore they are chosen for the battle, that they may fight against lust, lest they be overcome by luxury.

VERS. 37.-- Thirty-five thousand. Good married folk, because to signify the number of thirty the forefinger and the thumb are touched together in a gentle kiss. Whence one is thirtyfold, another sixtyfold, and another a hundredfold.

VERS. 39.-- Seven hundred. Those advanced by spiritual gifts, and to be placed at the right hand of God. For the centenary 385Anumber is the first reckoned upon the right hand, the thumb and the forefinger being bent into a crown; whence: Now the right hand counts the years.

(RAB. in Num., tom. II.) In the tribe of Levi, etc., as far as they carry the Church as it were upon the shoulders of labour and of patience.

VERS. 51.-- And when they must set forward, etc. That is, by word and by example they shall dispose how each one, humbled, may walk in the advance of the virtues.

Whosoever of strangers, etc. (AUG., quaest. 3.) Let the stranger who shall approach die. Here the stranger is to be understood also of those sons of Israel, etc., as far as that they be called allophyli, as it were men of other tribes.

385BAllegorically. He who approaches the sacred ministry unworthily does not escape the penalty of presumption: for no man ought to take the honour to himself, unless he be called by God, as Aaron was (Hebr. V). [Vulg. Nec quisquam sumit sibi honorem, sed qui vocatur a Deo, tamquam Aaron, "nor doth any man take the honour to himself, but he that is called"] Whence Ozias, who usurped the priesthood, was struck with leprosy.

VERS. 52.-- Every man by his troops, etc. (ORIG., hom. 2 in Num.) Every man according to his own standards, etc., as far as if nothing restless, nothing disordered, nothing dishonourable be found in us.

CAPUT II.

VERS. 1.-- Locutusque est Dominus. (ORIG., hom. 2 in Num.) Homo secundum ordinem suum, etc., usque ad et nihil secundum ordinem gerere.

(ID.) Unusquisque nostrum, etc., usque ad ne 385Cpropter eum nomen Dei blasphemetur.

(ID.) Secundum sua signa, etc. Quibus scilicet cujusque proprietas designatur, etc., usque ad quanto magis reliqui homines propria signa in motibus animorum et animae virtutibus gerunt?

VERS. 2.-- Per gyrum tabernaculi, etc. (RAB. in Num.) Circa tabernaculum, etc., usque ad quorum precibus et doctrina munitur Ecclesia.

VERS. 9.-- Universi qui in castris Judae. His tribubus Judas praesidet, scilicet regalis tribus, de qua ortus est Christus, humani generis rex et salvator.

VERS. 10.-- Meridianam plagam erit princeps. Haec significat antiquam Dei plebem, quae lucem Evangelii, vel legis suscipiens, amore Conditoris fervebat.

385DVERS. 17.-- Levabitur autem tabernaculum testimonii, etc. Tabernaculi erectio vel depositio Ecclesiae situm significat, quae in credentibus erigitur, in negantibus deponitur, in quibusdam ordinibus sacris sublimatur, in aliis judicio sacerdotum excommunicatione humiliatur.

Ad occidentalem plagam erunt, etc. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Dicamus adhuc aliquid quod adjuvet lectorem, etc., usque ad ut secure possint divinum implere officium.

VERS. 18.-- Occidentalem. Haec plaga utrumque parietem tabernaculi in se recipiens, in se jungendo consummat, et consummationem Ecclesiae in fine mundi significat, quando in fructu bonorum operum 386Aprobabuntur electi, in oblivionem mortis tradentur impii, ad dexteram Dei locabuntur justi.

VERS. 25.-- Ad aquilonis partem castrametati sunt filii Dan. Haec plaga significat multitudinem gentium usque ad Christum frigore infidelitatis torpentem, sed repulsis Judaeis judicio Dei in Ecclesia dilatata aeternam beatitudinem exspectat.

CHAPTER II.

VERS. 1.-- And the Lord spoke. (ORIG., hom. 2 in Num.) A man according to his own order, etc., as far as and to do nothing according to order.

(ID.) Each one of us, etc., as far as lest 385Cbecause of him the name of God be blasphemed.

(ID.) According to their own standards, etc. By which, namely, the property of each one is designated, etc., as far as how much more do the rest of men bear their own standards in the motions of their minds and in the virtues of the soul?

VERS. 2.-- Round about the tabernacle, etc. (RAB. in Num.) About the tabernacle, etc., as far as by whose prayers and teaching the Church is fortified.

VERS. 9.-- All that were in the camp of Juda. Over these tribes Judas presides, namely the royal tribe, from which Christ arose, the king and saviour of the human race.

VERS. 10.-- On the south side shall be the prince. This signifies the ancient people of God, which, receiving the light of the Gospel or of the law, glowed with love of the Creator.

385DVERS. 17.-- But the tabernacle of the testimony shall be lifted up, etc. The raising or the setting down of the tabernacle signifies the condition of the Church, which is raised up in those who believe, is set down in those who deny, in some is exalted by sacred orders, in others is humbled by excommunication at the judgment of the priests.

They shall be on the west side, etc. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Let us say something further which may help the reader, etc., as far as that they may be able securely to fulfil the divine office.

VERS. 18.-- The west. This side, receiving into itself both walls of the tabernacle, completes them by joining them into itself, and signifies the consummation of the Church at the end of the world, when in the fruit of good works 386Athe elect shall be approved, the impious shall be delivered over to the oblivion of death, the just shall be placed at the right hand of God.

VERS. 25.-- On the north side the sons of Dan encamped. This side signifies the multitude of the Gentiles, torpid with the cold of unbelief until Christ, but, the Jews being cast off by the judgment of God, it awaits eternal blessedness, spread abroad in the Church.

CAPUT III.

VERS. 3.-- Sacerdotum qui uncti sunt, etc. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Quatuor fili Aaron, etc., usque ad igne divinae ultionis consumuntur.

VERS. 6.-- Applica tribum. (AUG., quaest. 4 in Num.) Accipe tribum Levi, etc., usque ad in aliis circumquaque castris filiorum Israel.

386BVERS. 10.-- Externus. (ID.) Alienigena qui tetigerit, morietur. Quaerendum quomodo dixerit, etc., usque ad hinc enim loquebatur.

VERS. 12.-- Ego tuli Levitas. (ID., quaest. 6.) Quid est quod Levitas, etc., usque ad nec pro his computari posteros Levitarum vel pecorum eorum.

(ORIG. hom. 3 in Num.) Ego assumpsi Levitas de medio filiorum Israel pro omni primogenito, etc. Levitae assumuntur pro primogenitis, etc., usque ad ut sit plus pro primogenito assumi, quam primogenitum nasci.

LXX: Ex medio filiorum Israel, etc. Tertius est Levi inter filios Israel, de quo ergo medio assumuntur Levitae? Invenio Sunamitem fiducialiter regi Israel respondentem: In medio populi mei ego habito 386C(IV Reg. IV). Et de Christo Joannem dicentem: Medius vestrum stat, quem vos nescitis. Puto ergo eum qui nunquam declinaverit ad dexteram vel ad sinistram, dici posse medium stare, qui peccatum non fecit, nec inventus est dolus in ore ejus (Isa. LIII). Et quia semper stat, medius stare dicitur. Qui vero imitator ejus est, sicut Sunamitis, stare quidem non dicitur, quia aliquando aut ad dexteram inclinatur, aut ad sinistram, Nemo enim mundus a sorde, nec si unius diei fuerit vita ejus (Job XV): habitare tamen dicitur in medio populi. Levitae ergo assumuntur de medio filiorum Israel. Levitae enim sunt, qui non cognoverunt dexteram et sinistram suam, sed sequentes Mosen, id est, legem Dei, non pepercerunt patri vel matri. Et tu ergo si, veniente tentatione et 386Dira peccati, nec inclineris ad dexteram vel ad sinistram, non praevariceris legem Dei, sed stes medius fixus et stabilis, nec curves genua tua peccato, nec pecudis caput, scilicet, stultitiae sequaris imaginem: assumeris de medio filiorum Israel, et in primitivorum numero collocaberis.

Aperit. Non quicunque aperuit vulvam, primatu dignus est, unde: Alienati sunt peccatores a vulva, erraverunt ab utero, locuti sunt falsa (Psal. LVII). Quomodo enim quis errare potuit a via Dei, statim ut de ventre matris exivit? Aut quomodo potuit falsa loqui nuper editus puer? Necessarium est ergo ventrem et vulvam talem requirere cui possit convenire, Alienati sunt peccatores a vulva, etc. Et illa erit 387Avulva, quam aperit omnis primitivus qui sanctificatur Domino. Aperuit Dominus vulvam Liae quae erat clausa, et peperit patriarchas; similiter et Rachel ut pareret etiam ipsa cujus visus erat perspicax et decorus aspectus (Gen. XXIX, XXX). In multis vero Scripturae locis invenies aperiri vulvas, quarum si singulas pro locis consideres, invenies quomodo errant peccatores a vulva, et alii aperientes vulvam sanctificantur in ordine primitivorum.

Ex quo. (ORIG.) Quia non prius sanctificati sunt primogeniti Israel, etc., usque ad et in primitivorum ordine collocaret.

VERS. 15.-- Numera filios Levi. (ID.) Recenseamus attentius, etc., usque ad excubiisque perpetuis divina ministeria celebrant.

387BVERS. 17.-- Levi per nomina sua. Levi additus vel assumptus. Hic est ordo doctorum, qui per gratiam Dei de mundo electus ad servitium divinum coetibus angelorum unitur. Unde: Ego vos elegi de mundo (Joan. XV). Et alibi: Sed sunt sicut angeli Dei in coelo (Matth. XXII).

Gerson et Caath et Merari. Tres filii Levi significant praedicatores sanctae Trinitatis, confessione insignes, fide, spe et charitate mirabiles. Nec huic expositioni eorum nominum interpretatio aliquo modo repugnat, sed valde concordat. Interpretatur enim Gerson advena, Caath patientia, vel molares dentes. Merari amara, vel amaritudo; minister enim Dei advenam se et peregrinum in mundo cognoscit, et 387Cpatiens est. Verbum Dei dente discretionis molit, secundum auditorum capacitates; et saeculum sibi amarum ostendens, alios a voluptatibus revocat. Hi ab uno mense numerantur, quia initium vitae Deo debent consecrare, ne post multa scelera promoti faciant blasphemare nomen Dei, unde: Oportet episcopum irreprehensibilem esse, etc. (I Tim. III.)

VERS. 23.-- Hi post tabernaculum metabuntur. Filiorum Levi ordo conveniens ostenditur, ubi scilicet qui primi sunt in ordine, orientis plagam observent Moyses et Aaron cum filiis suis habentes custodiam sanctuarii in medio filiorum Israel. Caathitae, unde Moyses et Aaron orti sunt, meridianam plagam obtineant, sub cura Eleazari sacerdotis, qui major est filiis Aaron. Post hos Gersonitae ad occidentalem 387Dplagam sub cura Eliasaph. Novissimi Meraritae ad septentrionalem plagam, sub custodia Ithamar filii Aaron minoris. In his vero moraliter exprimitur quod ministri ecclesiae debent diligenter ordinem suum servare, nec majora appetere quam proprius locus exposcit.

VERS. 24.-- Ad occidentem sub principe Eliasaph filio Lael. Ubi dies clauditur, astra occidunt. In his significatur, quod ministri tabernaculi debent concupiscentiam fugere, carnalia desideria Dei adjutorio mortificare, diem ultimum et finem laboris exspectare ut mortui mundo et viventes Deo dicere possint illud: Mihi autem absit gloriari nisi in cruce Domini nostri Jesus Christi (Gal. VI).

388AVERS. 29.-- Ad meridianam. Haec significat post lucem scientiae perceptam, praedicatorem verbum patientiae et solatii debere cum discretione proximis impendere. Sub Eleazaro, id est Dei adjutorio, secundum illud: Nisi Dominus aedificaverit domum, in vanum laboraverunt qui aedificant eam.

VERS. 35.-- In plaga. Extremi Meraritae sub Ithamar (qui interpretatur amarus, vel ubi palma) plagam aquilonis sortiti sunt, ubi glacialis horror perdurat. In quo monentur Ecclesiae ministri, ne securitate torpeant; quia licet per januam fidei jam in Ecclesiam intraverint, lumen scientiae perceperint, desideria carnis mortificaverint, nondum tamen ad perfectam suavitatem, id est, ad aeternam beatitudinem, pervenerunt. Restat aliquid amarum; 388Bcum illo certandum est, qui ad aquilonem dirigit solium suum, cujus duritiam molliri impossibile est; unde: Cor ejus indurabitur quasi lapis, etc. (Isa. XIV; Job XXI). Cum autem superabitur, adepta palma exsultandum est et laetandum.

VERS. 38.-- Ad orientalem plagam. Orientalis plaga ortum fidei significat, ubi Moyses, qui interpretatur assumptus de aqua, et Aaron mons fortitudinis, cum filiis suis castrametantur. Qui enim in Ecclesia praesunt, quibus claves regni coelestis commissae sunt (Matth. VI), debent introitus ordinem cognoscere, et aliis praedicare. Quia oportet quemque in lucem fidei per portam confessionis ad aquam baptismi intrare, ut de aqua assumptus sit filius adoptionis; et robore constantiae gignens filios boni 388Coperis, introitum fidei, per quem in Ecclesiam ingressus est, diligenter custodiat, ne otiosus et inutilis abjiciatur.

VERS. 39.-- Viginti duo millia. Apte numero viginti duorum Levitae intitulantur. Hoc enim numero comprehenduntur Hebraea elementa: et, secundum quosdam, libri Veteris Testamenti. Ministri Dei hac praescriptione monentur, secundum legem Dei vivere et docere, nec ullo modo excedere.

(ORIG., hom. 4 in Num.) Multae sunt numerorum differentiae, etc. usque ad etiam mysterium humanae generationis exponitur.

VERS. 47.-- Quinque siclos. Mystice, quisquis initium conversationis et voluntatis vult Deo consecrare, quinque sensus corporis debet diligenter custodire, 388Dne per lasciviam in errorem aut immunda desideria defluat. Sed juxta siclum sanctuarii, qui est oboli 20, id est secundum decalogi doctrinam, quae in duobus praeceptis charitatis consistit, omnia opera sua referat in domum summi sacerdotis et filiorum ejus.

CHAPTER III.

VERS. 3.-- Of the priests that were anointed, etc. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) The four sons of Aaron, etc., as far as they are consumed by the fire of divine vengeance.

VERS. 6.-- Bring the tribe. (AUG., quaest. 4 in Num.) Take the tribe of Levi, etc., as far as in the other camps round about of the children of Israel.

386BVERS. 10.-- The stranger. (ID.) The stranger that shall touch it shall die. It must be asked how he said it, etc., as far as for from hence he was speaking.

VERS. 12.-- I have taken the Levites. (ID., quaest. 6.) What is it that the Levites, etc., as far as nor are the descendants of the Levites or of their cattle reckoned for these.

(ORIG. hom. 3 in Num.) I have taken the Levites from the midst of the children of Israel for every firstborn, etc. The Levites are taken for the firstborn, etc., as far as so that it is more to be taken for a firstborn than to be born a firstborn.

LXX: From the midst of the children of Israel, etc. Levi is the third among the sons of Israel; from what midst therefore are the Levites taken? I find the Sunamite answering the king of Israel confidently: In the midst of my people I dwell 386C(IV Reg. IV). And John saying of Christ: There standeth one in the midst of you, whom you know not. [Vulg. medius autem vestrum stetit, "hath stood"; the gloss below turns upon the present tense] I think therefore that he who has never turned aside to the right hand or to the left can be said to stand in the midst, who did no sin, neither was guile found in his mouth (Isa. LIII). And because he always stands, he is said to stand in the midst. But he who is his imitator, like the Sunamite, is indeed not said to stand, because sometimes he leans either to the right or to the left, for no one is clean from filth, not even if his life were of one day (Job XV): yet he is said to dwell in the midst of the people. The Levites therefore are taken from the midst of the children of Israel. For the Levites are they who did not know their own right hand and left, but, following Moses, that is, the law of God, did not spare father or mother. And thou therefore, if, when temptation comes and 386Dthe wrath of sin, thou neither lean to the right hand nor to the left, nor transgress the law of God, but stand in the midst fixed and stable, nor bend thy knees to sin, nor follow the head of a beast, that is, the image of folly: thou shalt be taken from the midst of the children of Israel, and shalt be placed in the number of the firstborn.

He openeth. Not everyone who has opened the womb is worthy of the primacy, whence: The wicked are alienated from the womb, they have gone astray from the belly, they have spoken false things (Psal. LVII). For how could anyone go astray from the way of God as soon as he came forth from his mother's womb? Or how could a boy but lately brought forth speak false things? It is necessary therefore to seek such a belly and womb as this may fit: The wicked are alienated from the womb, etc. And that will be the 387Awomb which every firstborn opens who is sanctified to the Lord. The Lord opened the womb of Lia, which was closed, and she bore the patriarchs; and likewise Rachel's, that she too might bear, whose sight was keen and her aspect comely (Gen. XXIX, XXX). But in many places of Scripture thou wilt find wombs opened, of which, if thou consider each according to its place, thou wilt find how sinners go astray from the womb, and others opening the womb are sanctified in the order of the firstborn.

From which. (ORIG.) Because the firstborn of Israel were not sanctified before, etc., as far as and might place him in the order of the firstborn.

VERS. 15.-- Number the sons of Levi. (ID.) Let us review more attentively, etc., as far as and with perpetual watches they celebrate the divine ministries.

387BVERS. 17.-- Levi by their names. Levi, added or taken up. This is the order of the doctors, which, chosen out of the world by the grace of God for the divine service, is united to the companies of the angels. Whence: I have chosen you out of the world (Joan. XV). And elsewhere: But they are as the angels of God in heaven (Matth. XXII). [Vulg. erunt sicut angeli Dei in caelo, "shall be as the angels"]

Gerson and Caath and Merari. The three sons of Levi signify the preachers of the holy Trinity, notable in confession, wonderful in faith, hope and charity. Nor does the interpretation of their names in any way conflict with this exposition, but greatly agrees with it. For Gerson is interpreted a stranger, Caath patience, or grinding teeth. Merari bitter things, or bitterness; for the minister of God knows himself a stranger and a pilgrim in the world, and 387Cis patient. He grinds the word of God with the tooth of discretion, according to the capacities of his hearers; and showing the world bitter to himself, he calls others back from pleasures. These are numbered from one month old, because they ought to consecrate the beginning of life to God, lest, promoted after many crimes, they cause the name of God to be blasphemed, whence: A bishop must be blameless, etc. (I Tim. III.)

VERS. 23.-- These shall pitch behind the tabernacle. A fitting order of the sons of Levi is shown, namely where those who are first in order should keep the east side, Moses and Aaron with their sons having the custody of the sanctuary in the midst of the children of Israel. Let the Caathites, from whom Moses and Aaron sprang, hold the south side, under the care of Eleazar the priest, who is the elder among the sons of Aaron. After these the Gersonites to the western 387Dside, under the care of Eliasaph. Last the Merarites to the northern side, under the guardianship of Ithamar the younger son of Aaron. But in these it is morally expressed that the ministers of the church ought diligently to keep their own order, and not to seek greater things than their proper place demands.

VERS. 24.-- On the west, under the prince Eliasaph the son of Lael. Where the day is shut up, the stars set. In these it is signified that the ministers of the tabernacle ought to flee concupiscence, to mortify carnal desires by God's help, to await the last day and the end of labour, so that, dead to the world and living to God, they may be able to say that: But God forbid that I should glory, save in the cross of our Lord Jesus Christ (Gal. VI).

388AVERS. 29.-- To the south. This signifies that, after the light of knowledge has been received, the preacher ought with discretion to bestow the word of patience and of consolation upon his neighbours. Under Eleazar, that is, by the help of God, according to that: Unless the Lord build the house, they have laboured in vain that build it.

VERS. 35.-- On the side. The last, the Merarites under Ithamar (who is interpreted bitter, or where the palm is), obtained by lot the north side, where an icy dread endures. In which the ministers of the Church are warned not to grow torpid through security; because although they have already entered the Church by the door of faith, have received the light of knowledge, have mortified the desires of the flesh, yet they have not yet come to perfect sweetness, that is, to eternal blessedness. Something bitter remains; 388Bone must contend with him who directs his throne to the north, whose hardness it is impossible to soften; whence: His heart shall be hardened as a stone, etc. (Isa. XIV; Job XXI). But when he shall be overcome, the palm being obtained, one must exult and rejoice.

VERS. 38.-- To the east side. The east side signifies the rising of faith, where Moses, who is interpreted taken up out of the water, and Aaron, a mountain of strength, encamp with their sons. For they who preside in the Church, to whom the keys of the heavenly kingdom have been committed (Matth. VI), ought to know the order of entrance, and to preach it to others. Because everyone must enter into the light of faith through the gate of confession to the water of baptism, that, taken up out of the water, he may be a son of adoption; and, begetting by the strength of constancy sons of good 388Cwork, may diligently guard the entrance of faith by which he entered the Church, lest, idle and useless, he be cast away.

VERS. 39.-- Twenty-two thousand. Fitly are the Levites entitled by the number of twenty-two. For by this number the Hebrew letters are comprehended, and, according to some, the books of the Old Testament. God's ministers are warned by this prescription to live and to teach according to the law of God, and in no way to go beyond it.

(ORIG., hom. 4 in Num.) Many are the differences of numbers, etc. as far as the mystery also of human generation is expounded.

VERS. 47.-- Five sicles. Mystically, whoever wishes to consecrate to God the beginning of his conversation and of his will ought diligently to guard the five senses of the body, 388Dlest through wantonness he slip away into error or unclean desires. But according to the sicle of the sanctuary, which is 20 oboli, that is, according to the doctrine of the decalogue, which consists in the two precepts of charity, let him refer all his works to the house of the high priest and of his sons.

CAPUT IV.

VERS. 2.-- Tolle summam. (ORIG.) Cum in tres ordines dividantur filii Levi, etc., usque ad et purioribus sensibus et liberis a saecularibus indicamus.

(ID.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad ut faciat opera in tabernaculo testimonii.

VERS. 3.-- In taber. (ID.) Tabernaculum testimonii, etc., usque ad non ab Israelitis sed a Levitis. 389AInvolventque eo arcam. Ne forte aliqui videant nudam et moriantur. Exterminatur enim qui ineffabilia sacramenta contingit, nisi meritis et scientia in sacerdotii gradum promotus fuerit.

(ORIG., hom. 4 in Num.) Septem quaedam species numerantur, etc., usque ad alii autem haec operta videant, imo velata in humeris portent.

VERS. 6.-- Et operient. Filii Aaron sacerdotes, quibus omnia nuda et aperta videre concessum est.

VERS. 7.-- Panes semper. (AUG., quaest. 7 in Num.) Et panes qui semper super eam erunt. Non utique iidem semper, sed similes eorum, quia illi auferebantur, et recentes quotidie ponebantur, dum tamen sine panibus mensa non relinqueretur. Ideo dixit, qui semper super eam erunt, id est, quia semper 389Bpanes, non quia illi semper.

VERS. 11.-- Nec non et altare. Alia littera: et super altare aureum adoperient vestimentum hyacinthinum: et adoperient illud operimento pelliceo ianthino. Posset ista locutio videri, quam velut absurdam et non integram Latini interpretes transferre voluerunt, id est super altare aureum adoperient vestimentum hyacinthinum, tanquam debuerit dici, et altare aureum adoperient vestimento hyacinthino: non adoperient vestimentum hyacinthinum. Hoc videtur significare quod ipsum vestimentum hyacinthinum aliunde adoperiretur. Sed mihi videtur non tam genus locutionis esse, quam subobscurus sensus; hoc enim intelligi potest: super altare aureum adoperient vestimentum hyacinthinum, ut ipsum 389Cvestimentum hyacinthinum alia re operiendum praeceperit, quod vestimentum jam super altare esset, ac si breviter utrumque complecteretur, ut vestimento hyacinthino altare operiendum, et vestimentum hyacinthinum alio tegmine operiendum. Subjunxit denique unde operiri vellet vestimentum hyacinthinum, cum adjecit: et adoperient illud operimento pelliceo ianthino.

VERS. 15.-- Tunc intrabunt filii Caath. (ORIG., hom. 4.) Qui licet non sint sacerdotes, etc., usque ad cujus haereditatem praestabit nobis Dominus noster Jesus Christus.

VERS. 18.-- Nolite perdere. (ORIG.) Nolite exterminare plebem Caath, etc. Intellige prius collocationem tabernaculi, etc., usque ad humeri autem 389Dopera significant.

VERS. 20.-- Alii nulla, etc. (ORIG.) In Ecclesiasticis quoque observationibus, etc., usque ad et in opus proferri ratio poposcerit.

VERS. 23.-- A triginta. Tricenarius, qui denario ter multiplicato componitur, integritatem fidei et perfectionem operis significat, sine quibus nemo accedere debet ad divina ministeria. Joseph tricenarius Aegypti suscepit gubernacula (Gen. XI), et David regnum, quod septuagenarius, id est, perpetua quiete dignus complevit (III Reg. II). Et Ezechiel prophetiae dona promeruit (Ezech. I). Et quia per fidem adversa debent longanimiter ferri, et praemia sublimiter sperari arcae vel templi altitudo, et tabernaculi 390Alongitudo triginta cubitis mensurantur.

Quinquaginta, etc. (ORIG.) In hoc quoque libro de hostium spoliis, etc., usque ad et tradere filiis Caath portanda humeris, et manibus vehenda.

Omnes qui ingrediuntur, etc. (ORIG., hom. 5 in Num.) Dicitur de Levitis, etc., usque ad qui omni ex parte multiplicatis et purificatis sensibus invenitur perfectus.

VERS. 35.-- Ad annum quinquagesimum. (ORIG.) Quinquagesimus autem annus remissionem et indulgentiam significat, etc., usque ad si addatur unius decadae consummatio, fiunt quingenti.

CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 2.-- Take the sum. (ORIG.) Since the sons of Levi are divided into three orders, etc., as far as and we declare them by purer senses, and by such as are free from worldly things.

(ID.) Another reading, etc., as far as that he may do the works in the tabernacle of the testimony.

VERS. 3.-- In the tabernacle. (ID.) The tabernacle of the testimony, etc., as far as not by the Israelites but by the Levites. 389AAnd they shall wrap the ark therein. Lest perchance some see it naked and die. For he is cut off who touches the ineffable sacraments, unless he has been promoted to the degree of the priesthood by merits and by knowledge.

(ORIG., hom. 4 in Num.) Certain seven kinds are numbered, etc., as far as but let others see these things covered, nay rather carry them veiled upon their shoulders.

VERS. 6.-- And they shall cover. The sons of Aaron, the priests, to whom it is granted to see all things naked and open.

VERS. 7.-- The loaves always. (AUG., quaest. 7 in Num.) And the loaves which shall be always upon it. Not indeed the same ones always, but ones like them, because those were taken away, and fresh ones were set out daily, yet so that the table was not left without loaves. Therefore he said, which shall be always upon it, that is, because there are always 389Bloaves, not because those same ones are always there.

VERS. 11.-- And likewise the altar. Another reading: and over the golden altar they shall spread a violet cloth: and they shall cover it with a covering of violet skins. This expression, which the Latin translators chose to render as though absurd and not entire, that is, over the golden altar they shall spread a violet cloth, might seem as though it ought to have been said, and they shall cover the golden altar with a violet cloth: not, they shall cover the violet cloth. This seems to signify that the violet cloth itself was covered from elsewhere. But to me it seems to be not so much a manner of speech as a somewhat obscure sense; for this can be understood: over the golden altar they shall spread a violet cloth, so that he commanded the violet cloth itself 389Cto be covered with another thing, which cloth was already upon the altar, as if he briefly comprehended both, that the altar was to be covered with a violet cloth, and the violet cloth to be covered with another covering. He subjoined at last from what he would have the violet cloth covered, when he added: and they shall cover it with a covering of violet skins.

VERS. 15.-- Then shall the sons of Caath enter. (ORIG., hom. 4.) Who, although they are not priests, etc., as far as whose inheritance our Lord Jesus Christ will bestow upon us.

VERS. 18.-- Destroy not. (ORIG.) Do not cut off the people of Caath, etc. Understand first the placing of the tabernacle, etc., as far as but the shoulders 389Dsignify works.

VERS. 20.-- Others with no curiosity, etc. (ORIG.) In ecclesiastical observances also, etc., as far as and reason shall have demanded that it be brought forth into work.

VERS. 23.-- From thirty. The number of thirty, which is composed of ten multiplied thrice, signifies the integrity of faith and the perfection of work, without which no one ought to approach the divine ministries. Joseph at thirty took up the government of Egypt (Gen. XI), and David the kingdom, which at seventy, that is, being worthy of perpetual rest, he completed (III Reg. II). And Ezechiel earned the gifts of prophecy (Ezech. I). And because through faith adversities ought to be borne with long-suffering, and rewards hoped for on high, the height of the ark or of the temple, and the 390Alength of the tabernacle, are measured by thirty cubits.

Fifty, etc. (ORIG.) In this book also, concerning the spoils of the enemy, etc., as far as and to deliver to the sons of Caath the things to be carried upon the shoulders and borne in the hands.

All that go in, etc. (ORIG., hom. 5 in Num.) It is said of the Levites, etc., as far as who, his senses being on every side multiplied and purified, is found perfect.

VERS. 35.-- Unto the fiftieth year. (ORIG.) But the fiftieth year signifies remission and indulgence, etc., as far as if the completion of one decade be added, they make five hundred.

CAPUT V.

VERS. 2.-- Omnem leprosum. Leprosi sunt haeretici variis errorum maculis polluti, non habentes 390Buniformem colorem doctrinae vel fidei: nec abscondunt imperitiam, sed ostendunt. Nulla vero falsa doctrina est quae non aliqua vera aliquando admisceat. Vera ergo falsis permista in aliqua disputatione vel narratione cujuslibet, tanquam in unius corporis colore apparentia, significant lepram, tanquam veris falsisque colorum locis, humana corpora variantem.

VERS. 6.-- Vir sive mulier. (RAB. in Num.) Hoc capitulo admonemur, ut si quid per negligentiam delinquimus, per confessionem et poenitentiam diluamus et damnum restituamus. Si autem proximo damnum intulimus, coram illo poeniteamus, et damnum restituamus. Si vero non superest, confitendo, poenitendo et eleemosynas largiendo, Domino satisfaciamus.

390C(AUG., etc., quaest. 9 in Num.) Alia littera usque ad quae per delictum ablata est.

VERS. 7.-- Reddent ipsum. (AUG., quaest. 10 in Num.) Quaeritur quomodo in Exodo legitur, etc., usque ad quo furem comprehensum vel convictum plecti oporteret.

VERS. 8.-- Excepto ariete. Arietis oblatio Christi passionem significat, in qua maxime confidimus, quia sine eo nihil possumus. Ipse est sacerdos qui nos Patri per oblationem carnis suae reconciliat (Job XV): ideo illi munera nostra offerimus, quo intercedente veniam mereamur.

VERS. 9. Ad sacerdotem. Christum scilicet, cui dicitur: Tu es sacerdos in aeternum secundum ordinem 390DMelchisedech (Psal. CIX). Ejus scilicet judicio cuncta reservans, ne forte judicet rem incertam, unde: Nolite ante tempus judicare quoadusque veniat Dominus, etc.

(RAB. in Num.) Farina hordeacea scientiam veteris legis significat, etc., usque ad damnanda aut liberanda reservatur.

VERS. 15.-- Non fundet. Non decet oleo adulationis vel indiscretae misericordiae, nec thure superfluae laudis vel inordinatae orationis, impoenitentes liniri, sed zelo justitiae secundum praeceptum Domini vitium instanter et ordinate persequi.

VERS. 17.-- Aquam sanctam. (ID.) Huic aquae miscet sacerdos pauxillum terrae de pavimento tabernaculi, 391Acum Christi decreto, commemoratio patriarcharum, prophetarum et apostolorum intromissa reperitur.

VERS. 23.-- Scribetque sacerdos in libello, etc. Quia scilicet poena peccatorum in Scriptura sancta explanatur. Quicunque autem eam pie et fideliter hauserit, supplicia peccatorum, quae in ea narrantur, pertimescens et abstinens a peccatis, fiet ei aqua amarissima in salutem. Qui autem contempserit, inflato ventre, computrescet femur ejus, et erit in maledictionem et in exemplum, quia justo Dei judicio punietur, et erunt ei in opprobrium desideria sua et actus.

VERS. 24.-- Dabit ei bibere, ut manifestetur peccatum illius, vel hic ad correctionem, vel in futuro 391Bad damnationem. Nihil enim occultum, quod non reveletur (Matt. X). Hinc Dominus ait: Sermo quem locutus sum vobis, ille vos judicabit in novissimo die (Joan XII).

CHAPTER V.

VERS. 2.-- Every leper. The lepers are the heretics, defiled with the various stains of errors, not having 390Ba uniform colour of doctrine or of faith: nor do they hide their ignorance, but display it. But there is no false doctrine which does not sometimes mingle in some true things. True things therefore mingled with false in the disputation or narration of any man, appearing as it were in the colour of one body, signify leprosy, which varies human bodies as it were by true and false places of colour.

VERS. 6.-- A man or a woman. (RAB. in Num.) By this chapter we are admonished that, if we offend in anything through negligence, we should wash it away by confession and by penance, and restore the loss. But if we have brought a loss upon our neighbour, let us repent before him, and restore the loss. But if he does not survive, let us satisfy the Lord by confessing, by repenting, and by bestowing alms.

390C(AUG., etc., quaest. 9 in Num.) Another reading as far as which was taken away by the offence.

VERS. 7.-- They shall restore it. (AUG., quaest. 10 in Num.) It is asked how it is read in Exodus, etc., as far as with which a thief apprehended or convicted ought to be punished.

VERS. 8.-- Except the ram. The oblation of the ram signifies the passion of Christ, in which we chiefly trust, because without him we can do nothing. He himself is the priest who reconciles us to the Father through the oblation of his own flesh (Job XV): therefore we offer him our gifts, by whose intercession we may obtain pardon.

VERS. 9. To the priest. Namely Christ, to whom it is said: Thou art a priest for ever according to the order 390Dof Melchisedech (Psal. CIX). Reserving all things, namely, to his judgment, lest perchance he judge an uncertain matter, whence: Judge not before the time, until the Lord come, etc.

(RAB. in Num.) Barley meal signifies the knowledge of the old law, etc., as far as she is reserved either to be condemned or to be set free.

VERS. 15.-- He shall not pour. It is not fitting that the impenitent be anointed with the oil of flattery or of indiscreet mercy, nor with the incense of superfluous praise or of disordered prayer, but that vice be pursued instantly and orderly with the zeal of justice according to the precept of the Lord.

VERS. 17.-- Holy water. (ID.) To this water the priest mixes a little earth from the pavement of the tabernacle, 391Awhen, together with the decree of Christ, the commemoration of the patriarchs, of the prophets and of the apostles is found brought in.

VERS. 23.-- And the priest shall write in a little book, etc. Because, namely, the punishment of sins is set forth in Holy Scripture. But whoever shall drink it piously and faithfully, dreading the punishments of sinners which are narrated in it and abstaining from sins, to him the most bitter water shall become unto salvation. But he who shall despise it, his belly swelling, his thigh shall rot, and he shall be for a curse and for an example, because he shall be punished by the just judgment of God, and his own desires and deeds shall be to him for a reproach.

VERS. 24.-- He shall give her to drink, that her sin may be made manifest, either here unto correction, or in the future 391Bunto damnation. For nothing is hidden which shall not be revealed (Matt. X). Hence the Lord says: The word which I have spoken to you, that shall judge you in the last day (Joan XII). [Vulg. sermo quem locutus sum, ille judicabit eum in novissimo die, "shall judge him"]

CAPUT VI.

VERS. 3.-- A vino et omni quod inebriare potest, (RAB. in Num.) In duodecima mansione, etc., usque ad et corruptione veteris nequitiae delectantur.

VERS. 5.-- Novacula non transibit. (ID.) Id est, nec dolum, nec malitiam in mente retinebit, ne polluatur, scilicet, caesaries capitis, id est, cogitatio mentis. Sed innocentiam teneat usque ad finem vitae. Aliter enim non erit votum acceptabile. Qui autem perseveraverit, salvus erit (Matth. X).

391CVERS. 6.-- Super mortuum non ingredietur. (ID.) Id est, mortuum in peccatis non imitabitur, nec iniquitatis ejus vitam ingredietur, ne perdat laborem suum. Non enim qui inchoat, sed qui consummat, coronabitur (II Tim. II).

VERS. 7.-- Nec super patris (ID.) Id est, nullus carnalis eum retrahat affectus, omnis persona, omnis dignitas, omnis consanguinitas Christi servitio postponenda est.

Consecratio Dei. (ID.) Id est, Spiritus sancti gratia in corde ejus diffusa est, unde: Nolite contristare Spiritum sanctum, in quo signati estis omnes (Ephes. IV).

VERS. 8.-- Omnes dies separationis. Id est, usque in finem perseveret sine contaminatione peccati atque delicti.

391DVERS. 9.-- Sin autem mortuus fuerit. (ID.) Id est, si mortali peccato pollutum imitari voluerit, et usque ad consensum descenderit, sciat necesse est ejus continentiam esse pollutam, et caput radendum, id est, mentem purgandam a prava cogitatione et noxia delectatione.

Radet illico. (ID.) Tempore scilicet compunctionis, quo cognoscit se deliquisse, statim se emendare studeat et semper similem ruinam timeat.

VERS. 10.-- Duos turtures. (ID.) Turtures castitatem, pulli columbarum simplicitatem significant. Qui ergo vult ad resurrectionem bonorum pervenire, offerat vero sacerdoti, id est, Christo, castitatem mentis et corporis, et simplicitatem in fide catholica, 392Aper quam in Ecclesiam intrat, ut per tanti pontificis opitulationem expietur.

VERS. 11.-- Et deprecabitur. (ID.) Unde: Advocatus noster apud Patrem (Joan. I.) Vel quia faciet nos deprecari secundum illud: Spiritus postulat pro nobis gemitibus inenarrabilibus (Rom. VIII).

VERS. 12.-- Ut dies priores. Nihil de pristina conversatione praesumat, sed in sola Dei gratia confidat, cujus dono percipit veniam et requiem sempiternam.

Polluta est sanctificatio, unde: Cum conversus fuerit justus a justitia sua et fecerit iniquitatem, omnes justitiae ejus oblivioni tradentur (Ezech. XVIII).

Ista est lex consecrationis. Postquam legislator Nazaraeum instruxit quomodo mundetur, si ejus consecratio super mortuo polluatur, addit quomodo 392Bpost completum votum se praesentet Domino, cum oblatione gratias redditurus, mystice innuens continentis vitam cum oblatione operis et munditia cordis in fine bono Domino placituram.

VERS. 14.-- Agnum anniculum. Hunc offert, qui totam spem suam in ejus immolatione ponit, de quo dicitur: Ecce agnus Dei, ecce qui tollit peccata mundi (Joan. I.) Hunc in holocaustum offert, cum pro ejus amore carnes mortificando totam vitam suam ardore charitatis incensam Domino consecrat.

(AUG., quaest. 12 in Num.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad et hoc sacrificium salutaris, quod prius dixerat, in salutare.

(RAB.) Agnum offert in holocaustum, qui Deo exhibet simplicitatem cordis, cum devotione charitatis; 392Covem, quae innocentiae exemplum ostendit; arietem vero pacificam hostiam, cum se in via Dei bene regit, et carnem rebellem animae imperio subjicit.

Et ovem. Ovem offert pro peccato, qui Christum innocentem pro peccato humani generis fideliter credit oblatum.

Et arietem. Hoc fit cum ipsum esse confidit, qui per dispensationem carnis coelestia et terrestria pacificavit, evacuans omnem principatum et potestatem; quem Deus exaltavit, et donavit illi nomen quod est super omne nomen, etc.

VERS. 15.-- Et lagana: veterem legem a Spiritu sancto dictatam et temperatam ad utilitatem humanam.

Absque fermento: quia spiritualis gratia in lege et in Evangelio recte intelligentibus patet.

392DVERS. 16.-- Quae offeret. Meritum et laborem, cujusque meditationem, et observationem legis in conspectu Dei remunerando praesentans, scilicet, ad ingressum vitae coelestis, peracto boni operis certamine, perducit.

VERS. 17.-- Arietem vero: dignum scilicet ostendens mercede quietis perpetuae, tanquam principem sui vel aliorum; et ducem optimum, qui se bene rexit, et duxit in semita mandatorum Dei, secundum doctrinam Scripturarum et gratiam spiritualium charismatum.

(GREG., lib. II, Moral., cap. 39.) Quid est quod Nazaraei capillos nutriunt? etc., usque ad devotionis perfectione consumat.

393AVERS. 19.-- Et armum coctum arietis, tortamque. Fortitudinem scilicet operis, coctam et probatam igne tribulationis, facit summus sacerdos sanctum suum offerre Deo Patri.

Et laganum, scientiam scilicet legis, dono Spiritus sancti juvans in opere consummare.

VERS. 20.-- Elevabit: bona scilicet, quae continens operatur, Patri praesentabit, et perpetua mercede faciet remunerari.

Sacerdotis, quia ad ejus gloriam pertinet quidquid Nazaraeus bene voluit et opere consummavit: Ipse enim est qui operatur in nobis et velle et perficere (Philip. II).

Post haec, id est, post rectam intentionem, discretam promissionem, operis perfectionem, et mercedis 393Bretributionem.

Bibere: plene et libere haurire spirituale donum, unde: Torrente voluptatis tuae potabis eos (Psal. XXXV).

VERS. 22.-- Locutusque est. Moses legem, Aaron sacerdotium, et filii ejus ordinem Ecclesiae significant. Docet ergo Dominus per legem sacerdotum ordinem, quomodo populum praedicatione aedificent, frequenti oratione et benedictione confirment.

(RAB., ubi supra. ) Moses multiplicem habet significationem, etc., usque ad dum tabernaculum complet, erigit et ungit.

VERS. 24.-- Benedicat. (ID.) Notandum quia in hac benedictione nomen Domini tertio inducitur, ut sancta Trinitas intelligatur a quo et per quem et in quo omnia bona sunt petenda et impetranda, quia 393Cex ipso et per ipsum et in ipso sunt omnia.

Et custodiat. (ID.) Benedictionem quam percepisti, sua conservet gratia: Nisi Dominus custodierit civitatem, frustra vigilat qui custodit eam (Psal. CXXVI).

VERS. 25.-- Ostendat. Ostensio vultus Dei propitiatio est, et verae salutis ostensio; unde: Ostende faciem tuam, et salvi erimus (Psal. LXXVII).

VERS. 26.-- Convertat, etc. Conversio vultus significat misericordiam, aversio iram; unde: Respice in me et miserere mei (Psal. CXVIII). Et alibi: Quare avertis faciem tuam, et oblivisceris inopiae nostrae? (Psal. XLIII.) Rogandum est ergo ut cujus auxilium avertimus a nobis peccando, per pietatem suam vultum suum super nos illuminare dignetur, ut nos ab 393Dinimicis eruens, tribuat perpetuam pacem.

VERS. 27.-- Invocabunt. (ID.) Sacerdotum est nomen Domini invocare: Domini autem, quod petitur, perficere. Nemo ergo sibi tribuat quod sola Trinitas cui vult praestat.

CHAPTER VI.

VERS. 3.-- From wine and from everything that can intoxicate, (RAB. in Num.) At the twelfth station, etc., as far as and they take delight in the corruption of the old wickedness.

VERS. 5.-- The razor shall not pass. (ID.) That is, he shall keep neither guile nor malice in his mind, lest, namely, the hair of the head, that is, the thought of the mind, be defiled. But let him hold innocence unto the end of life. For otherwise the vow will not be acceptable. But he that shall have persevered shall be saved (Matth. X) [Vulg. qui autem perseveraverit usque in finem, hic salvus erit, "he that shall persevere unto the end, he shall be saved"].

391CVERS. 6.-- He shall not go in over the dead. (ID.) That is, he shall not imitate one dead in sins, nor shall he enter upon the life of his iniquity, lest he lose his labour. For not he that beginneth, but he that finisheth, shall be crowned (II Tim. II) [Vulg. at 2 Tim 2:5 Nam et qui certat in agone, non coronatur nisi legitime certaverit, "he that striveth is not crowned unless he strive lawfully"].

VERS. 7.-- Nor over his father (ID.) That is, let no carnal affection draw him back, every person, every dignity, every kinship is to be set after the service of Christ.
