The consecration of God. (ID.) That is, the grace of the Holy Spirit has been poured out in his heart, whence: Grieve not the Holy Spirit, in whom you are all sealed (Ephes. IV) [Vulg. Spiritum Sanctum Dei … in quo signati estis in diem redemptionis, "the Holy Spirit of God … wherein you are sealed unto the day of redemption"].

VERS. 8.-- All the days of separation. That is, let him persevere unto the end without the contamination of sin and of offence.

391DVERS. 9.-- But if one shall have died. (ID.) That is, if he shall have wished to imitate one polluted by mortal sin, and shall have gone down as far as consent, let him know that of necessity his continence has been polluted, and his head must be shaved, that is, his mind must be purged of depraved thought and hurtful delight.

He shall shave forthwith. (ID.) At the time, namely, of compunction, when he knows that he has offended, let him study to amend himself at once, and let him always fear a like ruin.

VERS. 10.-- Two turtledoves. (ID.) Turtledoves signify chastity, young pigeons simplicity. He therefore who wishes to come to the resurrection of the good, let him offer to the true priest, that is, to Christ, chastity of mind and of body, and simplicity in the catholic faith, 392Athrough which he enters into the Church, that by the help of so great a pontiff he may be expiated.

VERS. 11.-- And he shall pray. (ID.) Whence: Our advocate with the Father (Joan. I.) Or because he will make us pray, according to that: The Spirit asketh for us with unspeakable groanings (Rom. VIII).

VERS. 12.-- That the former days. Let him presume nothing of his former manner of life, but let him trust in the grace of God alone, by whose gift he receives pardon and everlasting rest.

The sanctification is defiled, whence: When the just man shall have turned away from his justice and shall have done iniquity, all his justices shall be delivered to oblivion (Ezech. XVIII) [Vulg. Si autem averterit se justus a justitia sua … Omnes justitiae ejus quas fecerat, non recordabuntur, "all his justices which he had done shall not be remembered"].

This is the law of consecration. After the lawgiver has instructed the Nazarite how he is to be cleansed, if his consecration be defiled over a dead body, he adds how, 392Bthe vow being completed, he is to present himself to the Lord, about to render thanks with an oblation, intimating mystically that the life of the continent man, with the oblation of work and cleanness of heart, will please the Lord in a good end.

VERS. 14.-- A lamb of a year old. He offers this who places all his hope in his immolation, of whom it is said: Behold the Lamb of God, behold him who taketh away the sins of the world (Joan. I.) [Vulg. peccatum mundi, "the sin of the world"] He offers him in holocaust when, for love of him, mortifying the flesh, he consecrates to the Lord his whole life kindled with the ardour of charity.

(AUG., quaest. 12 in Num.) Another reading, etc., as far as and this sacrifice of the peace offering, which he had said before, into a peace offering.

(RAB.) He offers a lamb in holocaust who shows forth to God simplicity of heart with the devotion of charity; 392Ca sheep, which displays an example of innocence; but a ram as a peace offering, when he rules himself well in the way of God, and subjects the rebellious flesh to the command of the soul.

And a sheep. He offers a sheep for sin who faithfully believes that Christ the innocent was offered for the sin of the human race.

And a ram. This is done when he trusts that he himself is the one who through the dispensation of the flesh pacified things heavenly and earthly, emptying out every principality and power; whom God exalted, and gave him the name which is above every name, etc.

VERS. 15.-- And wafers: the old law, dictated and tempered by the Holy Spirit for human profit.

Without leaven: because spiritual grace lies open, in the law and in the Gospel, to those who understand rightly.

392DVERS. 16.-- Which he shall offer. Presenting in the sight of God, for reward, the merit and the labour, the meditation of each man, and the observance of the law, he brings him, namely, to the entrance of the heavenly life, the contest of good work being accomplished.

VERS. 17.-- But the ram: showing him worthy, namely, of the reward of perpetual quiet, as a prince of himself or of others; and a most excellent leader, who has ruled himself well and led himself in the path of the commandments of God, according to the doctrine of the Scriptures and the grace of spiritual charisms.

(GREG., lib. II, Moral., cap. 39.) What is it that the Nazarites nourish their hair? etc., as far as he may consume it in the perfection of devotion.

393AVERS. 19.-- And the boiled shoulder of the ram, and a cake. The strength, namely, of work, boiled and proved by the fire of tribulation, the high priest causes his holy one to offer to God the Father.

And a wafer, namely, the knowledge of the law, helping by the gift of the Holy Spirit to complete it in work.

VERS. 20.-- He shall lift up: he will present to the Father the good things, namely, which the continent man works, and will cause them to be repaid with perpetual reward.

Of the priest, because whatever the Nazarite has well willed and has completed in work pertains to his glory: For it is he that worketh in us both to will and to accomplish (Philip. II) [Vulg. Deus est enim, qui operatur in vobis, "God … who worketh in you"].

After these things, that is, after right intention, discreet promise, perfection of work, and 393Bthe repayment of reward.

To drink: to draw fully and freely the spiritual gift, whence: Thou shalt make them drink of the torrent of thy pleasure (Psal. XXXV).

VERS. 22.-- And he spoke. Moses signifies the law, Aaron the priesthood, and his sons the order of the Church. The Lord therefore teaches through the law the order of the priests, how they may build up the people by preaching, and confirm them by frequent prayer and blessing.

(RAB., ubi supra. ) Moses has a manifold signification, etc., as far as while he completes, raises and anoints the tabernacle.

VERS. 24.-- May he bless. (ID.) It is to be noted that in this blessing the name of the Lord is brought in three times, that the holy Trinity may be understood, from whom and through whom and in whom all good things are to be sought and obtained, because 393Cof him and through him and in him are all things.

And may he keep. (ID.) May he preserve by his grace the blessing which thou hast received: Unless the Lord keep the city, he watcheth in vain that keepeth it (Psal. CXXVI).

VERS. 25.-- May he show. The showing of the face of God is propitiation, and the showing of true salvation; whence: Show thy face, and we shall be saved (Psal. LXXVII).

VERS. 26.-- May he turn, etc. The turning of the face signifies mercy, the turning away, wrath; whence: Look upon me and have mercy on me (Psal. CXVIII). And elsewhere: Why turnest thou away thy face, and forgettest our want? (Psal. XLIII.) We must pray therefore that he whose help we turn away from us by sinning may through his own goodness vouchsafe to make his face shine upon us, so that, rescuing us from 393Dour enemies, he may grant perpetual peace.

VERS. 27.-- They shall invoke. (ID.) It belongs to the priests to invoke the name of the Lord: but to the Lord, to accomplish what is asked. Let no one therefore ascribe to himself what the Trinity alone bestows on whom it wills.

CAPUT VII.

VERS. 1.-- Factum est autem, etc., tabernaculum. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Ecclesiam vel perfectos, in quibus aromata virtutum et orationum adolentur, vel humanitatem Christi: in qua hostiam nostrae redemptionis Patri exhibuit. Ipse enim sacerdos, ipse hostia, et altare; quia in potestate mortem tenens propria voluntate suscepit. Unde: Nemo tollit 394Aanimam meam a me, sed ego pono eam, et iterum sumo eam (Joan. X).

VERS. 2.-- Principes Israel et capita, etc. (RAB. in Num.) Patriarchae, prophetae et apostoli, etc., usque ad fiet illis a Patre meo.

VERS. 3.-- Sex plaustra. (RAB.) Plaustra quae filii Israel Levitis ad subvectionem tabernaculi obtulerunt, temporalia solatia significant, quae devoti populi praedicatoribus compensant, ut sine cura et impedimento divino vacent obsequio. Ut et qui Evangelium annuntiant, de Evangelio vivant: Si enim seminant spiritualia, non est magnum si metunt temporalia (I Cor. IX).

Plaustra recte fide et misericordiae affectu ornata ut Deo sint accepta, duodecim sunt boves: qui enim 394Bin obsequio sanctorum laborant sub jugo apostolicae doctrinae omnia honeste et ordinate faciunt. Vel boves, doctores accipimus, qui in agro Domini digne operantur. De quibus dicitur: Non alligabis os bovis triturantis (Deut. XXV; I Tim. V). Qui enim apostolicam normam vivendo et praedicando conservant dupli honore honorandi sunt.

Unum bovem, etc. (ID.) Quia unitas fidei, etc., usque ad tabernaculum Dei cum debito obsequio properare debere.

VERS. 9.-- Filiis aut. Cat. (RAB.) Superius scriptum est, etc., usque ad nec ipsi qui habere aliquid exercitii et eruditionis videntur.

VERS. 10.-- Obtulerunt duces in dedicationem altaris die qua. Unusquisque pro posse suo, fidem, 394Cdoctrinam, et operationem bonam ad profectum et honorem Ecclesiae debet offerre.

Unctum est, oblationem suam ante altare. Tempore incarnationis, quando post resurrectionem et ascensionem susceperunt discipuli Spiritus sancti effusionem (Act. II).

VERS. 11.-- Duces per singulos dies offerant munera. Praedicatores recti itineris duces, qui licet eamdem fidei doctrinam, et boni operis eadem exempla offerant, pro diversitate tamen loci et temporis oblationes distribuunt.

VERS. 13.-- Acetabulum argenteum. Haec habent os angustum, et angustiam litterae legis, et obscuritatem prophetarum significant.

Varia vasa ad offerenda libamina facta, variae sunt 394Ddistinctiones verbi pro varia capacitate auditorum. Aliter enim sapientes, aliter insipientes; aliter divites, aliter pauperes; aliter sani, aliter infirmi docendi sunt; aliter rudis populus Judaeorum sub umbra legis, aliter Christianus in veritate Evangelii legis mysteria explanata conspiciens, in virum perfectum nutriendus est.

VERS. 19.-- Phialam argenteam habens septuaginta siclos, etc. Spiritualis scientia in lege et in Evangelio est, quae spirituali gratia ministrante ad salutem hominum scripta et prolata est, hinc rite sacrificium fit, quia nihil tam acceptum Deo, quam meditatio legis cum exsecutione operis.

(RAB., in Num., tom. II.) Phiala prius de vitro 395Afacta, etc., usque ad in Novo Testamento manifestius datus est.

VERS. 20.-- Mortariolum. (RAB.) Cor electorum divina sapientia plenum, continens aromata virtutum, unde dicitur: Quia multitudinis credentium erat cor unum et anima una (Act. IV).

Ex decem siclis, etc. (ID.) Quia cum impletione decem mandatorum odorem doctrinae et orationis emittunt, ut denarium aeternae vitae accipere mereantur.

VERS. 21.-- Bovem de armento, etc. (ID.) Haec diversas personas, etc., usque ad nostram vitam semper in meliora reformare certaremus.

Bovem offert, qui utilem in Ecclesia Dei proximis laborem impendit; arietem, qui se et alios in via 395Bjustitiae dirigit; agnum anniculum, qui simplex est et innocens. Haec omnia in holocaustum pro solius Dei amore et desiderio aeternae vitae. Hircum pro peccato offert, qui pro peccatis suis poenitere et orare non desistit. In sacrificium pacificorum offert duos boves, qui cum bonis operibus implet duo praecepta charitatis, Dei scilicet dilectionem, custodiendo mandata ejus; et proximi consulendo necessitatibus, in quibus tota lex pendet et prophetae. Offert autem arietes quinque hircos quinque, agnos anniculos quinque, quia quinque sensus corporis in via veritatis fortiter ducit, et a peccatis compescit, et simplicitatis exemplar proponit.

VERS. 85.-- Duo millia, etc. (RAB.) In vasis argenteis, etc., usque ad illuc non pervenitur.

395CVERS. 86.-- Mortariola aurea duodecim. (ID.) Quorum singula denos siclos habent, et simul omnia auri centum viginti siclos, corda electorum significant scientiam legis in omnibus servare secundum apostolicam doctrinam. Duodecies enim deni centum viginti faciunt, quo numero primitiva Ecclesia in Jerusalem Spiritum paracletum accipit (Act. II). Unde subditur:

Pondere, etc. (ID.) Sanctuarium enim Ecclesia est, cujus regulam fidei et intelligentiae sequentes, omnia quae in lege et prophetis scripta sunt nobis salubria esse credimus. Quidquid autem extra Ecclesiae unitatem sentitur et intelligitur aut servatur, animae damnationem parit.

VERS. 87.-- Boves, etc. (ID.) Boves, arietes et 395Dagni duodenario numero comprehensi, illos significant, qui vel in officio doctoris, vel in principatu alicujus honoris, vel simpliciter viventes, omnia secundum doctrinam apostolicam et propheticam agere student.

Hirci, etc. (ID.) Hi sunt qui poenitentiam student secundum regulam apostolicae fidei perficere.

VERS. 88.-- Boves, etc. (ID.) In his spiritualis scientia cum bonorum operum perfectione significatur, quia viginti quatuor horis cursus diei peragitur: lumen scientiae et fidei omni Christiano necessarium esse significant.

Sexaginta. Sexagenarius, id est, sexties denarius perfectionem mandatorum monstrat. Necesse est 396Aenim ut praelati, subditi, et poenitentes omnia secundum Ecclesiae doctrinam faciant, quatenus oblatio eorum ritum et ordinem teneat pacificorum, ut Dei gratiam mereantur, non offensam.

VERS. 89.-- Cumque, etc. (GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 21, tom. II.) Quid est quod Moyses, etc., usque ad et quomodo benigne descendit ad infima, valenter recurrit ad summa.

De propitiatorio. Quia per gratiam divinae propitiationis post culpam praevaricationis aperuit nobis Deus viam veritatis.

Inter duos cherubin. Quia per angelicam visionem, non in sua substantia apparuit Deus Moysi; unde: Lex ordinata per angelos in manu mediatoris (Gal. III). Vel quia per utrumque Testamentum consona 396Bvoce ad fidem veritatis nos erudit; vel, quia per Filium, qui in medio duorum Testamentorum in carne apparuit, locutus est Pater humano generi.

CHAPTER VII.

VERS. 1.-- It came to pass moreover, etc., the tabernacle. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) The Church, or the perfect, in whom the spices of the virtues and of prayers are burned, or the humanity of Christ: in which he presented to the Father the victim of our redemption. For he himself is priest, he himself victim and altar; because, holding death in his power, he took it upon himself by his own will. Whence: No man taketh 394Amy life from me, but I lay it down, and I take it up again (Joan. X) [Vulg. Nemo tollit eam a me: sed ego pono eam a meipso, et potestatem habeo ponendi eam, et potestatem habeo iterum sumendi eam, "No man taketh it from me, but I lay it down of myself"].

VERS. 2.-- The princes of Israel and the heads, etc. (RAB. in Num.) The patriarchs, the prophets and the apostles, etc., as far as it shall be done for them by my Father.

VERS. 3.-- Six wagons. (RAB.) The wagons which the children of Israel offered to the Levites for the carriage of the tabernacle signify temporal comforts, which devout peoples repay to their preachers, so that without care and hindrance they may have leisure for the divine service. And so that they also who announce the Gospel may live by the Gospel: For if they sow spiritual things, it is no great matter if they reap temporal things (I Cor. IX) [Vulg. Si nos vobis spiritualia seminavimus, magnum est si nos carnalia vestra metamus?, "If we have sown unto you spiritual things, is it a great matter if we reap your carnal things?"].

The wagons rightly adorned with faith and with the affection of mercy, that they may be acceptable to God, are twelve oxen: for they who 394Blabour in the service of the saints under the yoke of apostolic doctrine do all things honestly and in order. Or by oxen we understand teachers, who work worthily in the field of the Lord. Of whom it is said: Thou shalt not muzzle the mouth of the ox that treadeth out the corn (Deut. XXV; I Tim. V). For they who by living and by preaching keep the apostolic rule are to be honoured with double honour.

One ox, etc. (ID.) Because the unity of faith, etc., as far as that they ought to hasten to the tabernacle of God with due obedience.

VERS. 9.-- But to the sons of Caath. (RAB.) It is written above, etc., as far as nor do they themselves who seem to have something of exercise and of learning.

VERS. 10.-- The princes offered for the dedication of the altar on the day whereon. Each one according to his power ought to offer faith, 394Cdoctrine, and good work to the profit and honour of the Church.

It was anointed, his oblation before the altar. At the time of the incarnation, when after the resurrection and the ascension the disciples received the outpouring of the Holy Spirit (Act. II).

VERS. 11.-- Let the princes offer gifts on each several day. The preachers are the leaders of the right way, who, although they offer the same doctrine of faith and the same examples of good work, yet distribute their oblations according to the diversity of place and of time.

VERS. 13.-- A silver dish. These have a narrow mouth, and signify the narrowness of the letter of the law and the obscurity of the prophets.

The various vessels made for offering libations are the various 394Ddistinctions of the word according to the various capacity of the hearers. For the wise are to be taught in one way, the unwise in another; the rich in one way, the poor in another; the whole in one way, the sick in another; in one way the rude people of the Jews under the shadow of the law, in another the Christian, beholding in the truth of the Gospel the mysteries of the law laid open, who is to be nourished unto a perfect man.

VERS. 19.-- A silver bowl having seventy sicles, etc. Spiritual knowledge is in the law and in the Gospel, which by the ministry of spiritual grace was written and set forth for the salvation of men, hence the sacrifice is duly made, because nothing is so acceptable to God as the meditation of the law with the execution of the work.

(RAB., in Num., tom. II.) The bowl, first made of glass, 395Aetc., as far as in the New Testament it was more manifestly given.

VERS. 20.-- A little mortar. (RAB.) The heart of the elect, full of divine wisdom, containing the spices of the virtues, whence it is said: Because the multitude of believers had one heart and one soul (Act. IV).

Of ten sicles, etc. (ID.) Because with the fulfilling of the ten commandments they send forth the odour of doctrine and of prayer, that they may deserve to receive the penny of eternal life.

VERS. 21.-- An ox of the herd, etc. (ID.) These, various persons, etc., as far as we might strive always to reform our life into better things.

He offers an ox who spends useful labour upon his neighbours in the Church of God; a ram, who directs himself and others in the way 395Bof justice; a lamb of a year old, who is simple and innocent. All these in holocaust, for the love of God alone and the desire of eternal life. He offers a he-goat for sin who does not cease to repent and to pray for his sins. In the sacrifice of peace offerings he offers two oxen, who with good works fulfils the two precepts of charity, namely, the love of God, by keeping his commandments; and of his neighbour, by taking thought for his necessities, on which the whole law hangs, and the prophets. And he offers five rams, five he-goats, five lambs of a year old, because he strongly leads the five senses of the body in the way of truth, and restrains them from sins, and sets forth a pattern of simplicity.

VERS. 85.-- Two thousand, etc. (RAB.) In the silver vessels, etc., as far as thither one does not come.

395CVERS. 86.-- Twelve golden mortars. (ID.) Of which each severally has ten sicles, and all together a hundred and twenty sicles of gold, signify that the hearts of the elect keep the knowledge of the law in all things according to the apostolic doctrine. For twelve times ten make a hundred and twenty, at which number the primitive Church in Jerusalem received the Paraclete Spirit (Act. II). Whence it is added:

In weight, etc. (ID.) For the sanctuary is the Church, whose rule of faith and of understanding we follow, believing that all things which are written in the law and the prophets are wholesome for us. But whatever is held and understood or observed outside the unity of the Church begets the damnation of the soul.

VERS. 87.-- Oxen, etc. (ID.) Oxen, rams and 395Dlambs, comprehended in the number of twelve, signify those who, whether in the office of teacher, or in the headship of some honour, or living simply, strive to do all things according to the apostolic and prophetic doctrine.

He-goats, etc. (ID.) These are they who strive to accomplish penance according to the rule of the apostolic faith.

VERS. 88.-- Oxen, etc. (ID.) In these is signified spiritual knowledge with the perfection of good works, because the course of the day is accomplished in twenty-four hours: they signify that the light of knowledge and of faith is necessary to every Christian.

Sixty. Sixty, that is, six times ten, shows the perfection of the commandments. For it is necessary 396Athat prelates, subjects, and penitents do all things according to the doctrine of the Church, so that their oblation may hold the rite and order of peace offerings, that they may deserve the grace of God, and not his offence.

VERS. 89.-- And when, etc. (GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 21, tom. II.) What is it that Moses, etc., as far as and how kindly he descends to the lowest, and mightily runs back to the highest.

From the propitiatory. Because through the grace of the divine propitiation God opened to us, after the fault of transgression, the way of truth.

Between the two cherubim. Because through an angelic vision, not in his own substance, God appeared to Moses; whence: The law ordained by angels in the hand of a mediator (Gal. III). Or because through both Testaments with a harmonious 396Bvoice he instructs us unto the faith of the truth; or because through the Son, who appeared in the flesh in the midst of the two Testaments, the Father spoke to the human race.

CAPUT VIII

VERS. 2.-- Septem lucernas. Septem dona Spiritus sancti, quae in Christo semper manserunt, et in membris pro voluntate ejus distributa sunt.

(BEDA, in Num.) Mensa et candelabrum, etc., usque ad prohibens ei testimonium.

VERS. 3.-- Super candelabrum. Christum, scilicet, primogenitum de radice Jesse, super quem requievit spiritus sapientiae et intellectus, spiritus consilii et fortitudinis (Isa. XI).

VERS. 4.-- Ex auro. Quia Christus specialiter 396Cimmunis a peccato, et operibus justitiae praeclarus, et membra ejus innocentiam et justitiam, quantum valent imitantur, in futuro perficientur.

Ductili. Quia Christus ex conceptione et nativitate perfectus Deus et homo exstitit, passionum dolores pertulit (II Tim. III), et sic ad resurrectionis gloriam pervenit, et omnes qui pie volunt vivere in ipso quasi metallum feriendo dilatatum per passionis contumelias ad immortalitatis gratiam perveniunt.

Stipes. Christus scilicet, de quo procedunt calami, quia ab ipso accipiunt justi quidquid habent boni; unde: Sine me nihil potestis facere (Joan. XV).

VERS. 6.-- Tolle Levitas de me. Mystice. Ordinem ecclesiasticum instruit, quomodo rite provehatur ad 396Ddignitatem sacerdotii.

VERS. 7.-- Pilos carnis suae. (GREG., lib. V Moral., cap. 23.) Pili carnis sunt quaelibet superflua humanae carnis, etc., usque ad cum speculationis alta penetramus.

Laverint. Mundaverint, scilicet opera, ut in conspectu Dei sit eorum conversatio munda, et coram hominibus irreprehensibilis.

VERS. 8.-- Bovem. Mediatorem Dei et hominum: cujus immolatione peccata nostra deleta sunt. In utroque bove incarnatio ejus significatur. Una est enim passio Christi, sed duo nobis impetravit, expiationem scilicet a peccatis et sanctificationem, ut nos offerret Deo Mortificatos, quidem carne, vivificatos 397Aautem spiritu. Unde Paulus: Sed abluti estis, sed sanctificati estis in nomine Domini Jesu (I Cor. VI). Possunt autem per hos duos boves, quorum unus pro peccato, alter offertur in holocaustum, duae actionum species exprimi, quarum una quae male gessimus, per poenitentiae lamentum abluimus; altera bonorum operum et virtutum fructibus gratiam Dei et praemium beatitudinis promeremur.

Libamentum. Sacramentum passionis, quod spiritali scientia intellectum atque tractatum, fidelibus saluberrimum et Deo gratissimum est. Si tamen talibus mysteriis percipiendis minister Domini intus forisque se mundum et dignum praebuerit.

VERS. 9.-- Et applicabis. Applicantur Levitae coram tabernaculo foederis, cum electi ad officium altaris 397Bsecundum apostolicam doctrinam perducuntur: qua in verum tabernaculum intratur (I Tim. III), si juxta doctrinam et exempla sanctorum bene incoeperint, et usque ad finem perseveraverint.

VERS. 10.-- Ponent filii. Quia assensum devotionis suae electioni debent praebere, et ad imperium eorum bonis operibus instare.

VERS. 11.-- A filiis Israel. (BED.) Sub multorum scilicet testimonio et cognitione facienda est electio, ut examinatio digna celebretur, ut nemo reprehendere possit. Debent enim testimonium habere bonum etiam ab his qui foris sunt.

VERS. 12.-- Levitae quoque, etc. Quia ministri Domini proprio labore initia bonorum operum Domino consecrare festinant, ut sive poenitentia si 397Cagenda, sive studia virtutum exercenda, efficaciter perficiant.

VERS. 14.-- Separabis. Non debet minister Domini contentus esse populari disciplina: moribus et doctrina separatus et spectabilis debet esse.

VERS. 17.-- Omne primogenitum in terra. (RAB.) Primogenita Aegyptiorum, etc., usque ad et divinis mysteriis verus Levita digne possit aptari.

VERS. 19.-- Ut serviant. Videant ministri Domini quam necessarium sit eis officii sui jura custodire; nec terrenis lucris vel voluptatibus inhiare, quibus proprium est tabernaculo deservire, et pro populo orare, ne sit in eo plaga disperdens: alioquin et se et populum in perditionem praecipitant, et dicitur eis: Munus non suscipiam de manu vestra. 397D(Malach. I).

VERS. 24.-- A viginti quinque. (ISID., quaest. in Num. tom. V.) A vigesimo et quinto anno mandantur Levitae, etc., usque ad in illis omnimodo exstinguitur, ne subsistant.

CHAPTER VIII

VERS. 2.-- Seven lamps. The seven gifts of the Holy Spirit, which always remained in Christ, and are distributed in the members according to his will.

(BEDA, in Num.) The table and the candlestick, etc., as far as forbidding him testimony.

VERS. 3.-- Upon the candlestick. Christ, namely, the firstborn from the root of Jesse, upon whom rested the spirit of wisdom and understanding, the spirit of counsel and of fortitude (Isa. XI) [Vulg. Et requiescet super eum spiritus Domini: spiritus sapientiae et intellectus, "And the spirit of the Lord shall rest upon him: the spirit of wisdom"].

VERS. 4.-- Of gold. Because Christ is specially 396Cfree from sin, and eminent in works of justice, and his members imitate his innocence and justice as far as they are able, and in the time to come shall be made perfect.

Beaten work. Because Christ from his conception and nativity stood forth perfect God and man, endured the pains of the passion (II Tim. III), and so came to the glory of the resurrection, and all who wish to live godly in him, as metal spread out by beating, come through the reproaches of the passion to the grace of immortality.

The shaft. Christ, namely, from whom the branches proceed, because from him the just receive whatever of good they have; whence: Without me you can do nothing (Joan. XV).

VERS. 6.-- Take the Levites from me. [Vulg. Tolle Levitas de medio filiorum Israel, "Take the Levites from the midst of the children of Israel"] Mystically. He instructs the ecclesiastical order, how one is duly advanced to 396Dthe dignity of the priesthood.

VERS. 7.-- The hairs of their flesh. (GREG., lib. V Moral., cap. 23.) The hairs of the flesh are whatever things are superfluous to human flesh, etc., as far as when we penetrate the heights of contemplation.

They shall wash. They shall cleanse, namely, their works, so that in the sight of God their conversation may be clean, and before men blameless.

VERS. 8.-- An ox. The mediator of God and of men, by whose immolation our sins have been blotted out. In both oxen his incarnation is signified. For the passion of Christ is one, but it obtained two things for us: expiation, namely, from sins, and sanctification, that he might offer us to God put to death indeed in the flesh, but quickened 397Ain the spirit. [Vulg. mortificatus … vivificatus, of Christ himself, "being put to death indeed in the flesh, but enlivened in the spirit"] Whence Paul: But you are washed, but you are sanctified in the name of the Lord Jesus (I Cor. VI) [Vulg. adds a third member, sed justificati estis, "but you are justified", and reads Domini nostri Jesu Christi]. Now by these two oxen, of which one is offered for sin, the other in holocaust, two kinds of action may be expressed, by the one of which we wash away, by the lamentation of penance, the things we have done ill; by the other we earn the grace of God and the reward of blessedness by the fruits of good works and of virtues.

A libation. The sacrament of the passion, which, understood and handled with spiritual knowledge, is most wholesome to the faithful and most acceptable to God. If, however, for the receiving of such mysteries the minister of the Lord has shown himself clean and worthy within and without.

VERS. 9.-- And thou shalt bring. The Levites are brought before the tabernacle of the covenant when the elect are led to the office of the altar 397Baccording to apostolic doctrine: by which one enters into the true tabernacle (I Tim. III), if according to the doctrine and the examples of the saints they have begun well, and have persevered unto the end.

VERS. 10.-- The sons shall put. Because they ought to give the assent of their devotion to the election, and at their bidding to press on in good works.

VERS. 11.-- From the children of Israel. (BED.) The election is to be made, namely, under the testimony and knowledge of many, that a worthy examination may be held, so that no one may be able to reprove it. For they ought to have a good testimony even from those who are without.

VERS. 12.-- The Levites also, etc. Because the ministers of the Lord hasten to consecrate to the Lord by their own labour the beginnings of good works, so that whether penance is to be done, or the pursuits of the virtues to be exercised, 397Cthey may effectually accomplish them.

VERS. 14.-- Thou shalt separate. The minister of the Lord ought not to be content with the common discipline: he ought to be set apart and notable in manners and in doctrine.

VERS. 17.-- Every firstborn in the land. (RAB.) The firstborn of the Egyptians, etc., as far as and that a true Levite may worthily be fitted to the divine mysteries.

VERS. 19.-- That they may serve. Let the ministers of the Lord see how necessary it is for them to keep the rights of their office; and not to gape after earthly gains or pleasures, whose proper work it is to serve the tabernacle and to pray for the people, lest there be among them a destroying plague: otherwise they cast down both themselves and the people into perdition, and it is said to them: I will not receive a gift from your hand. 397D(Malach. I).

VERS. 24.-- From five and twenty. (ISID., quaest. in Num. tom. V.) From the twenty-fifth year the Levites are commanded, etc., as far as in them it is utterly extinguished, so that they do not subsist.

CAPUT IX.

VERS. 12.-- Faciant filii Israel phase in tempore suo, quartadecima die mensis hujus, etc. (RAB.-BED.) Cum in veteri Testamento, etc., usque ad Et: Ecce nova facio omnia (Apoc. XXI)

(BED.) Deinde tertiam septimanam, etc., usque ad extrema charismate spiritali per seipsum illustravit.

(ID.) Ipsa quoque lunae conversio, etc., usque ad 398Apraecepit eos dispensationis suae testes esse usque ad ultimum terrae (Act. I).

(ID.) Bene autem luna, etc., usque ad merito quintadecima paschali gaudio adaptatur.

(ID.) His quidem paschae vel phase a lege assumptis indiciis, haeredes Novi Testamenti etiam diem Dominicam annectimus, quia et conditione primitivae lucis excellit, et triumpho Dominicae resurrectionis.

(ID.) Septem quoque dies lunae, usque ad quasi: Quod uni dico, omnibus dico (Marc. XIII).

(ID.) In nomine quoque paschae, etc., usque ad per singulos gradus spiritalis transitus largitori illius vicem tendamus.

(ID.) Vel in crescente ad homines luna, etc., 398Busque ad convenienter intelligi.

VERS. 6.-- Ecce autem quidam immundi super anima hominis, etc. (AUG., quaest. 15 in Num.) Cumque paschae tempore quidam immundi essent super anima hominis, etc., usque ad aut forte si pascha non egissent, ad culpam non pertineret.

VERS. 8.-- State ut consulam. (GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 21, tom. II.) Moyses crebro de rebus dubiis ad tabernaculum recurrit relictis turbis, etc., usque ad dum in meditatione mentis a carnalibus sensibus abstrahuntur.

VERS. 10.-- Homo qui fuerit immundus super anima, etc. (ISID.) Praecipitur ut qui longius habitabant, vel immundi in anima fuerant, in secundo mense pascha faciant, quia Gentiles cum omnibus 398Cdaemonibus fornicati, immundi erant et a Deo remoti. Sed salubri confessione mundati ad Christi nativitatem quasi ad secundum mensem transire praecipiuntur.

(RAB.) Immundus est in anima, etc., usque ad ad vesperam mundi cum azymis et lactucis agrestibus agnum comedit.

VERS. 11.-- Azymis. (RAB.) Azyma comedit, qui recta opera sine corruptione vanae gloriae facit, et mandata misericordiae sine peccati admixtione.

Agrestibus. Quia cum corpus Christi accipimus pro peccatis compungi debemus, ut amaritudo poenitentiae abstergat amorem perversae vitae.

VERS. 12.-- Omnem ritum. Quidquid in lege historialiter praeceptum est, in celebratione paschae 398DEcclesia spiritualiter observat.

Observabunt. Quia ejus dicta sollicite sunt discutienda, ut in praesenti vita intelligendo et operando penetrentur.

VERS. 13.-- Exterminabitur. Eos qui sub lege erant, comminando instruit, ne tempus paschae observare negligant. Nos quoque admonet, ut spirituale pascha suo tempore faciamus, de quo Paulus ait: Ecce nunc tempus acceptabile, etc. (II Cor. VI). Quia quisquis redemptionem humani generis, quae veri agni immolatione facta est, fide digna non celebraverit, et vitam suam in meliora non converterit, et a vitiis ad virtutes non transierit tempore hujus mortalitatis: ad resurrectionis gloriam in fine 399Averis cultoribus Christi tribuendam non pertinebit, sed exterminabitur a consortio sanctorum.

VERS. 14.-- Peregrinus quoque et advena si fuerint apud vos, etc. (RAB.) Ostendit nullam distantiam esse personarum apud Deum. Quicunque enim ad fidem catholicam conversus, et Christi baptismate ablutus, Ecclesiae unitatem intraverit, ecclesiasticis poterit communicari mysteriis.

VERS. 15.-- Nubes. (ISID.) Christus est columna, qui rectus et firmus, qui fulciens infirmitatem nostram, per noctem lucens, per diem non lucens, ut qui non vident videant, et qui vident caeci fiant. Christi quoque sacramentum tanquam in die manifestum est, in carne, velut in nube: in judicio vero tanquam in terrore nocturno. Quia tunc erit tribulatio 399Bmagna tanquam ignis, et lucebit justis, et ardebit injustis (Exod. XII).

Mystice, incarnatio Christi, cujus clementia tabernaculum Ecclesiae protegit, bonitas statum disponit, prudentia in via hujus saeculi transitum dirigit.

(GREG., hom. 21 in Evang., tom. III.) In igne terror, etc., usque ad peccatorum suorum tenebras agnoscentibus igne sui amoris infulsit.

VERS. 23.-- Per verbum Domini figebant tentoria. Significatione nubis, quae erat signum divinae voluntatis, sicut vox loquentis.

CHAPTER IX.

VERS. 12.-- Let the children of Israel keep the phase in its own season, on the fourteenth day of this month, etc. (RAB.-BED.) Since in the Old Testament, etc., as far as And: Behold, I make all things new (Apoc. XXI)

(BED.) Then the third week, etc., as far as he enlightened the last by himself with spiritual grace.

(ID.) The very turning of the moon also, etc., as far as 398Ahe commanded them to be witnesses of his dispensation unto the uttermost part of the earth (Act. I).

(ID.) But well the moon, etc., as far as deservedly the fifteenth is fitted to the paschal joy.

(ID.) These signs of the pasch or phase being taken from the law, we the heirs of the New Testament add also the Lord's day, because it excels both in the condition of the first light and in the triumph of the Lord's resurrection.

(ID.) The seven days also of the moon, as far as as it were: What I say to one, I say to all (Marc. XIII) [Vulg. Quod autem vobis dico, omnibus dico, "what I say to you, I say to all"].

(ID.) In the name also of the pasch, etc., as far as through each several degree of the spiritual passage let us render our part to the giver of it.

(ID.) Or in the moon waxing toward men, etc., 398Bas far as to be fitly understood.

VERS. 6.-- But behold certain men unclean upon the soul of a man, etc. (AUG., quaest. 15 in Num.) And when at the time of the pasch certain men were unclean upon the soul of a man, etc., as far as or perhaps, if they had not kept the pasch, it would not have pertained to guilt.

VERS. 8.-- Stand, that I may consult. (GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 21, tom. II.) Moses, leaving the crowds, often runs back to the tabernacle in doubtful matters, etc., as far as while in the meditation of the mind they are drawn away from carnal senses.

VERS. 10.-- The man that shall be unclean upon a soul, etc. (ISID.) It is commanded that they who dwelt further off, or had been unclean in soul, should keep the pasch in the second month, because the Gentiles, having committed fornication with all 398Cthe demons, were unclean and removed from God. But, cleansed by wholesome confession, they are commanded to pass over to the nativity of Christ as to a second month.

(RAB.) He is unclean in soul, etc., as far as at the evening of the world he eats the lamb with unleavened bread and wild lettuce.

VERS. 11.-- With unleavened bread. (RAB.) He eats unleavened bread who does right works without the corruption of vain glory, and the commandments of mercy without the admixture of sin.

Wild. Because when we receive the body of Christ we ought to be pricked with compunction for our sins, so that the bitterness of penance may wipe away the love of a perverse life.

VERS. 12.-- Every rite. Whatever is commanded historically in the law, the Church observes spiritually in the celebration of the pasch 398D.

They shall observe. Because his sayings are to be carefully examined, so that in the present life they may be penetrated by understanding and by working.

VERS. 13.-- He shall be cut off. He instructs by threatening those who were under the law, lest they neglect to observe the time of the pasch. He admonishes us also that we keep our spiritual pasch in its own season, of which Paul says: Behold, now is the acceptable time, etc. (II Cor. VI). Because whosoever shall not have celebrated with worthy faith the redemption of the human race, which was made by the immolation of the true Lamb, and shall not have turned his life to better things, and shall not have passed over from vices to virtues in the time of this mortality: he shall not attain to the glory of the resurrection to be bestowed at the end 399Aupon the true worshippers of Christ, but shall be cut off from the fellowship of the saints.

VERS. 14.-- The stranger also and the sojourner, if they shall be with you, etc. (RAB.) He shows that there is no distinction of persons with God. For whosoever, converted to the catholic faith and washed with the baptism of Christ, shall have entered into the unity of the Church, will be able to be made partaker of the ecclesiastical mysteries.

VERS. 15.-- A cloud. (ISID.) Christ is the pillar, who is upright and firm, who, upholding our infirmity, shining by night, not shining by day, that they who see not may see, and they who see may become blind. The sacrament of Christ also is manifest as in the day, in the flesh, as it were in a cloud: but in the judgment as in the terror of the night. Because then there shall be great tribulation 399Bas fire, and it shall give light to the just, and shall burn the unjust (Exod. XII).

Mystically, the incarnation of Christ, whose clemency protects the tabernacle of the Church, whose goodness orders its state, whose prudence directs its passage in the way of this world.

(GREG., hom. 21 in Evang., tom. III.) In fire, terror, etc., as far as he shone with the fire of his love upon those who acknowledge the darkness of their sins.

VERS. 23.-- At the word of the Lord they pitched their tents. By the signification of the cloud, which was the sign of the divine will, as the voice of one speaking.

CAPUT X.

399CVERS. 2.-- Fac tibi duas tubas argenteas, etc. (ISID., in Num.) Per duas tubas exercitus ducitur, etc., usque ad auditorum mentes ad tentationum certamina ardentius excitantur.

VERS. 4.-- Si semel clangueris. (RAB., in Num.) Semel tubis clangere, etc., usque ad et id ipsum dicant omnes in eodem sensu et in eadem scientia.

VERS. 5.-- Primi. Quia scilicet lux fidei primum infulsit. Ordinatis ergo Evangelii praedicatoribus, primum eis dicitur: In viam gentium ne abieritis, sed ite ad oves quae perierunt domus Israel (Matth. X): quibus ipse Christus scilicet praesentialiter Evangelium praedicavit. Post resurrectionem vero discipulis apparens ait: Euntes docete omnes gentes, etc. (Matth. XVIII). Tribus ergo Juda cum caeteris quae 399Dsecum ad orientem habitabant, primum castra movebat: deinde caeterae secundum ordinem suum. Quia primum in Judaea oportuit primitivam Ecclesiam construi, post de cunctis orbis nationibus gentes ad fidem convocari.

VERS. 7.-- Quando autem congregandus. (RAB.) In convocatione populi, etc., usque ad nihil enim quaerimus, nisi ut pateat quod clausum est.

VERS. 8.-- Filii autem. Praedicatores, scilicet verbum Dei praedicant, et viam justitiae auditoribus demonstrant, ut in semita mandatorum Dei boni operis gressum ponant, et iter impiorum fugiant.

Clangent tubis. Subditos, scilicet ad poenitentiam et ad compunctionem lacrymarum excitando, et 400Acorda auditorum ad Dei misericordiam implorandam provocando.

VERS. 10.-- Si quando habebitis epulum. (RAB.) Nota quia in adversis, etc., usque ad et alibi: Praedica verbum, insta opportune importune, etc. (I Tim. IV).

VERS. 11.-- Anno secundo. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam, tom. I.) Duodecima mansione venerunt filii Israel in desertum Sinai, etc., usque ad intus autem plena sunt ossibus mortuorum, et omni spurcitia.

(GREG., Pastoralis parte III, admon. 18.) Plerumque suadere elatis utilia melius possemus, etc., usque ad inde se verbis exnortantis inclinaret.

VERS. 23.-- Arcaque foederis. (RAB.) Arca foederis 400BDomini, etc., usque ad callem veritatis et justitiae sequentibus demonstrat.

VERS. 34.-- Nubes quoque. Protectio divina, quae Spiritus sancti gratia electos ab initio mundi defendit, et ad futuram quietem custodit.

VERS. 35.-- Cumque elevaretur. Exemplo Mosis rectores plebis provocantur, ut semper solliciti commissum sibi gregem in principio operis, et in consummatione, Domino commendent, ut proprias oves protegat et inimicos depellat.

CHAPTER X.

399CVERS. 2.-- Make thee two trumpets of silver, etc. (ISID., in Num.) By the two trumpets the army is led, etc., as far as the minds of the hearers are more ardently stirred up to the contests of temptations.

VERS. 4.-- If thou shalt sound but once. (RAB., in Num.) To sound the trumpets once, etc., as far as and that all should say the same thing in the same sense and in the same knowledge.

VERS. 5.-- The first. Because, namely, the light of faith first shone forth. When therefore the preachers of the Gospel had been ordained, it was first said to them: Go ye not into the way of the Gentiles, but go to the sheep that are lost of the house of Israel (Matth. X) [Vulg. In viam gentium ne abieritis, et in civitates Samaritanorum ne intraveritis: sed potius ite ad oves quae perierunt domus Israel, "and into the cities of the Samaritans enter ye not: but go rather to the sheep"]: to whom Christ himself, present in person, preached the Gospel. But after the resurrection, appearing to the disciples, he said: Going, teach ye all nations, etc. (Matth. XVIII). The tribe of Juda therefore, with the others which 399Ddwelt with it toward the east, first moved the camp: then the rest according to their own order. Because it behoved that the primitive Church should first be built in Judaea, and afterwards that the Gentiles from all the nations of the world be called to the faith.

VERS. 7.-- But when they are to be gathered together. (RAB.) In the calling together of the people, etc., as far as for we seek nothing but that what is closed may lie open.

VERS. 8.-- And the sons. The preachers, namely, preach the word of God, and show the way of justice to their hearers, that they may set the step of good work in the path of the commandments of God, and flee the way of the wicked.

They shall sound the trumpets. Rousing their subjects, namely, to penance and to the compunction of tears, and 400Aprovoking the hearts of the hearers to implore the mercy of God.

VERS. 10.-- If at any time you shall have a banquet. (RAB.) Note that in adversities, etc., as far as and elsewhere: Preach the word, be instant in season, out of season, etc. (I Tim. IV).

VERS. 11.-- In the second year. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam, tom. I.) At the twelfth station the children of Israel came into the desert of Sinai, etc., as far as but within they are full of dead men's bones, and of all filthiness.

(GREG., Pastoralis parte III, admon. 18.) For the most part we might better persuade the elated of profitable things, etc., as far as he would thence be bent by the words of one exhorting him.

VERS. 23.-- And the ark of the covenant. (RAB.) The ark of the covenant 400Bof the Lord, etc., as far as it shows the path of truth and of justice to those who follow.

VERS. 34.-- The cloud also. The divine protection, which by the grace of the Holy Spirit has defended the elect from the beginning of the world, and keeps them for the quiet that is to come.

VERS. 35.-- And when it was lifted up. By the example of Moses the rulers of the people are stirred up, that, always solicitous, they may commend to the Lord the flock committed to them, at the beginning of the work and at its completion, so that he may protect his own sheep and drive off their enemies.

CAPUT XI.

VERS. 1.-- Interea ortum est murmur. (RAB., in Num., tom. II.) Murmurantem populum ignis Domini accensus devorat, etc., usque ad tam in anima 400Cquam in corpore uretur poena perpetua.

VERS. 5.-- Pepones porrique. (GREG., lib. XX Moral., cap. 19.) Carnalium mentes pro amore saeculi, etc., usque ad ubi carnaliter gemant.

VERS. 16.-- Congrega mihi septuaginta. (GREG., lib. XXIX Moral., cap. 13.) Sicut plerumque juvenes dicuntur, etc., usque ad patet quia senectus mentis, non corporis, eligatur.

VERS. 17.-- Ut descendam. (ISID.) In tredecima mansione, etc., usque ad quod quondam fuerat in Moyse et prophetis.

Et auferam de spiritu tuo, tradamque eis ut sustentent, etc. (AUG., quaest. 18 in Num.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad non ut ideo Moyses minus haberet.

400DVERS. 21.-- Sexcenta millia peditum hujus populi sunt. (AUG., quaest. 19.) Quaeritur utrum hoc Moses diffidendo dixerit an quaerendo? etc., usque ad quando sententia Dei secuta non est quae vindicaret, sed potius quae doceret.

VERS. 24.-- Venit igitur Moyses, et narravit populo verba Domini, congregans. (ORIG., Hom. 6 in Num.) Alia littera: Exiit, inquit, Moyses, etc., usque ad et similia, etc.

VERS. 25.-- Auferens de spiritu. (ID.) Non intelligendum est, etc., usque ad et origo ipsius nihil damni ex derivatione accepit.

Requievisset. (ID.) Et requievit super eos spiritus, et prophetaverunt omnes, etc. Non legitur requievisse 401Aspiritus, etc., usque ad laudem vitae eorum et virtutes exposuit.

(ORIG.) Et requievit in eis spiritus, et prophetaverunt, etc. Continuo operatur spiritus, etc., usque ad neque decet Spiritus sancti adesse praesentiam.

VERS. 26.-- Remanserant autem in castris. Non negligentia praecepit Domini, sed devotione humilitatis indignos se judicantes perceptione tanti muneris: ideo judex cordium Deus dignos eos judicavit, et ostendit.

VERS. 29.-- Quid, inquit, aemularis. (GREG., lib. XXIII Moral., cap. 24.) Pia mens pastorum, etc., usque ad qui bonum quod habuit non invidit.

Coturnicum multitudinem, qui parum, etc. (RAB. in Num. ) Carnalis populus Judaeorum, etc., usque ad 401BNostra autem conversatio in coelis est (II Cor. X).

VERS. 34.-- Sepulcra concupiscentiae. (ISID., in Num., tom. V.) Tertia decima mansio est in sepulcris concupiscentiae, etc., usque ad intus plena sunt ossibus mortuorum et omni spurcitia.

Venerunt, etc. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam.) Quarta decima mansio, etc., usque ad qui ait per prophetam: Nunquid qui cadit non adjiciat ut resurgat? (Jer. VIII.)

(ISID.) Quarta decima mansio, etc., usque ad ad veniam reduxerit eam.

CHAPTER XI.

VERS. 1.-- In the meantime there arose a murmuring. (RAB., in Num., tom. II.) The kindled fire of the Lord devours the murmuring people, etc., as far as he shall be burned with perpetual punishment as much in soul 400Cas in body.

VERS. 5.-- The melons and the leeks. (GREG., lib. XX Moral., cap. 19.) The minds of carnal men, for the love of the world, etc., as far as where they may groan carnally.

VERS. 16.-- Gather unto me seventy. (GREG., lib. XXIX Moral., cap. 13.) As for the most part young men are called, etc., as far as it is plain that the old age of the mind, not of the body, is to be chosen.

VERS. 17.-- That I may come down. (ISID.) At the thirteenth station, etc., as far as what once had been in Moses and in the prophets.

And I will take of thy spirit, and will deliver to them, that they may sustain, etc. (AUG., quaest. 18 in Num.) Another reading, etc., as far as not that Moses should therefore have the less.

400DVERS. 21.-- There are six hundred thousand footmen of this people. (AUG., quaest. 19.) It is asked: did Moses say this out of distrust or out of inquiry? etc., as far as when the sentence of God which followed was not one to punish, but rather one to teach.

VERS. 24.-- Moses therefore came, and told the people the words of the Lord, gathering them. (ORIG., Hom. 6 in Num.) Another reading: Moses went out, he says, etc., as far as and the like, etc.

VERS. 25.-- Taking away of the spirit. (ID.) It is not to be understood, etc., as far as and its origin received no loss from the derivation.

Had rested. (ID.) And the spirit rested upon them, and they all prophesied, etc. It is not read that the spirit rested, etc., as far as 401Ahe set forth the praise of their life and their virtues.

(ORIG.) And the spirit rested in them, and they prophesied, etc. The spirit works continually, etc., as far as nor is it fitting that the presence of the Holy Spirit be there.

VERS. 26.-- But there remained in the camp. Not by negligence did the Lord command, but by the devotion of humility, judging themselves unworthy of the receiving of so great a gift: therefore God, the judge of hearts, judged them worthy, and showed it.

VERS. 29.-- Why, saith he, art thou jealous. (GREG., lib. XXIII Moral., cap. 24.) The devout mind of pastors, etc., as far as who did not envy the good that he had.

A multitude of quails, who a little, etc. (RAB. in Num. ) The carnal people of the Jews, etc., as far as 401BBut our conversation is in heaven (II Cor. X).

VERS. 34.-- The graves of lust. (ISID., in Num., tom. V.) The thirteenth station is at the graves of lust, etc., as far as within they are full of dead men's bones and of all filthiness.

They came, etc. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam.) The fourteenth station, etc., as far as who says by the prophet: Shall he that falleth not add that he should rise again? (Jer. VIII.) [Vulg. Numquid qui cadit non resurget?, "Shall not he that falleth rise again?"]

(ISID.) The fourteenth station, etc., as far as he brought it back to pardon.

CAPUT XII.

VERS. 1.-- Locutaque est Maria, etc. (ORIG., hom. 7 in Num.) Maria, plebs Judaica; Moyses lex Domini 401Cest, etc., usque ad cum fiet unus grex et unus pastor (Joan. X).

(ID.) Quia detraxerunt Aaron et Maria Moysi, correpti sunt, etc., usque ad lepra enim consequitur detrectantes et cacologos.

(ID.) Omnis etiam qui scripta Moysi male intelligit, et spiritalem legem carnaliter suscipit, detrahit ei, quia de verbis spiritus carnaliter sentit.

Mystice, non solum Judaei haeretici, qui non recipiunt legem et prophetas, detrahunt Moysi. Qui impingunt etiam ei quod homicida fuit, quia Aegyptium interfecit, et alia multa etiam in alios prophetas blasphemo ore concinnant: ideo sunt in anima leprosi, et ejiciuntur ab ecclesia Dei. Vel, haeretici, qui detrahunt proximis.

401DAethiopissam. (AUG., quae est. 20.) De uxore Moysi etc., usque ad sicut solent locorum et gentium nomina plerumque vetustate mutari.

VERS. 6.-- Si quis. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Audite quantum beneficii conferunt obtrectantes, etc., usque ad vel ad ipsum Deus non loquitur in aenigmate, sed in specie.

VERS. 8.-- Dominum. (ID.) Alia littera: Et gloriam Domini videt. Vidit Moyses gloriam Domini, quando transformatus est Dominus in monte, et aderat ei Moyses cum Elia, et colloquebatur (Luc. IX). Ideo recte addit:

Quare igitur. (ID.) Quod ad eos dicitur, etc., usque ad sed pro magnitudine sensuum eum glorificemus.

402AVERS. 10.-- Nubes quoque recessit. (ID.) Et nubes recessit de tabernaculo, etc. Prius recessit nubes, etc., usque ad et appareat in nobis lepra peccati.

Cumque respexisset. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Alia littera: Et respexit Aaron ad Mariam, et ecce Maria erat leprosa. Vult in hoc ostendere, etc., usque ad ad perfectae nativitatis instituta revocat Apostolus: Filioli mei, quos iterum parturio, donec formetur Christus in vobis, etc. (Gal. IV.)

VERS.-- Clamavitque Moyses ad Dominum dicens: Deus, obsecro, sana eam. Cui respondit Dominus. (ID.) Alia littera: Et proclamavit Moyses ad Dominum dicens: Domine, precor te, sana eam, etc. Quem oportebat orare pro sanitate populi sui, nisi Moysen? Forte hoc erat quod cum Domino loquebatur in 402Bmonte transformato (Matth. XVII), petens ut cum plenitudo gentium introisset omnis Israel salvus fieret (Rom. XI).

VERS. 14.-- Spuisset in faciem illius, nonne debuerat saltem, etc. Signum repudii est in faciem conspui. Unde proximus qui repudiat conjugium, discalciatus in faciem conspuitur. Hinc Isaias: Omnes gentes sicut gutta situlae, et sicut sputum reputatae sunt (Isa. XL, secundum LXX). Ostenditur ergo quod etiam ille populus, sicut caeterae gentes, quae ut sputum reputantur, abjectus est. Si enim consideres priorem honorem, pontificalem ordinem, insignia sacerdotum, Levitica ministeria, majestatem templi, et eos cum coelestibus in terris habere consortia, qui fuit ille honor, quae gloria? (Rom. V.) Nunc autem 402Cmulto dedecore horrescunt sine templo, sine altari, sine sacrificio, sine propheta, sine sacerdotio, et sine coelesti visitatione. Unde patet quod pater conspuens perfudit vultus eorum ignominia. Septem ergo diebus separantur extra castra, id est per septimam hujus mundi; in septimana autem dierum totius creaturae visibilis videntur productae esse substantiae; tunc enim quae non erant, facta sunt. In septimanam totius mundi quaedam secreta et Deo soli cognita dispensatione quae tunc producta sunt, explicantur: in hac septimana (quia sequestrata est Maria) non moventur castra filiorum Israel, sed stant in uno conclusi, et nullus ex eis profectus est, donec mundaretur Maria a lepra sua.

Septem diebus: et populus non est motus de loco 402Dillo. (ORIG.) Maria quidem interveniente Aaron pontifice septimo die curatur. Nos autem obtrectantes usque ad finem septimanae mundi, id est usque ad resurrectionem permanebimus leprosi, nisi, dum poenitendi tempus est, corrigamur conversi ad Jesum et per poenitentiam supplicantes ei.

CHAPTER XII.

VERS. 1.-- And Mary spoke, etc. (ORIG., hom. 7 in Num.) Mary is the Jewish people; Moses is the law of the Lord 401C, etc., as far as when there shall be one flock and one shepherd (Joan. X).

(ID.) Because Aaron and Mary detracted from Moses, they were rebuked, etc., as far as for leprosy follows detractors and evil-speakers.

(ID.) Everyone also who ill understands the writings of Moses, and receives the spiritual law carnally, detracts from him, because he thinks carnally of the words of the spirit.

Mystically, not only Jews, heretics, who do not receive the law and the prophets, detract from Moses. They also cast it in his teeth that he was a homicide, because he killed the Egyptian, and with blasphemous mouth they frame many other things besides against other prophets: therefore they are leprous in soul, and are cast out from the Church of God. Or, heretics, who detract from their neighbours.

401DThe Ethiopian woman. (AUG., quae est. 20.) Of the wife of Moses, etc., as far as as the names of places and of nations are wont for the most part to be changed by age.

VERS. 6.-- If any. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Hear how much benefit detractors confer, etc., as far as or God speaks not to him in a riddle, but in open form.

VERS. 8.-- The Lord. (ID.) Another reading: And he seeth the glory of the Lord. Moses saw the glory of the Lord when the Lord was transfigured on the mountain, and Moses was present with him together with Elias, and was speaking with him (Luc. IX). Therefore he rightly adds:

Why therefore. (ID.) What is said to them, etc., as far as but let us glorify him for the greatness of the meanings.

402AVERS. 10.-- The cloud also departed. (ID.) And the cloud departed from the tabernacle, etc. First the cloud departed, etc., as far as and the leprosy of sin appear in us.

And when he had looked. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Another reading: And Aaron looked upon Mary, and behold Mary was leprous. He wishes in this to show, etc., as far as the Apostle calls them back to the ordinances of a perfect nativity: My little children, of whom I am in labour again, until Christ be formed in you, etc. (Gal. IV.)

VERS.-- And Moses cried to the Lord, saying: O God, I beseech thee, heal her. To whom the Lord answered. (ID.) Another reading: And Moses cried out to the Lord, saying: O Lord, I pray thee, heal her, etc. Whom did it behove to pray for the health of his people, if not Moses? Perhaps this was what he was speaking of with the Lord on 402Bthe mount when he was transfigured (Matth. XVII), asking that, when the fulness of the Gentiles had come in, all Israel might be saved (Rom. XI).

VERS. 14.-- Had spit in her face, ought she not at the least, etc. It is a sign of repudiation to be spit upon in the face. Whence the next of kin who repudiates a marriage is unshod and spit upon in the face. Hence Isaias: All nations are as a drop of a bucket, and are reputed as spittle (Isa. XL, secundum LXX). It is shown therefore that that people also, like the other nations which are reputed as spittle, has been cast off. For if thou shouldst consider their former honour, the pontifical order, the insignia of the priests, the Levitical ministries, the majesty of the temple, and that they had fellowship on earth with the heavenly ones, what was that honour, what the glory? (Rom. V.) But now 402Cthey shudder with much dishonour, without temple, without altar, without sacrifice, without prophet, without priesthood, and without heavenly visitation. Whence it is plain that the Father, spitting, has covered their faces with ignominy. For seven days therefore they are separated outside the camp, that is, through the seventh age of this world; and in the week of days the substances of the whole visible creation are seen to have been brought forth; for then the things which were not were made. Into the week of the whole world are unfolded certain secrets, known to God alone, which were then brought forth by dispensation: in this week (because Mary is set apart) the camp of the children of Israel is not moved, but they stand shut up in one place, and none of them set out, until Mary was cleansed of her leprosy.

For seven days: and the people was not moved out of that 402Dplace. (ORIG.) Mary indeed is cured on the seventh day, Aaron the pontiff interceding. But we detractors shall remain leprous until the end of the week of the world, that is, until the resurrection, unless, while there is time for repenting, we be corrected, turned to Jesus and beseeching him by penance.

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 4.-- Pharan. (RAB. in Num.) Notandum quod Pharan non est speciale nomen mansionis, etc., usque ad usque ad tricesimam secundam haec continentur.

Pharan interpretatur os visibile, in quo potest intelligi quod Verbum caro factum est; et invisibilis visibilis effectus. Et potest ex hoc significari, quod postquam finis et perfectio omnium, quae erga illum 403Apopulum gerenda erant, venit, tunc transiit, et venit ad eum quem Verbum carnem factum ante non credidit.

Mitte viros qui, etc. (RAB.) Duodecim exploratores mittuntur ad sanctam terram, etc., usque ad et cinis ejus piacularis aspersio est.

VERS. 18.-- Ascendite ad meridianam plagam. (AUG., quaest. 21.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad et ibi erat quidam depressior locus, de qua valle botrus ablatus est.

VERS. 24.-- Quem portaverunt, etc. (ISID. in Num., tom. V.) Botrum in ligno de terra promissionis duo advehunt, etc., usque ad quia nec Christus sine lege, nec lex sine Christo esse potest.

VERS. 24.-- De malis quoque granatis et de ficis, 403Betc. Quae legem significant, quia nec Christus sine lege, nec lex sine Christo.

VERS. 26.-- Exploratores terrae post quadraginta dies, etc. (ISID., ibid.) Exploratores qui ad terram uberem missi terruerunt populum, etc., usque ad et pepones vitiorum et libidinum corruptione marcentes.

VERS. 27.-- In desertum Pharan, quod est in Cades. (RAB.) Solitudo Pharan decem et octo mansiones continet, etc., usque ad et prohibentur transire Jordanem.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 4.-- Pharan. (RAB. in Num.) It is to be noted that Pharan is not the special name of a station, etc., as far as these things are contained up to the thirty-second.

Pharan is interpreted a visible mouth, in which may be understood that the Word was made flesh; and the invisible was made visible. And it can be signified from this, that after the end and the perfection of all the things which were to be done toward that 403Apeople had come, then he passed over, and came to him whom he had not before believed to be the Word made flesh.

Send men who, etc. (RAB.) Twelve spies are sent to the holy land, etc., as far as and its ashes are an expiatory sprinkling.

VERS. 18.-- Go up to the south side. (AUG., quaest. 21.) Another reading, etc., as far as and there was there a certain lower place, from which valley the cluster was taken.

VERS. 24.-- Which they carried, etc. (ISID. in Num., tom. V.) Two men carry the cluster on a staff from the land of promise, etc., as far as because neither can Christ be without the law, nor the law without Christ.

VERS. 24.-- Of the pomegranates also and of the figs, 403Betc. Which signify the law, because neither is Christ without the law, nor the law without Christ.

VERS. 26.-- The spies of the land after forty days, etc. (ISID., ibid.) The spies who, sent to a fertile land, terrified the people, etc., as far as and melons rotting with the corruption of vices and of lusts.

VERS. 27.-- Into the desert of Pharan, which is in Cades. (RAB.) The wilderness of Pharan contains eighteen stations, etc., as far as and they are forbidden to pass over the Jordan.

CAPUT XIV.

VERS. 7.-- Terra, etc. (ORIG., hom. 7 in Num.) Quae est terra ista, sancta quidem et bona, sed ab 403Cimpiis habitata? etc., usque ad nobis ingredientibus illi pellantur, et ascendentibus cadant.

VERS. 9.-- Neque timeatis populum terrae hujus, quia sicut panem ita possumus. (AUG., quaest. 23 in Num.) Caleb et Jesus Nave loquentes ad populum, etc., usque ad isti Dominum temporum creatorem, et ordinatorem, et omnium dispensatorem.

VERS. 12.-- Feriam igitur eos. (ORIG., hom. 8 in Num.) Comminatio haec non est iracunda, sed prophetica, etc., usque ad ideo pluribus exorat pro populo illo.

(ID.) Feriam igitur. Fit commmatio haec a Domino, etc., usque ad quod alium populum hoc abjecto resuscitet.

VERS. 31.-- Parvulos (ORIG., hom. 8.) Sed filii 403Dipsorum qui sunt hic mecum, quicunque ignorant bonum vel malum, etc. Patres nostri fuerunt populus ille prior, etc., usque ad si vermanseris in bonitate, etc.

VERS. 34.-- Annus pro die. (ID.) Quasi pro die per annum, etc., usque ad nisi cui Pater omne judicium tradidit (Joan. V).

(ID.) Neget quis fortasse bonitati Dei convenire, etc., usque ad sed ultra non sinit animam peccato vulnerari.

(ID.) Aliud adjiciam, etc., usque ad ut multo magis confidamus quod advocatus noster Jesus veniam nobis impetrabit a Patre.

VERS. 40.-- Parati sumus. (RAB., in Num. ) O mira 404Aprotervitas humanae mentis, etc., usque ad sic patriae coelestis gaudia quae nobis promissa sunt percipiemus.

CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 7.-- The land, etc. (ORIG., hom. 7 in Num.) What is this land, holy indeed and good, but inhabited by 403Cthe ungodly? etc., as far as that as we enter in they may be driven out, and as we go up they may fall.

VERS. 9.-- Neither fear ye the people of this land, because as bread so we are able. (AUG., quaest. 23 in Num.) Caleb and Josue the son of Nun, speaking to the people, etc., as far as these men the Lord, the creator of times, and the orderer, and the dispenser of all things.

VERS. 12.-- I will strike them therefore. (ORIG., hom. 8 in Num.) This threat is not one of anger, but prophetic, etc., as far as therefore he entreats with many words for that people.

(ID.) I will strike them therefore. This threat is made by the Lord, etc., as far as that he may raise up another people, this one being cast off.

VERS. 31.-- The little ones (ORIG., hom. 8.) But their sons 403Dwho are here with me, whosoever know not good or evil, etc. Our fathers were that former people, etc., as far as if thou shalt remain in goodness, etc.

VERS. 34.-- A year for a day. (ID.) As it were a year for a day, etc., as far as save him to whom the Father hath given all judgment (Joan. V).

(ID.) Someone will perhaps deny that it befits the goodness of God, etc., as far as but he does not suffer the soul to be wounded further by sin.

(ID.) I will add another thing, etc., as far as that we may much more trust that our advocate Jesus will obtain pardon for us from the Father.

VERS. 40.-- We are ready. (RAB., in Num. ) O wondrous 404Awantonness of the human mind, etc., as far as so we shall receive the joys of the heavenly country which have been promised to us.

CAPUT XV.

VERS. 4.-- Sacrificium, etc. Fidem incarnationis Christi, quae in lege plenissime commendatur, vel observantiam Decalogi.

(RAB., in Num. ) Lex praecipit in privatis publicisque sacrificiis, etc., usque ad quia sexta pars est congii.

(ID.) Jubetur ergo per singulas species animalium, etc., usque ad omnis stultitia et versutia Deo abominabilis est.

VERS. 6.-- Et arietes. (ID.) In oblatione arietis 404Bduae decimae similae offeruntur; olei et vini, tertia pars hin. Quia aries rectorum ordinem signat, bene cum duabus decimis offertur, cum quilibet Decalogum legis intellectu et operatione tenens, rectoris officio delegatur: olei vero atque vini tertia pars hin exhibetur, quando ab eo charitas et misericordia, et gratia spiritualis doctrinae circa subjectos impenditur.

VERS. 8.-- Quando vero de bobus feceris holocaustum, etc. (ID.) In bovis oblatione tres decimae similae et medietas hin in oleo vel vino, etc., usque ad in coelesti beatitudine per Dei gratiam in se perficiendum sperent.

Cum veneritis in terram, etc. (RAB.) Cum quilibet praesentem Ecclesiam per fidem intrat, etc., usque 404Cad a quo speratur perfectionis supplementum.

VERS. 22.-- Quod si per. (AUG., quaest. 24.) Quod praecipitur quomodo peccata expientur, etc., usque ad quae discernuntur a peccatis volentium.

VERS. 24.-- Vitulum offeret. (RAB.) Filii Israel vitulum de armento offerunt in holocaustum, etc., usque ad exauditus est pro sua reverentia.

VERS. 28.-- Sacerdos. Christus, qui per proprium sanguinem introivit semel in sancta, aeterna redemptione inventa (Hebr. V).

VERS. 30.-- Anima vero, etc. (AUG., quaest. 25 in Num.) Alia littera: Anima quaecunque peccaverit, etc., usque ad unde nunc longum est disputare.

(ISID., in Num., tom. V.) Calumniantur impii quod tam atrociter jussu Dei ab omni populo trucidatur, 404Dqui ligna collegit in sabbato, etc., usque ad quia a spiritualibus judicatur.

VERS. 38.-- Fimbrias. (ID., ibid.) Jubentur filii Israel in quatuor angulis, etc., usque ad omnia in gloriam Dei faciamus.

CHAPTER XV.

VERS. 4.-- A sacrifice, etc. The faith of the incarnation of Christ, which is most fully commended in the law, or the observance of the Decalogue.

(RAB., in Num. ) The law commands, in private and in public sacrifices, etc., as far as because it is the sixth part of a congius.

(ID.) Therefore it is commanded, for the several kinds of animals, etc., as far as all folly and craftiness is abominable to God.

VERS. 6.-- And rams. (ID.) In the oblation of a ram 404Btwo tenths of fine flour are offered; of oil and of wine, the third part of a hin. Because the ram signifies the order of rulers, it is fittingly offered with two tenths, since anyone who holds the Decalogue of the law by understanding and by working is appointed to the office of a ruler: but the third part of a hin of oil and of wine is presented, when by him charity and mercy, and the grace of spiritual teaching, is bestowed upon those set under him.

VERS. 8.-- But when thou shalt make a holocaust of oxen, etc. (ID.) In the oblation of an ox three tenths of fine flour and the half of a hin in oil or in wine, etc., as far as they may hope for it to be perfected in themselves in the heavenly beatitude through the grace of God.

When you shall come into the land, etc. (RAB.) When anyone enters the present Church by faith, etc., as far 404Cas from whom the supplement of perfection is hoped for.

VERS. 22.-- But if through ignorance. (AUG., quaest. 24.) That which is commanded, how sins are to be expiated, etc., as far as which are distinguished from the sins of them that sin willingly.

VERS. 24.-- He shall offer a calf. (RAB.) The children of Israel offer a calf of the herd for a holocaust, etc., as far as he was heard for his reverence.

VERS. 28.-- The priest. Christ, who by his own blood entered in once into the holies, having obtained eternal redemption (Hebr. V).

VERS. 30.-- But the soul, etc. (AUG., quaest. 25 in Num.) Another reading: Whatsoever soul shall have sinned, etc., as far as whereon it were now long to dispute.

(ISID., in Num., tom. V.) The ungodly cavil that he is so cruelly slain by all the people at the command of God, 404Dwho gathered sticks on the sabbath, etc., as far as because he is judged by them that are spiritual.

VERS. 38.-- Fringes. (ID., ibid.) The children of Israel are commanded, in the four corners, etc., as far as let us do all things unto the glory of God.

CAPUT XVI.

VERS. 12-- Vocaret Dathan? (AUG., quaest. 26 in Num.) Quid est quod Dathan et Abiron in seditione vocati a Moyse, etc., usque ad ut non dicerent non ascendemus, sed non ascendimus, quodam locutionis genere.

VERS. 26.-- Recedite a tabernaculis. (AUG., quaest. 27.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad justi fulgeant sicut sol in regno Patris sui (Matth. XIII).

405A(ISID.) in Num., tom. V.) Per Dathan, et Abiron, et Core, etc., usque ad in aeterno igne praeparata ultione peribunt.

Descenderuntque. (AUG., quaest. 29.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad tanquam carceribus inferi puniendos reservari.

VERS. 37-- Praecipe Eleazaro. (ID., quaest. 30.) Alia littera, etc., usque, ad quia per eos exemplum datum est caeteris, quid timerent.

Tollat thuribula. (ORIG., hom. 9 in Num.) Core figuram tenet eorum qui contra ecclesiasticam fidem et doctrinam veritatis insurgunt, etc., usque ad: sanctificata enim sunt et Domino oblata.

(ID.) Potest et alio modo intelligi, etc., usque ad non enim tam magnifice in eis virtus animi claruisset, 405Bnisi reliquorum ignaviae formido patuisset.

VERS. 38.-- Eo quod. (AUG., quaest. 30 in Num.) Circumpositionem vero altari, etc., usque ad qui minus diligenter intendunt, eodem facta ordine quo narrantur.

VERS. 39.-- Tulit. (ORIG., hom. 9 in Num.) Apud Deum nihil est otiosum, etc., usque ad cum universorum cineribus disperirent?

VERS. 40.-- Sicut passus est. (ID.) Posteris quoque datur exemplum, ne quis praesumptione superbi spiritus non sibi a Deo datum munus pontificatus invaderet; sed illi cedat quem non ambitio humana, non favor corruptus, nec largitio damnanda asciverit.

405CVERS. 43.-- Moyses et Aaron. (ID.) In Sodomis quidem, etc., usque ad per quos interitum possumus evadere.

Operuit nubes. (ID.) Non enim legimus antea quod obtexerit nubes tabernaculum, etc., usque ad et pericula maris ac fluminum, etc. (II Cor. XI).

VERS. 41.-- Recedite. (ID.) Jubentur ergo Moyses et Aaron, etc., usque ad sunt enim mortui qui in ignem aeternum mittuntur, vivi autem qui mittuntur ad regnum.

VERS. 48.-- Et stans inter. (GREG.) Aaron, ut iram Dei placaret, thuribulum sumpsit atque inter viventes et mortuos stetit, quia sacerdotis est negotium Deo orationem fundere, et pro justis ne cadant et pro peccatoribus ut resurgant, unde: Intret oratio 405Dmea, sicut incensum in conspectu tuo (Psal. LXXXVII).

(ISID.) Aaron thuribulum accipiens occurrit quassationi, et stans in medio vivorum et mortuorum, lethalem plagam objectu suo quasi quidam murus excludit. Iste sacerdos Christus qui ruinam mortis in mundo aspiciens occurrit a summo coelo, venitque obvius quasi gigas ad currendam viam, stetitque inter vivos et mortuos, quia natus et mortuus est, sicque thuribulum passionis accipiens, et in odorem suavitatis praetendens, suspendit ignis aeterni perniciem, et inimicam pertulit mortem.

Et plaga cessavit. (ORIG.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad luteus enim et terrenus sensus non capit mysteria.

406ADicitur etiam corpus nostrum vas fictile vel littera legis, in eo quod Apostolus ait: Habentes thesaurum istum in vasis fictilibus (II Cor. IV): quia in corpore positis thesaurum gratiae per Spiritum sanctum Dominus largitur; et in sermonibus legis qui contemnuntur, quia nulla arte grammatica expositi videntur, reconditus sit thesaurus sapientiae et scientiae Dei.

CHAPTER XVI.

VERS. 12-- That he should call Dathan? (AUG., quaest. 26 in Num.) What is this, that Dathan and Abiron, called by Moses in the sedition, etc., as far as so that they said not, We will not go up, but, We do not go up, by a certain manner of speech.

VERS. 26.-- Depart from the tents. (AUG., quaest. 27.) Another reading, etc., as far as the just may shine as the sun in the kingdom of their Father (Matth. XIII).

405A(ISID.) in Num., tom. V.) By Dathan, and Abiron, and Core, etc., as far as they shall perish in eternal fire, the vengeance being made ready.

And they went down. (AUG., quaest. 29.) Another reading, etc., as far as to be reserved for punishment as it were in the prisons of hell.

VERS. 37-- Command Eleazar. (ID., quaest. 30.) Another reading, etc., as far, as because by them an example was given to the rest, what they should fear.

Let him take up the censers. (ORIG., hom. 9 in Num.) Core holds the figure of those who rise up against the faith of the Church and the doctrine of the truth, etc., as far as: for they are sanctified and offered to the Lord.

(ID.) It can also be understood in another way, etc., as far as for the virtue of their mind would not have shone forth so magnificently, 405Bhad not the fear of the cowardice of the rest lain open.

VERS. 38.-- Forasmuch as. (AUG., quaest. 30 in Num.) But the setting round about the altar, etc., as far as who attend less diligently, the things done being in the same order in which they are narrated.

VERS. 39.-- He took. (ORIG., hom. 9 in Num.) With God nothing is idle, etc., as far as when they were perishing with the ashes of them all?

VERS. 40.-- As he suffered. (ID.) To those that come after also an example is given, that no one should by the presumption of a proud spirit seize upon the office of the high priesthood not given to him by God; but let him yield to that man whom no human ambition, no corrupt favour, nor any damnable largess has brought in.

405CVERS. 43.-- Moses and Aaron. (ID.) In Sodom indeed, etc., as far as through whom we are able to escape destruction.

The cloud covered. (ID.) For we do not read before this that a cloud covered the tabernacle, etc., as far as and perils of the sea and of rivers, etc. (II Cor. XI).

VERS. 41.-- Depart ye. (ID.) Moses therefore and Aaron are commanded, etc., as far as for they are dead who are sent into eternal fire, but they are alive who are sent unto the kingdom.

VERS. 48.-- And standing between. (GREG.) Aaron, that he might appease the wrath of God, took the censer and stood between the living and the dead, because it is the priest's business to pour out prayer to God, both for the just that they fall not and for sinners that they may rise again, whence: Let my prayer 405Denter in, as incense in thy sight (Psal. LXXXVII). [Vulg. Ps 140:2 reads Dirigatur, "let it be directed"; and at Ps 87:3 the words stand in another order]

(ISID.) Aaron, taking the censer, ran to meet the shattering, and standing in the midst of the living and the dead, shut out the deadly stroke by his own interposing, as it were a kind of wall. This priest is Christ, who, beholding the ruin of death in the world, ran from the height of heaven, and came to meet it as a giant to run the way, and stood between the living and the dead, because he was born and died, and so, taking the censer of his passion, and holding it forth for an odour of sweetness, he stayed the destruction of eternal fire, and bore the death that was his enemy.

And the plague ceased. (ORIG.) Another reading, etc., as far as for a muddy and earthly sense does not take in the mysteries.

406AOur body also is called an earthen vessel, or the letter of the law, in that the Apostle says: Having this treasure in earthen vessels (II Cor. IV): [Vulg. 2 Cor 4:7 Habemus autem thesaurum istum, "But we have this treasure"] because to those set in the body the Lord bestows the treasure of grace through the Holy Spirit; and in the words of the law which are despised, because they seem set forth with no art of grammar, there lies hidden the treasure of the wisdom and knowledge of God.

CAPUT XVII.

VERS. 1.-- A cunctis principibus. Omnis princeps habet virgam, quia sine ea populum regere non potest. Unde Paulus, quia princeps populi erat, dicebat. Quid vultis, in virga veniam ad vos, an in charitate? (I Cor. IV.)

406B(ORIG.) Omnes principes tribuum necesse est ut habeant virgas, etc., usque ad et cum nucibus sacerdotalium pomorum copiam invenisse.

VERS. 5.-- Germinabit virga. (ORIG., ubi supra.) Unum fructum promisit Deus in virga et dedit plura, etc., usque ad sed quae in sola sapientia continentur.

VERS. 8.-- Invenit germinasse virgam, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra.) Cum sacerdotium Aaron, etc., usque ad, sed nostra adhuc corpora usque in finem mundi differunt a gloria resurrectionis.

(ID.) Virga arida germinat, etc., usque ad corporis in resurrectione germinabit.

Virgam, (GREG., lib. IV Moral., cap. 29.) Carnem Christi, quae de radice Jesse succisa et mortificata, etc., usque ad corona martyrum, gratia continentium.
