406CTurgentibus, etc. (ORIG.) Hac vero ratione, etc., usque ad sed secundum Scripturam in mille generationes.

(ORIG., ubi supra.) Possumus etiam sic intelligere eorum, etc., usque ad qui sunt charitas, gaudium, pax et caeterae virtutes (Gal. V).

CHAPTER XVII.

VERS. 1.-- From all the princes. Every prince has a rod, because without it he cannot rule the people. Whence Paul, because he was a prince of the people, said. What will you, shall I come to you with a rod, or in charity? (I Cor. IV.)

406B(ORIG.) It is necessary that all the princes of the tribes should have rods, etc., as far as and with the nuts to have found an abundance of priestly fruits.

VERS. 5.-- The rod shall blossom. (ORIG., ubi supra.) God promised one fruit in the rod and gave many, etc., as far as but those things which are contained in wisdom alone.

VERS. 8.-- He found that the rod had blossomed, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra.) When the priesthood of Aaron, etc., as far as, but our bodies as yet differ from the glory of the resurrection until the end of the world.

(ID.) The dry rod blossoms, etc., as far as shall blossom in the resurrection of the body.

The rod, (GREG., lib. IV Moral., cap. 29.) The flesh of Christ, which, cut down and mortified from the root of Jesse, etc., as far as the crown of martyrs, the grace of the continent.

406CThe buds swelling, etc. (ORIG.) But by this reasoning, etc., as far as but according to the Scripture unto a thousand generations.

(ORIG., ubi supra.) We can also understand thus concerning them, etc., as far as which are charity, joy, peace and the other virtues (Gal. V).

CAPUT XVIII.

(RAB., in Num., tom. II.) VERS. 1.-- Tu et Filii, etc. Alia littera, etc., usque ad et pertinere dicit ad sacerdotem.

(ORIG. hom. 10 in Num.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad remissionem peccatorum significari.

(ID.) Jesus pontifex noster, etc., usque ad scit nos 406DDeus idoneos non esse martyrio.

(ID.) Quaerendum est quomodo aliqui sancti dicantur, etc., usque ad sanctus enim est, qui peccatum suum per pontificem curat.

VERS. 7.-- Tu autem et filii. (ID., hom. 16.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad quae si bene excolamus fructum vitae afferent nobis.

Tu autem et filii tui. Allegorice. Perfectiores scilicet in Ecclesia, sancti doctores, quorum officium est sacramenta divina manibus contrectare, vasa sancta, id est Scripturae mystica verba ventilare et ad intellectum perducere (II Par. XXVI). Caetera autem turba meditetur diligenter quae capere potest, et quae magistrorum discretio jubet.

Si quis externus, etc. Non de tribu Levi. Ozias 407Aenim de tribu Juda, quia usurpavit sacerdotium, lepra percussus est.

VERS. 8.-- Locutus est Dominus, etc. (RAB., in Num., tom. II.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad qui cum semper comedatur semper permanet, imo augetur.

Primitiarum. Primitiae litterales consumuntur, spirituales custodiuntur. Quanto enim verbum Dei amplius sumpseris, tanto abundabit.

Omnia quae sanctificantur a filiis Israel. (ORIG., hom. 11.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad non sanctificatur, nec inter primitias recipitur.

(ORIG.) Videtur autem secundum litteram proselytos excludere, etc., usque ad qui faciunt patres in Ecclesiam introire.

(ID.) Ter etiam in anno apparere jubetur omne 407Bmasculinum, non femininum (Exod. XXIII).

A filiis, non a filiabus. Non enim otiose alibi nominat cum filiis etiam filias, alibi filiarum non facit mentionem. Quae tamen specialiter non ad sexum, sed ad discretionem animarum referenda sunt.

(ID.) Difficile invenies sanctos, quibus Deus eximium praebeat testimonium filias genuisse, Abraham et Isaac non genuerunt filias. Solus Jacob unam filiam genuit, et ipsa fratribus et parentibus opprobrium fuit, et vindictae furorem fratribus incitavit.

VERS. 9.-- Et hoc sit vobis ex his quae sanctificantur sanctis de sacrificiis, etc. (ID.) Spiritus sanctus ita sanctus est, etc., usque ad tertio autem loco ex hoc fructu diligere debeo etiam inimicos meos.

(ID., hom. 11.) Primitias omnium frugum sacerdotibus 407Clex mandat offerri, etc., usque ad invenies apud Mosen legis titulo signari.

(ID.) Ubi vero dicit: Non occides, Non adulterabis, et hujusmodi, legis titulum non praemisit. Haec enim mandata sunt, et ideo non exinaniuntur apud discipulos Evangelii, sed implentur. Non enim mandatum, sed lux umbram habere futurorum dicitur bonorum

(ID.) Notandum vero quod lex dupliciter dicitur. Generaliter enim omnia haec, mandatum, justificationes, praecepta, testimonia, judicia lex dicuntur. Specialiter autem aliqua pars eorum quae in lege scripta sunt, ut supra dicta. Generaliter vero lex significatur, cum dicitur: Non veni solvere legem, sed adimplere (Matth. V). Et alibi: Plenitudo legis est dilectio 407D(Rom. XIII). Omnia enim quae in lege scripta sunt legem nominavit.

(ID.) Alibi quoque: Juste, inquit, sectare quod justum est (Deut. XVI). Et quid opus est etiam in his allegoriam quaerere, cum aedificet littera?

(ID.) Quaero quoque si aliqua, etc., usque ad adjecit tamen Apostolus: Haec autem sunt allegorica (Gal. IV).

Omnis oblatio, etc. (ID.) Est autem sapientis scribae et docti de regno Dei, ut sciat de his thesauris proferre nova et vetera, et quomodo penitus litteram occidentem abjiciat et spiritum vivificantem requirat (Matth. XIII; I Cor. III), aut confirmet utilem et necessariam litterae doctrinam, aut manente historia 408Aetiam mysticum sensum introducat, sicut in praesenti loco.

(ID.) Utile est enim sacerdotibus Evangelii offerre primitias, etc., usque ad discipulis autem dicitur: Qui irascitur fratri suo, reus erit judicio (Matth. V).

(ID.) Christus nos redemit de maledicto legis, non de maledicto mandati vel testimonii, vel judiciorum, ne scilicet subjecti essemus circumcisioni carnis et sabbatorum observationi, et hujusmodi quae continentur in lege, non in mandatis (Galat. III). Abundat ergo justitia nostra, plus quam scribarum et Pharisaeorum (Matth. V); et illi de fructibus terrae gustare non audent, priusquam primitias sacerdotibus offerant, et Levitis decimas separent: ego autem nihil horum faciens, fructibus terrae ita abutar, 408But sacerdos et Levita et Dei altare non sentiat.

(ID.) Petrus quoque et Paulus, etc., usque ad et singularum Ecclesiarum eligit et offert primitias.

(ID.) Quisquis etiam doctor, quia docet, praedicat et instruit auditores, agrum Ecclesiae suae, id est, credentium corda videtur excolere. Habet quoque culturae suae fructum, et in fructu electum aliquem et praecipuum, quem quasi primitias offerat, et alium pro primogenitis, alium pro decimis.

(ID.) Habet forte aliquis primogenitos et inferiores, quos neque pro primitiis, neque pro primogenitis, sed pro decimis possit offerre.

VERS. II.-- Jure perpetuo. Quomodo potest aeternum esse quod visibile est? Quae enim videntur temporalia sunt (I Cor. V), primitiae autem quae offeruntur 408CAaron, visibiles sunt; circumcisionem quoque et azyma et pascha visibile, necesse est aeternum non esse, sed temporale. Invisibilis ergo circumcisio, quae in occulto est, aeterna est, et azyma sinceritatis et veritatis (Rom. II). Sic et haec ei, qui non in manifesto, sed in occulto Judaeus, qui spiritu, non littera legem custodit, secundum interiorem hominem legitima aeterna dicuntur (II Cor. IV).

VERS. 12.-- Omnem medullam, etc. (ORIG.) Ostendimus mandatum de primitiis frugum, etc., usque ad unde per angelum videtur quasi primitiae Deo oblatus.

Christus est primitiae primitiarum. Cornelius primitiae gentium. Alii primitiae Ecclesiarum, sicut quidam primitiae Asiae, alii Achaiae.

408D(ORIG.) Aderit unusquisque angelorum, etc., usque ad quae non angeli vel principis, sed Dei portio est.

(ID.) Offerunt ergo angeli ex nobis primitias, etc., usque ad ut quaerant quae sursum sunt, non quae super terram.

(ID.) Offerunt angeli primitias, etc., usque ad alius princeps multorum esse ponitur, alius principatui subjectus.

(ID.) Agri angelorum corda nostra sunt, quisque eorum ex agro quem colit offert primitias Domino.

(ID.) Sicut Christus Rex regum, etc., usque ad sufficeret nobis haec gratia.

VERS. 13.-- Universa frugum. (AUG., quaest. 32 in 409ANum.) Primogenita omnia, etc., usque ad quae primitus sumebantur.

VERS. 15.-- Quidquid primum. (ORIG.) Possunt forte illi primogeniti dici, qui cum mulieribus non sunt coinquinati, vel etiam martyres; primitia quoque Ecclesiae possunt dici virgines (Apoc. 14); decimae vero, qui post conjugium continentes sunt.

Omne. (RAB.) In utroque testamento ministris altaris oblationes et decimae ad necessaria tribuuntur, ne terrenis possessionibus audeant inhiare, nec lucrum de ministerio quaerere: unde, Gratis accepistis, gratis date. Nolite possidere aurum neque argentum, neque pecuniam (Matth. X).

Mystice autem decimae observantiam legis significant, vel sinceritatem integritatis fidei, vel perfectionem 409Bboni operis, quae maxime habere debent qui ministerio divino se exhibent. In denario enim numero creaturae et creatoris cognitio comprehenditur, quia septenarius respicit ad humanam naturam, tribus scilicet vim animae ostendentibus; unde, Diliges Dominum Deum tuum ex toto corde tuo, et ex tota anima tua, et ex tota fortitudine tua (Deut. VI). Corpus autem ex quatuor constat elementis. Septenarium autem, si ternario numero fidem et confessionem Trinitatis exprimenti adjunxeris, denarium complebis.

VERS. 19.-- Pactum salis. (RAB.) Pactum salis nominat oblationum universitatem, quae ad ministerium vel ad jus sacerdotum pertinet, unde in Levitico: Quidquid obtuleris sacrificii sale condies, etc. 409C(Levit. II).

Mystice ostenditur sapientiam convenire sacerdotibus et praedicatoribus divinae legis, non saecularem, sed apostolicam, de qua Paulus dicit: Sapientiam loquimur inter perfectos, sapientiam non hujus saeculi (I Cor. II). De qua apostolis dicitur: Vos estis sal terrae (Matth. V). Sicut enim omnis cibus sale conditur, sic omnis sermo apostolica sapientia. Aaron ergo et filiis ejus pactum est salis sempiternum, quia non solum episcopis necessaria est sapientia, sed etiam presbyteris, diaconis et omnibus ordinibus: et hoc coram Deo, id est, ut pura et simplex sine versutia, et malitia Deo placeat. Sapientia enim hujus mundi inimica est Deo (Rom. VIII).

CHAPTER XVIII.

(RAB., in Num., tom. II.) VERS. 1.-- Thou and thy sons, etc. Another reading, etc., as far as and he says that it pertains to the priest.

(ORIG. hom. 10 in Num.) Another reading, etc., as far as that the remission of sins is signified.

(ID.) Jesus our high priest, etc., as far as God knows that we 406Dare not fit for martyrdom.

(ID.) It must be asked how certain men are called holy, etc., as far as for he is holy who has his own sin healed by the high priest.

VERS. 7.-- But thou and thy sons. (ID., hom. 16.) Another reading, etc., as far as which, if we till them well, will bring us the fruit of life.

But thou and thy sons. Allegorically. The more perfect, namely, in the Church, the holy teachers, whose office it is to handle the divine sacraments with their hands, the holy vessels, that is, to winnow the mystical words of Scripture and to bring them through to understanding (II Par. XXVI). But let the rest of the crowd meditate diligently on what it can take in, and on what the discretion of the masters commands.

If any stranger, etc. Not of the tribe of Levi. For Ozias, 407Abeing of the tribe of Juda, because he usurped the priesthood, was struck with leprosy.

VERS. 8.-- The Lord spoke, etc. (RAB., in Num., tom. II.) Another reading, etc., as far as which, though it be always eaten, always remains, nay rather is increased.

Of the firstfruits. Literal firstfruits are consumed, spiritual ones are kept. For the more of the word of God thou shalt take, the more it will abound.

All things that are sanctified by the children of Israel. (ORIG., hom. 11.) Another reading, etc., as far as it is not sanctified, nor is it received among the firstfruits.

(ORIG.) But according to the letter he seems to exclude proselytes, etc., as far as who make the fathers to enter into the Church.

(ID.) Thrice also in the year every 407Bmale is commanded to appear, not the female (Exod. XXIII).

By the sons, not by the daughters. For not idly does he in one place name the daughters also together with the sons, and in another place make no mention of daughters. Which things nevertheless are to be referred specially not to sex, but to the distinction of souls.

(ID.) With difficulty wilt thou find holy men to whom God gives a singular testimony that they begat daughters, Abraham and Isaac begat no daughters. Jacob alone begat one daughter, and she herself was a reproach to her brothers and to her parents, and stirred up in her brothers the fury of vengeance.

VERS. 9.-- And let this be to you out of those things which are sanctified to the holy, of the sacrifices, etc. (ID.) The Holy Spirit is so holy, etc., as far as but in the third place, out of this fruit I ought to love even my enemies.

(ID., hom. 11.) The firstfruits of all crops the law commands to be offered to the priests, 407Cetc., as far as thou wilt find it marked in Moses with the title of law.

(ID.) But where he says: Thou shalt not kill, Thou shalt not commit adultery, and the like, he has not prefixed the title of law. For these are commandments, and therefore they are not made void among the disciples of the Gospel, but are fulfilled. For it is not the commandment, but the light [lux; read lex, "the law"] that is said to have a shadow of the good things to come

(ID.) But it is to be noted that law is spoken of in two ways. For generally all these, commandment, justifications, precepts, testimonies, judgments, are called law. But specially, some part of those things which are written in the law, as the things said above. But law is signified generally when it is said: I came not to destroy the law, but to fulfil (Matth. V). And elsewhere: The fulness of the law is love 407D(Rom. XIII). For he named as law all things which are written in the law.

(ID.) Elsewhere also: Justly, says he, pursue that which is just (Deut. XVI). And what need is there to seek an allegory even in these things, when the letter edifies?

(ID.) I ask also whether some things, etc., as far as yet the Apostle added: But these things are an allegory (Gal. IV).

Every oblation, etc. (ID.) But it belongs to a wise scribe, and one instructed concerning the kingdom of God, that he should know how to bring forth out of these treasures new things and old, and how utterly to cast away the letter that kills and to seek the spirit that gives life (Matth. XIII; I Cor. III), or to confirm the useful and necessary doctrine of the letter, or, the history remaining, 408Ato bring in also the mystical sense, as in the present place.

(ID.) For it is useful to offer the firstfruits to the priests of the Gospel, etc., as far as but to the disciples it is said: Whosoever is angry with his brother shall be in danger of the judgment (Matth. V).

(ID.) Christ redeemed us from the curse of the law, not from the curse of the commandment or of the testimony, or of the judgments, namely that we should not be subject to the circumcision of the flesh and to the observance of sabbaths, and to things of this kind which are contained in the law, not in the commandments (Galat. III). Our justice therefore abounds more than that of the scribes and Pharisees (Matth. V); and they dare not taste of the fruits of the earth before they offer the firstfruits to the priests, and set apart the tithes for the Levites: but I, doing none of these things, so misuse the fruits of the earth, 408Bthat the priest and the Levite and the altar of God feel it not.

(ID.) Peter also and Paul, etc., as far as and he chooses and offers the firstfruits of the several Churches.

(ID.) Every teacher too, because he teaches, preaches and instructs his hearers, is seen to till the field of his own Church, that is, the hearts of believers. He has also the fruit of his tillage, and in that fruit some chosen and chief one, whom he may offer as firstfruits, and another for the firstborn, another for the tithes.

(ID.) Someone perhaps has firstborn and lesser ones, whom he could offer neither for firstfruits, nor for firstborn, but for tithes.

VERS. II.-- By perpetual right. How can that be eternal which is visible? For the things which are seen are temporal (I Cor. V), but the firstfruits which are offered 408Cto Aaron are visible; circumcision too, and the unleavened bread and the visible pasch, must needs not be eternal, but temporal. Therefore the invisible circumcision, which is in secret, is eternal, and the unleavened bread of sincerity and truth (Rom. II). So also these things are called eternal ordinances, according to the inward man, for him who is a Jew not in what is manifest, but in what is secret, who keeps the law by the spirit, not by the letter (II Cor. IV).

VERS. 12.-- All the marrow, etc. (ORIG.) We have shown the commandment concerning the firstfruits of the crops, etc., as far as whence through an angel he seems to be offered to God as firstfruits.

Christ is the firstfruits of the firstfruits. Cornelius the firstfruits of the Gentiles. Others the firstfruits of the Churches, as certain ones the firstfruits of Asia, others of Achaia.

408D(ORIG.) Each one of the angels will be present, etc., as far as which is the portion not of an angel or of a prince, but of God.

(ID.) The angels therefore offer firstfruits from among us, etc., as far as that they may seek the things that are above, not the things that are upon the earth.

(ID.) The angels offer firstfruits, etc., as far as one is set to be prince of many, another subject to a principality.

(ID.) The fields of the angels are our hearts, each of them offers to the Lord the firstfruits out of the field which he tills.

(ID.) As Christ is King of kings, etc., as far as this grace would suffice us.

VERS. 13.-- All the firstlings of the crops. (AUG., quaest. 32 in 409ANum.) All the firstborn, etc., as far as which were taken first.

VERS. 15.-- Whatsoever first cometh forth. (ORIG.) Perhaps those can be called firstborn who are not defiled with women, or the martyrs also; virgins too can be called the firstfruits of the Church (Apoc. 14); but the tithes, those who are continent after marriage.

Every thing. (RAB.) In both testaments oblations and tithes are granted to the ministers of the altar for things necessary, lest they should dare to gape after earthly possessions, nor to seek gain from their ministry: whence, Freely have you received, freely give. Do not possess gold nor silver, nor money (Matth. X).

But mystically the tithes signify the observance of the law, or the sincerity of the integrity of faith, or the perfection 409Bof a good work, which they ought most of all to have who devote themselves to the divine ministry. For in the number ten the knowledge of the creature and of the Creator is comprised, because the number seven looks to human nature, three, namely, showing the power of the soul; whence, Thou shalt love the Lord thy God with thy whole heart, and with thy whole soul, and with thy whole strength (Deut. VI). But the body consists of four elements. And if thou shalt join the number seven to the number three, which expresses the faith and confession of the Trinity, thou wilt complete the number ten.

VERS. 19.-- A covenant of salt. (RAB.) He names a covenant of salt the whole body of the oblations, which pertains to the ministry or to the right of the priests, whence in Leviticus: Whatsoever sacrifice thou shalt offer, thou shalt season it with salt, etc. 409C(Levit. II).

Mystically it is shown that wisdom befits priests and preachers of the divine law, not a secular wisdom but an apostolic, of which Paul says: We speak wisdom among the perfect, a wisdom not of this world (I Cor. II). Of which it is said to the apostles: You are the salt of the earth (Matth. V). For as all food is seasoned with salt, so is all discourse seasoned by apostolic wisdom. To Aaron therefore and to his sons the covenant of salt is everlasting, because wisdom is necessary not only to bishops, but also to priests, to deacons and to all orders: and this before God, that is, that being pure and simple, without craftiness and malice, it may please God. For the wisdom of this world is an enemy to God (Rom. VIII). [Vulg. Rom 8:7 sapientia carnis, "the wisdom of the flesh"]

CAPUT XIX.

  • 409D

VERS. 1.-- Locutusque est Dominus. (AUG., quaest. 33 in Num.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad et observationes legis profanabat.

(RAB.) Per masculum fortitudo, per feminam infirmitas significatur: per vaccam ergo infirmitas Dominicae incarnationis ad sacrificium assumpta figuratur. Mortuus est enim Christus ex infirmitate, sed vivit ex virtute Dei (II Cor. ult.).

Vaccam rufam, rubram scilicet sanguine passionis, unde: Dilectus meus candidus et rubicundus (Cant. V): candidus scilicet ex divinitate, rubicundus ex sanguine, candidus etiam per justitiam vitae.

(GREG., lib. VI Moral., tom. I.) Vaccam rufam 410Amactamus, cum carnem a lascivia voluptatis exstinguimus: quam cum hyssopo, ligno cedrino et cocco offerimus, quia cum maceratione corporis sacrificium fidei spei et charitatis adolemus.

VERS. 2.-- Nulla sit macula. (AUG., quaest. 33.) Sine vitio. Hanc enim carnem caeterae hostiae significabant, etc., usque ad quae non habuit in se vitium?

Nec portaverit. Omnis qui peccat, servus est peccati: quia ergo Christus nunquam peccavit, jugum non portavit.

(AUG.) Alia littera. Et non est super eam superpositum jugum, etc. Non enim subjugata est iniquitati, cui subjugatos inveniens liberavit, et vincula eorum dirupit. Unde: Dirupisti vincula mea, tibi 410Bsacrificabo hostiam laudis, etc. Super illius enim carnem non est positum jugum, qui potestatem habuit ponendi animam suam, et iterum sumendi eam.

VERS. 3.-- Tradetisque. Vacca ad immolandum sacerdoti traditur, quia Dominus incarnatus pro redemptione nostra sacrificium semetipsum fecit, datus manibus Judaici populi.

(AUG.) Alia littera. Et dabis eam ad Eleazarum sacerdotem, etc. Cur non ad Aaron? Forte praefiguratum est, non ad tempus quod tunc erat, sed ad posteros hujus sacerdotii, passionem Domini perventuram.

Qui ejectam extra castra. (ID.) Quia extra portam passus est Christus, ut nos a conversatione praesentis vitae quasi a civitate educeret (Heb. XIII).

410C(ID.) Et ejectam eam extra castra. Sic ejectus est Dominus passurus extra civitatem (Joan. XIX).

(ID.) In locum mundum, quia non habuit causam malam: Et occident eam in conspectu ejus, sic occisa est caro Christi in conspectu eorum qui futuri erant in novo testamento Domini sacerdotes.

Immolabit. (ISID.) Vaccam rufam (cujus cinis est expiatio populi) immolare non poterit, nisi qui terrena opera non fecerit, jugumque delicti non traxerit, et vinculis peccatorum ligatus non fuerit.

In conspectu omnium. (RAB.) Quia Christus in conspectu omnium quasi exstinctus est: in conspectu Patris immolatus, quia quod ante oculos hominum poena, hoc ante oculos Patris sacrificium fuit.

VERS. 4.-- Et tingens digitum in sanguine ejus. 410D(AUG.) Alia littera. Et accipiet Eleazar sanguinem ejus, et asperget contra faciem testimonii a sanguine ejus septies. Quia Christus secundum Scripturas fudit sanguinem in remissionem peccatorum, ideo contra faciem tabernaculi testimonii, quia non aliter declaratum est, quam fuerat divino testimonio praenuntiatum. Et ideo septies, quia ipse numerus ad emundationem pertinet spiritalem.

(RAB.) In digito discretio operationis signatur: in sanguine vaccae digitum tingere est in passione Domini nostra opera consecrari, ut passionem ejus, quam cognoscimus, imitemur: Passus est enim nobis relinquens exemplum, ut sequamur vestigia ejus (I Petr. II).

411A(ID.) Eleazar sacerdos immolat, et in sanguine digitum tingit, quia plebs Judaea ex reproborum parte humilitatem Domini usque ad passionem persecuta est. In electis vero operationem humilitatis ejus imitata

Asperget contra fores tabernaculi. Tabernaculum synagogam significat. Qui ergo tinxerit digitum, contra fores tabernaculi aspergat, id est, quisquis passionis ejus vias imitatur, synagogae non credenti et resistenti per exempla bona et praedicationem rectam signum bene vivendi praebeat.

(ID.) Asperget, id est, resistenti plebi per praedicationem rectam et opera, passionis Christi exempla monstrabit.

Septem vicibus. Quia septem sunt dona Spiritus 411Bsancti, quae per Christi sanguinem super Ecclesiae populos distribuuntur, in quo ab omni delicto purgantur.

(GREG., lib. VI Moral., cap. 27.) Septem vicibus, etc. In septenario perfectio significatur vel accipitur, unde et septem diebus universum tempus impletur, et Spiritus sanctus septiformis vocatur. Septem ergo vicibus in sanguine digitum intingere est perfecta operatione mysterium passionis imitari. Et septem vicibus contra synagogam aspergere est perfecta praedicatione passionem infidelibus nuntiare.

VERS. 5.-- Comburetque eam cunctis videntibus. (ID.) Per pellem et carnem et sanguinem et fimum, sacrificia in veteri testamento habita, quae infirmitatem Domini nuntiabant, significantur. Quia omnia 411Cpostmodum spiritaliter a patribus intellecta juxta exterius mysterium stercora sunt vocata, unde Paulus: Omnia arbitratur ut stercora (Philip. III), quae flammae traduntur. Quamvis enim primum carnaliter sint exhibita, postmodum per Spiritum sanctum intelligentiae igne consumpta sunt.

(AUG.) Et cremabunt eam in conspectu ejus. Puto quia crematio ad signum pertinet resurrectionis. Natura enim est ignis ut in superna moveatur, et in ipsum convertatur quod crematur. Nam et ipsum cremare de Graeco in Latinum ductum verbum est a suspensione. Quod vero additum est in conspectu ejus, id est, in conspectu sacerdotis, significare videtur, quia illis apparuit resurrectio Christi, qui futuri erant regale sacerdotium.

411DTam pelle et carnibus ejus, etc. (ID.) Quod vero sequitur: pellis ejus et carnes et sanguis ejus cum stercore ejus comburentur: id expositum est quomodo cremabitur, et significatum est quod non solum substantia mortalis corporis Christi, quae commemoratione pellis et carnium et sanguinis intimata est, sed etiam contumelia et abjectio plebis quae in stercore significaretur, converteretur in gloriam quam combustionis flamma significat.

(RAB.) Per pellem et carnem exterior Christi operatio significatur: per sanguinem subtilis et interna virtus exteriora facta vivificans; per fimum lassitudo, sitis, esuries, et timor mortis, et quaecunque ex humanitate pati voluit. Quidquid enim 412Adefectu mortalitatis habuit, quasi fimus in eo abjiciendum fuit; quae omnia igni cremanda sunt, id est, juxta veritatem Spiritus sancti intelligenda, ut quae ab eo corporaliter gesta sunt per incorporeum divinitatis Spiritum disposita sentiantur.

VERS. 6.-- Lignum quoque cedrinum, et hyssopum, coccumque bis tinctum.

(APOLLI.) De morte igitur sacrorum cecinit vates: Asperges me, Domine, hyssopo et mundabor, lavabis me, etc. (Psal. L), et cui patefactum erat tale mysterium expiationis, quae per sanguinem Christi futura erat, proposuit: Incerta et occulta sapientiae tuae manifestasti mihi.

(RAB.) Cedrus lignum altum et imputribile est. Hyssopus herba valde humilis, quae tamen inflationem 412Bamovet pulmonis. Per cedrum ergo altam, perseverans contemplatio; per hyssopum mansueti cordis humilitas signatur. Qui enim contemplatione ad superna tollitur, per humilitatis compassionem debet condescendere inferioribus, nec se ex eo quod habet erigat, sed his, qui illud non habent, praedicando libenter impendat.

(AUG.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad ut cum illo sit abscondita vita nostra.

(RAB.) Potest intelligi per cedrum spes, etc., usque ad sola in nobis charitas ad ultimum multiplicatur.

(GREG., Moral. lib. VI, cap. 17.) Mittatur in flammam, usque ad ut in ipsa discat opera recta quae faciat

412CHyssopum (ISID.) Baptismum, quia hyssopo tincto sanguine agni spargebantur (Exod. XII).

Coccumque bis tinctum. (ID.) Coccus bis tinctus, sanguis Christi quo mente et corpore abluimur. Haec tria purgant peccata, cedrus, id est fides crucis; hyssopus, baptismus vel martyrium; coccus, sanguinis Dominici sacramentum.

(RAB.) In cocco bis tincto virtus charitatis exprimitur, quae flamma sancti amoris succensa, duobus praeceptis exhiberi jubetur, dilectione scilicet Dei et proximi. Bis tinctus ergo coccus est charitas duplici praecepto formata, ut sic diligatur Deus ne deseratur proximus, et sic proximus, ne contemnatur Deus.

Sacerdos. (ID.) Omnes qui in Christo credimus 412Dsacerdotes sumus, unde: Vos autem estis genus electum, regale sacerdotium, etc. (I Petr. II). Alta ergo quae per contemplationem sentimus, et quod ex mansuetudine humilitatis agimus, quod per ignem charitatis exhibemus, passionis Christi sanguine tingere debemus, ut omne quod in nobis est, illum imitetur a quo est, quia extra imitationem passionis non tam virtus quam vitium est.

(ID.) Potest iste sacerdos, qui vaccae carnes, pellem, sanguinem, fimumque comburit, ordinem Judaici sacerdotii cum plebe supposita in necem Domini consentientis significare, unde sequitur: Et tunc demum lotis vestibus et corpore suo.

VERS 7.-- Et tunc demum lotis vestibus. (ID.) 413APotest intelligi, etc., usque ad recte, Colliget autem cineres vaccae vir mundus, etc.

(AUG.) Lavabit vestimenta sua sacerdos, etc., usque ad resurrectioni eam velut cremationi mandantes.

VERS. 8.-- Usque ad vesperam. (ID.) Ad vesperam sacerdos intrat, quia infidelis populus, qui usque ad vesperam, id est, finem mundi, extra mandata vitae, velut extra castra positus est, pravis operibus commaculatus, aqua baptismi et poenitentia lotus ad cognitionem in fine mundi quasi in castra revertitur.

VERS. 9.-- Colliget autem. (AUG.) Et congregabit homo, etc., usque ad quia extra celebrationem Judaicae consuetudinis evangelicus honor claruit.

413B(GREG., lib. VI Moral., cap. 17.) Vaccam sacerdos comburit, cineres vir mundus colligit, quia infirmitatem Domini Judaeus mactavit, subtilia ejus mysteria Gentilis intelligit. Mundi sunt qui passionem Christi gentibus praedicant.

Effundet eos. Quia Gentilis sacramenta quae agnovit, praedicare non cessat, sed nescientibus manifestat.

(RAB.) Vir mundus in loco purissimo vaccae cineres fundit, dum quilibet doctus ex gentilitate assumptus, munda per humilitatem gentilium corda requirit, et mysteria eis Redemptoris tradere non desistit. Quod cum agitur, fides gentium multiplicatur, divina virtus agnoscitur, disciplina praeceptorum tenetur. Unde subdit, ut sint multitudini filiorum 413CIsrael in custodiam: Israel enim interpretatur vir videns Deum; et bene conversa Gentilitas Israel dicitur, quae Deum jam fide contemplatur.

Extra castra. Quia omnis Gentilitas extra legem, extra sacrificium, extra synagogam.

Purissimo. Quia pravis ac pollutis mentibus non sunt secreta Dominicae incarnationis tradenda, sed his qui ad fidem venientes cor mundaverunt.

Multitudini filiorum (AUG., ubi supra. ) Alia littera etc., usque ad ut ablutus etiam extrinsecus lavaret quodammodo vestimenta sua.

In custodiam. Quia subtiliora sacramenta mundis Gentilium cordibus tradita in mentis munitionem vertuntur Quia dum secreta et subtilia operationis illius per imitationem attendimus, per hoc nobis immunditiam 413Dprovidemus, propter operationem, in custodiam nostram fiant cineres, propter baptismum vero in aquam aspersionis. Tunc cineres in nostram custodiam provide custodimus, cum Redemptorem nostrum pro iniquitatibus nostris passum consideramus sollicite. Perpendimus enim quid retribuat bonis, qui voluit mori pro malis, quid ad justitiam fidei adducti ejus gratiae debeamus, qui in iniquitate positi intercessionem mortis ejus accepimus.

VERS. 10.-- Habebunt hoc. (AUG., quaest. 33.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad quod significat vespera.

VERS. 11.-- Qui tetigerit ea. (AUG.) Qui tetigerit morticinum, etc. Incipit dicere quomodo immundi facti homines, etc., usque ad secundum hoc dictum 414Aarbitror per prophetam: In tribus et quatuor iniquitatibus non adversabor.

VERS. 12.-- Si die tertio, etc. (ID.) Si autem purificatus non fuerit die tertio, etc., usque ad adhuc, id est, etiam post mortem.

VERS. 13.-- Polluet tabernaculum, quantum in se est, secundum illud: Spiritum nolite exstinguere (I Thess. V), cum exstingui ille non possit. Nam si ex hoc tabernaculum immundum factum vellet intelligi, mundari juberet.

VERS. 15.-- Vas quod, etc. (RAB.) Tegmen operculi vel ligatura, est censura disciplinae, qua quisquis non premitur, quasi vas immundum pollutumque reprobatur. Quasi enim vas sine operculo vel ligatura polluitur, qui per studium ostentationis patens nullo 414Bvelamine taciturnitatis operitur.

VERS. 16.-- (AUG., quaest. 34.) Si quis in agro. Alia littera, etc., usque ad id est vulnere peremptum aut mortuum sine vulnere.

VERS. 19.-- Lavabit et se, etc. (ID.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad immunda erit usque ad vesperam.

CHAPTER XIX.

  • 409D

VERS. 1.-- And the Lord spoke. (AUG., quaest. 33 in Num.) Another reading, etc., as far as and he profaned the observances of the law.

(RAB.) By the male, strength, by the female, weakness is signified: therefore by the heifer is figured the weakness of the Lord's incarnation taken up unto sacrifice. For Christ died out of weakness, but he liveth by the power of God (II Cor. ult.). [Vulg. 2 Cor 13:4 etsi crucifixus est ex infirmitate, "although he was crucified through weakness"]

A red heifer, red, that is, with the blood of the passion, whence: My beloved is white and ruddy (Cant. V): white, namely, from his divinity, ruddy from his blood, white also through the justice of his life.

(GREG., lib. VI Moral., tom. I.) We slay the red heifer 410Awhen we quench the flesh from the wantonness of pleasure: and we offer her with hyssop, with cedar wood and with scarlet, because with the mortifying of the body we burn the sacrifice of faith, of hope and of charity.

VERS. 2.-- Let there be no blemish. (AUG., quaest. 33.) Without fault. For this flesh the other victims signified, etc., as far as which had no fault in itself?

Nor hath borne. Everyone who sins is the servant of sin: because therefore Christ never sinned, he bore not the yoke.

(AUG.) Another reading. And there is no yoke laid upon her, etc. For she was not made subject to iniquity, whose subjects he found and set free, and broke their bonds. Whence: Thou hast broken my bonds, to thee 410Bwill I sacrifice the sacrifice of praise, etc. For upon his flesh no yoke was laid, who had power to lay down his own soul, and to take it up again.

VERS. 3.-- And you shall deliver her. The heifer is delivered to the priest to be immolated, because the Lord incarnate made himself a sacrifice for our redemption, being given into the hands of the Jewish people.

(AUG.) Another reading. And thou shalt give her to Eleazar the priest, etc. Why not to Aaron? Perhaps it was prefigured that the passion of the Lord should come, not to the time which then was, but to those that came after of this priesthood.

Who, when she is cast out without the camp. (ID.) Because Christ suffered without the gate, that he might lead us out from the conversation of the present life as out of a city (Heb. XIII). [Vulg. Nm 19:3 eductam, "led out"]

410C(ID.) And her, being cast out without the camp. So was the Lord, being about to suffer, cast out without the city (Joan. XIX).

(ID.) Into a clean place, because he had no evil cause: And they shall kill her in his sight, so was the flesh of Christ slain in the sight of those who were to be priests of the Lord in the new testament.

He shall immolate. (ISID.) None will be able to immolate the red heifer (whose ashes are the expiation of the people) save he who has not done earthly works, and has not drawn the yoke of transgression, and has not been bound with the chains of sins.

In the sight of all. (RAB.) Because Christ was as it were extinguished in the sight of all: immolated in the sight of the Father, because what before the eyes of men was a punishment, this before the eyes of the Father was a sacrifice.

VERS. 4.-- And dipping his finger in her blood. 410D(AUG.) Another reading. And Eleazar shall take of her blood, and shall sprinkle of her blood seven times toward the face of the testimony. Because Christ according to the Scriptures shed his blood unto the remission of sins, therefore toward the face of the tabernacle of the testimony, because it was declared not otherwise than it had been foretold by the divine testimony. And therefore seven times, because that very number pertains to spiritual cleansing.

(RAB.) By the finger the discretion of working is signified: to dip the finger in the blood of the heifer is that our works be consecrated in the passion of the Lord, so that we may imitate his passion, which we know: For he suffered, leaving us an example, that we should follow his steps (I Petr. II). [Vulg. 1 Pt 2:21 vobis relinquens exemplum ut sequamini vestigia ejus, "leaving you an example that you should follow his steps"]

411A(ID.) Eleazar the priest immolates, and dips his finger in the blood, because the Jewish people, on the side of the reprobate, persecuted the humility of the Lord even unto the passion. But in the elect it imitated the working of his humility

He shall sprinkle toward the doors of the tabernacle. The tabernacle signifies the synagogue. He therefore who has dipped his finger, let him sprinkle toward the doors of the tabernacle, that is, whosoever imitates the ways of his passion, let him hold forth to the synagogue, which believes not and resists, by good examples and by right preaching, a sign of living well.

(ID.) He shall sprinkle, that is, to the resisting people he shall show, by right preaching and by works, the examples of the passion of Christ.

Seven times. Because there are seven gifts of the 411BHoly Spirit, which through the blood of Christ are distributed upon the peoples of the Church, in which they are cleansed from every offence.

(GREG., lib. VI Moral., cap. 27.) Seven times, etc. In the number seven perfection is signified or received, whence also in seven days the whole of time is fulfilled, and the Holy Spirit is called sevenfold. To dip the finger seven times in the blood, therefore, is to imitate the mystery of the passion by a perfect working. And to sprinkle seven times toward the synagogue is to announce the passion to unbelievers by a perfect preaching.

VERS. 5.-- And he shall burn her in the sight of all. (ID.) By the skin and the flesh and the blood and the dung are signified the sacrifices held in the old testament, which announced the weakness of the Lord. Because all these things, 411Cbeing afterwards understood spiritually by the fathers, were, in respect of the outward mystery, called dung, whence Paul: He counteth all things as dung (Philip. III), which are delivered to the flames. [Vulg. Phil 3:8 arbitror, "I count"] For although they were first exhibited carnally, afterwards through the Holy Spirit they were consumed by the fire of understanding.

(AUG.) And they shall burn her in his sight. I think that the burning pertains to the sign of the resurrection. For it is the nature of fire to move toward the things above, and that what is burnt be turned into itself. For to burn is itself a word drawn from the Greek into the Latin from hanging. But what is added, in his sight, that is, in the sight of the priest, seems to signify this, that the resurrection of Christ appeared to those who were to be a royal priesthood.

411DAs well her skin and her flesh, etc. (ID.) But that which follows: her skin and her flesh and her blood with her dung shall be burnt: by this it is set forth how she shall be burnt, and it is signified that not only the substance of the mortal body of Christ, which is intimated by the mention of the skin and of the flesh and of the blood, but also the contumely and abjection of the people, which was signified in the dung, should be turned into the glory which the flame of the burning signifies.

(RAB.) By the skin and the flesh is signified the outward working of Christ: by the blood, the subtle and inward power which quickens the outward deeds; by the dung, weariness, thirst, hunger, and the fear of death, and whatsoever he willed to suffer of his humanity. For whatsoever 412Ahe had by the defect of mortality was to be cast away in him as dung; all which things are to be burnt with fire, that is, to be understood according to the truth of the Holy Spirit, so that the things which were done by him bodily may be perceived to have been ordered through the incorporeal Spirit of his divinity.

VERS. 6.-- Cedar wood also, and hyssop, and scarlet twice dyed.

(APOLLI.) Of the death of the holy things, therefore, the prophet sang: Thou shalt sprinkle me, O Lord, with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed, thou shalt wash me, etc. (Psal. L), and he to whom such a mystery of expiation, which was to be through the blood of Christ, had been laid open, set forth: The uncertain and hidden things of thy wisdom thou hast made manifest to me. [Vulg. Ps 50:9 Asperges me hyssopo, et mundabor, without the vocative]

(RAB.) The cedar is a high and incorruptible wood. Hyssop is a very lowly herb, which nevertheless takes away the swelling 412Bof the lung. By the high cedar, therefore, persevering contemplation; by hyssop, the humility of a meek heart is signified. For he who is lifted up to the things above by contemplation ought, through the compassion of humility, to condescend to those beneath, and let him not raise himself up because of what he has, but let him willingly spend himself in preaching upon those who have it not.

(AUG.) Another reading, etc., as far as that our life may be hidden with him.

(RAB.) By the cedar hope can be understood, etc., as far as charity alone is multiplied in us to the last.

(GREG., Moral. lib. VI, cap. 17.) Let it be cast into the flame, as far as that in it he may learn the right works which he should do

412CHyssop (ISID.) Baptism, because they were sprinkled with hyssop dipped in the blood of the lamb (Exod. XII).

And scarlet twice dyed. (ID.) The scarlet twice dyed, the blood of Christ, by which we are washed in mind and in body. These three purge sins, the cedar, that is, faith in the cross; hyssop, baptism or martyrdom; the scarlet, the sacrament of the Lord's blood.

(RAB.) In the scarlet twice dyed is expressed the virtue of charity, which, kindled by the flame of holy love, is commanded to be shown in two precepts, namely in the love of God and of one's neighbour. The scarlet twice dyed, therefore, is charity formed by a twofold precept, so that God is so loved that the neighbour be not forsaken, and the neighbour so loved that God be not despised.

The priest. (ID.) All we who believe in Christ 412Dare priests, whence: But you are a chosen generation, a kingly priesthood, etc. (I Petr. II). The high things therefore which we perceive through contemplation, and what we do out of the meekness of humility, what we show forth through the fire of charity, we ought to dip in the blood of the passion of Christ, that everything which is in us may imitate him from whom it is, because outside the imitation of the passion there is not so much virtue as vice.

(ID.) This priest, who burns the flesh, the skin, the blood and the dung of the heifer, can signify the order of the Jewish priesthood, together with the people set under it consenting to the slaying of the Lord, whence there follows: And then at length, his garments and his body being washed.

VERS 7.-- And then at length, his garments being washed. (ID.) 413AIt can be understood, etc., as far as rightly, But a clean man shall gather up the ashes of the heifer, etc.

(AUG.) The priest shall wash his garments, etc., as far as committing her to the resurrection as it were to the burning.

VERS. 8.-- Until the evening. (ID.) The priest enters at evening, because the unbelieving people, which until the evening, that is, the end of the world, is set outside the commandments of life, as it were outside the camp, defiled with depraved works, washed with the water of baptism and with penance, returns at the end of the world to knowledge as it were into the camp.

VERS. 9.-- But he shall gather up. (AUG.) And a man shall gather together, etc., as far as because the honour of the Gospel shone forth outside the celebration of the Jewish custom.

413B(GREG., lib. VI Moral., cap. 17.) The priest burns the heifer, a clean man gathers the ashes, because the Jew slew the weakness of the Lord, the Gentile understands his subtle mysteries. They are clean who preach the passion of Christ to the Gentiles.

He shall pour them out. Because the Gentile ceases not to preach the sacraments which he has come to know, but manifests them to those who know them not.

(RAB.) A clean man pours out the ashes of the heifer in a most clean place, when any learned man taken up out of the Gentile world seeks out hearts of the Gentiles made clean through humility, and ceases not to deliver to them the mysteries of the Redeemer. And when this is done, the faith of the Gentiles is multiplied, the divine power is acknowledged, the discipline of the precepts is held fast. Whence he adds, that they may be kept for the multitude of the children 413Cof Israel: for Israel is interpreted a man seeing God; and rightly the converted Gentile world is called Israel, which already contemplates God by faith.

Without the camp. Because all the Gentile world is outside the law, outside sacrifice, outside the synagogue.

Most clean. Because the secrets of the Lord's incarnation are not to be delivered to depraved and polluted minds, but to those who, coming to the faith, have cleansed their heart.

To the multitude of the children (AUG., ubi supra. ) Another reading etc., as far as that being washed he might also in some fashion outwardly wash his garments.

For a keeping. Because the more subtle sacraments delivered to the clean hearts of the Gentiles are turned into a fortifying of the mind Because while we attend by imitation to the secret and subtle things of that working of his, by this we provide against uncleanness 413Dfor ourselves, and, on account of the working, let the ashes be for our keeping, but on account of baptism, for the water of sprinkling. Then do we providently keep the ashes for our keeping, when we consider carefully our Redeemer, who suffered for our iniquities. For we weigh what he will render to the good, who willed to die for the evil, what we owe to his grace, being brought to the justice of faith, we who, set in iniquity, received the intercession of his death.

VERS. 10.-- They shall have this. (AUG., quaest. 33.) Another reading, etc., as far as which the evening signifies.

VERS. 11.-- He that toucheth these things. (AUG.) He that toucheth a dead body, etc. He begins to say how men are made unclean, etc., as far as according to this saying 414AI judge it to be, through the prophet: In three and four iniquities I will not be adverse. [Vulg. Nm 19:11 Qui tetigerit cadaver hominis, "he that toucheth the corpse of a man"]

VERS. 12.-- If on the third day, etc. (ID.) But if he shall not have been purified on the third day, etc., as far as still, that is, even after death.

VERS. 13.-- He shall defile the tabernacle, as much as in him lies, according to that: Extinguish not the Spirit (I Thess. V), though he cannot be extinguished. For if he wished the tabernacle to be understood as made unclean by this, he would command it to be cleansed.

VERS. 15.-- The vessel that, etc. (RAB.) The covering of a lid, or the binding, is the censure of discipline, by which whosoever is not restrained is reproved as an unclean and polluted vessel. For he is polluted as a vessel without a lid or a binding, who, lying open through the zeal of ostentation, is covered by no 414Bveil of silence.

VERS. 16.-- (AUG., quaest. 34.) If any man in the field. Another reading, etc., as far as that is, slain by a wound or dead without a wound.

VERS. 19.-- He shall wash both himself, etc. (ID.) Another reading, etc., as far as she shall be unclean until the evening.

CAPUT XX.

VERS. 20.-- Veneruntque filii, etc. (ISID., in Num., tom. V.) Tricesima tertia mansio est in deserto Sin: haec est Cades. Sin autem interpretatur sancta, per antiphrasin: sicut dicitur lucus cum minime luceat; vel bellum, cum sit horridum. In hac mansione Maria moritur et sepelitur, quae significat prophetiam mortuam, sicut Moyses et Aaron legis 414Cet sacerdotii finem, qui non valent ad terram promissionis pervenire, nec populum de solitudine hujus mundi educere, quod facit solus Jesus, id est, Salvator Dei filius.

Percutiens virga. (AUG., quaest. 35.) Quia de petra aqua educta est, etc., usque ad duo enim ligna sunt crux.

VERS. 12.-- Quia non credidistis. (ISID., in Num. ) Hic Moyses Dominum offendit, etc., usque ad Christus Dei virtus et Dei sapientia est.

VERS. 13.-- Haec est aqua contradictionis, ubi jurgati filii, etc. (AUG.) Quod de aqua illa dictum est, etc., usque ad de ipso enim dicitur: In signum cui contradicetur, etc. (Luc. II).

VERS. 22.-- In montem Hor, qui est in finibus. 414D(RAB.) Tricesima quarta mansio est, etc., usque ad ad paradisum ascensus.

VERS. 30.-- Omnis autem multitudo. (BED.) Aaron plangitur, Jesus non plangitur, quia in lege descensus ad inferum, in Evangelio ad paradisum transmigratio. Flevit populus triginta diebus, quia ante legem et sub lege et sub gratia synagoga non recipit spirituale gaudium, sed super occidentem litteram moesta incumbit.

CHAPTER XX.

VERS. 20.-- And the children came, etc. (ISID., in Num., tom. V.) The thirty-third station is in the desert of Sin: this is Cades. But Sin is interpreted holy, by antiphrasis: as a grove is called lucus when it shines not at all; or war is called bellum, when it is horrid. In this station Mary dies and is buried, who signifies prophecy dead, as Moses and Aaron signify the end of the law 414Cand of the priesthood, who avail not to come to the land of promise, nor to lead the people out of the wilderness of this world, which Jesus alone does, that is, the Saviour, the Son of God.

Striking with the rod. (AUG., quaest. 35.) Because water was brought out of the rock, etc., as far as for two pieces of wood are the cross.

VERS. 12.-- Because you have not believed. (ISID., in Num. ) Here Moses offends the Lord, etc., as far as Christ is the power of God and the wisdom of God.

VERS. 13.-- This is the water of contradiction, where the children strove, etc. (AUG.) What was said of that water, etc., as far as for of him it is said: For a sign which shall be contradicted, etc. (Luc. II).

VERS. 22.-- Into mount Hor, which is in the borders. 414D(RAB.) It is the thirty-fourth station, etc., as far as the ascent to paradise.

VERS. 30.-- But all the multitude. (BED.) Aaron is bewailed, Jesus is not bewailed, because in the law there is a descent to hell, in the Gospel a passing over to paradise. The people wept thirty days, because before the law and under the law and under grace the synagogue receives not spiritual joy, but lies mourning over the letter that kills.

CAPUT XXI.

VERS. 1.-- Quod cum audisset. Audivit Chananaeus quod venisset Israel in locum exploratorum, ubi quondam offendit populus, inivit praelium, et captivum 415Aduxit Israel. Rursumque in eodem loco pugnatur, ex voto victor vincitur, victi superant. In quo intelligitur, ut cum nos Dei auxilio destitutos invaserint hostes et captivaverint, non desperemus: potest enim fieri ut vincamus ubi victi sumus.

VERS. 3.-- Anathema. (AUG., quaest. 40 in Num.) Alia littera etc., usque ad idem est quod devoveo, detestor.

VERS. 4.-- Profecti sunt, etc. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam. ) Tricesima quinta mansio, etc., usque ad ore autem confessio ad salutem (Rom. X).

VERS. 8.-- Fac serpentem (ISID. in Num., tom. V.) Morsus serpentum exaltato et inspecto aeneo serpente sanabantur, etc., usque ad vivit per Divinitatem quasi aeneus.

415BVERS. 10.-- Profectique filii Israel castrametati, etc. Tricesima septima mansio est in Oboth, quae vertitur in magos vel pythones. Quia post imaginem Dei, quae in cordis ratione monstratur, et confessione fidei, quae ore profertur, consurgunt serpentes et maleficae artes; nos autem omni custodia servantes cor nostrum, obturemus aures, ne audiamus voces incantantium et carmina sirenarum.

VERS. 11.-- Unde egressi. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) In Jeabarim, etc., usque ad hoc autem eorum proprie est qui solis justitiae ortum considerant.

VERS. 12.-- Venerunt ad torrentem Zareth, etc. (RAB.) Tricesima nona mansio fuit in Dibongad, etc., usque ad sed propositam nobis solitudinem timeamus.

415CVERS. 14.-- In libro bellorum. (AUG., quaest. 42.) Non dixit in quo libro scriptum sit, etc., usque ad scriberetur in aliquo eorum libro bellum Domini.

VERS. 16.-- Ex eo loco, etc. (ORIG., hom. 12 in Num.) Quid est quod magnopere praecipit Dominus, etc., usque ad quia una est substantia et natura Trinitatis.

(ID., ibid.) Possunt et illi putei videri, etc., usque ad et terram removit, quam injecerant Philistaei.

Notandum autem quia, dum viveret Abraham, non sunt ausi Philistaei replere puteos aut injicere terram, sed eo mortuo invaluerunt et insidiati sunt puteis ejus, sed reparantur per Isaac. Puer quoque Abrahae ad puteos invenit Rebeccam, quae interpretatur Patientia: haec efficitur uxor Isaac, sed apud 415Dputeos (Gen. XXIV, XXIX); similiter Jacob cum ad Mesopotamiam venit habens praeceptum patris, ne alienigenam duceret uxorem, apud puteos invenit Rachel; et Moyses Sephoram (Exod. II). Si ergo intellexisti quae et quales sint sanctorum uxores, si vis patientiam in conjugium sumere, vel sapientiam et alias animi virtutes, et dicere quod de sapientia dictum est: Hanc quaesivi mihi ducere uxorem (Sap. VIII), esto assidue circa puteos istos, et ibi reperies hujusmodi conjugem, quia apud viventis verbi fluenta certum est habitare virtutes. Sunt ergo multi putei intra animam, et alii plurimi in singulis Scripturarum sermonibus et sensibus: est tamen hic eminentior quem foderunt, non quicunque homines, sed 416Aprincipes, et horum sublimiores, quos appellavit reges. Ideo in hymnis canitur Deo apud hunc puteum, et scriptum est quoniam hic est puteus super quo locutus est Dominus Moysi: Congrega populum et dabo ei aquam de puteo.

Congrega populum et dabo. Lex convocat te ad puteum, Christum, scilicet Dei Filium, propria quidem substantia subsistentem, uno tamen cum Patre et Spiritu Deitatis fonte nominatum. Ad hunc ergo puteum, id est ad Christi fidem convocat te lex. Inde ipse dicit: quia Moyses de me scripsit (Joan. IV). Vocat autem ut bibamus aquas, et cantemus ei canticum, id est, corde credamus ad justitiam et ore confiteamur ad salutem (Rom. X).

VERS. 17.-- Ascendat puteus. (ORIG.) Initiate illi 416Bputeum, id est initium omnium ponite puteum, quia ipse est principium et primogenitus omnis creaturae (Apoc. III).

Vel sic, ut verba dirigantur ex persona Moysi ad populum: Date initium cordi vestro, ut incipiat intelligere quid sit puteus, de quo spirituales aquae hauriendae sunt, et reficiendus est populus credentium. Initiate ergo ei, id est Israeli, hunc puteum, ut sensum mysticum si quis ex corde videt, Deum de profundis possit haurire. Ad hunc puteum Moyses, id est lex congregat, ad quem videtur aliquis pervenire, sed nisi per Moysen congregetur non est Deo acceptus. Marcion videtur sibi venisse, et Basilides, et Valentinus: sed quia non venerunt per Moysen, nec acceperunt legem et prophetas, non possunt laudare 416CDeum de fontibus Israel. Non veniunt ergo ad puteum tales, quem foderunt principes (Gen. XIV) et excuderunt reges. Est autem vallis salsa in qua sunt putei bituminis: omnis autem haeresis et omne peccatum in valle est, et in valle salsa. Peccatum enim non ascendit sursum, sed semper ad ima et inferiora descendit. Est ergo in valle positus, salsus et amarus omnis haereticus sensus, et omnis peccati actus. Quid enim dulce, quid suave potest habere peccatum? Si autem veneris ad haereticam sententiam et amaritudinem peccati, venisti ad puteos bituminis, quod est esca et nutrimentum ignis Si gustaveris aquam de his puteis, si haereticum sensum et peccati amaritudinem receperis, fomenta ignis et gehennae 416Dincendia in te praeparabis. Talibus dicitur: Incedite in lumine ignis vestri, etc. (Isa. L.)

VERS. 16.-- Foderunt illum principes et excuderunt reges. Principes ergo foderunt, reges excuderunt, quod ab excudendo, non excidendo dictum est. Excudi enim dicitur, quod in saxo caeditur vel formatur. Principes ergo tanquam inferiores foderunt, id est quasi in terrae mollitiem altitudinem quamdam demerserunt: hi vero quos reges appellat, quasi fortiores, non solum terrae profundum, sed saxi duritiam penetrant, ut perveniant ad profundiores aquas, et ipsas abyssi venas perscrutentur, scientes judicia Dei esse abyssum multam. Hi sunt apostoli, quorum aliquis dicit: Nobis autem revelavit Deus per Spiritum suum; Spiritus enim omnia scrutatur etiam alta Dei 417A(I Cor. II). Qui ergo per Spiritum sanctum possunt alta scrutari et penetrare profunda mysteria putei, reges esse dicuntur, qui puteum in petra excuderunt, quia dura et difficilia scientiae penetrarunt secreta. Foderunt ergo putenm istum principes, et excuderunt reges gentium, id est, Apostoli, qui gentes congregaverunt ad obediendum fidei, et omnibus patefecerunt scientiam Christi, in quo sunt omnes thesauri sapientiae et scientiae absconditi. Impleverunt enim mandatum Dei, docentes omnes gentes, et baptizantes eos in nomine Patris et Filii et Spiritus sancti (Matth. XXVIII). Et per hoc excuderunt, id est, patefecerunt scientiam putei. Haec quoque quae in manibus habemus, nobis puteus est, et omnis Scriptura legis et prophetarum: evangelica quoque et 417Bapostolica simul unus puteus est, quem non possunt fodere vel excudere, nisi reges et principes, qui scilicet possunt auferre terram et amovere superficiem litterae, et de inferiori petra, ubi Christus est, spiritales sensus velut aquam vivam proferre. Reges dicuntur, quia peccati regnum expulerunt, et justitiae regnum in membris suis posuerunt. Decet enim eum docere caeteros, qui prius fecerit quae docet, unde: Qui fecerit et docuerit sic homines, magnus vocabitur in regno coelorum (Matth. V); magnum autem in regno esse, est regem esse.

VERS. 18.-- Principes. Possunt iidem vocari principes et reges. Vel principes prophetas intelligamus: ipsi enim sensum et prophetiam de Christo defossam in profunditate litterae collocaverunt, ideo de illis 417Cdicitur: Nisi occulte audieritis, plorabit anima vestra. Et alibi: Quam magna multitudo dulcedinis tuae, Domine, quam abscondisti timentibus te (Psal. XXX). Reges sunt apostoli qui perscrutantur alta Dei et Ecclesias regunt, unde et Christus dicitur Rex regum.

Et paraverunt duces. (ORIG.) Excuderunt, inquit, etc., usque ad et verbi Dei arcana perscrutantes.

Profectique sunt. (ORIG.) Profecti a puteo, etc., usque ad ut affectus noster et fides probetur.

Qui de hoc puteo bibit, ad hoc proficit ut habeat munera quae Deo offerat, de quibus in lege dicitur: Munera mea data mea. (Levit. II). Ex his ergo quae Deus dedit, offertur Deo; dedit autem agnitionem sui, et homo offert ei fidem suam et affectum.

417DCum obtulerimus quae ex nobis sunt, consequimur quae ex Deo sunt. Cum enim nostram fidem et affectum obtulerimus, tunc ipse largitur diversa dona Spiritus sancti, de quo dicitur: Omnia autem ex Deo sunt (Rom. XI).

VERS. 20.-- In Bamoth. Bamoth adventus mortis, qua scilicet commorimur, ut convivamus ei, et mortificamus membra nostra super terram. Consepulti enim sumus illi per baptismum in morte (Rom. VI). Qui enim ordinem salutaris viae tenet, debet per haec singulariter agere et post multa ad hunc locum venire.

(ORIG.) Et ex Bamoth, inquit, etc., usque ad imo profectibus iter animae paratur ad coelum.

418AVERS. 21.-- Misit autem. (ID.) Mittit Israel legatos ad regem Amorrhaeorum, etc., usque ad congregat sibi lacus confractos vel contritos.

Ad Sehon regem. Sehon, qui interpretatur arbor infructuosa, vel elatus, hic est rex Amorrhaeorum, qui interpretantur in amaritudinem adducentes vel loquentes. Hic est diabolus elatus et infructuosus, de quo dicitur: Venit princeps mundi hujus, et in me non habet quidquam (Joan. XIV). Et alibi: Ecce princeps hujus mundi mittetur foras (Joan. XII), non quia creavit mundum, sed quia in mundo peccatores sunt, et ipse peccati princeps est, secundum quod dicitur: Quia omnis mundus in maligno positus es (Joan. V), id est, ipse mundi princeps est.

VERS. 22.-- Via regia. Quae dicit: Ego sum via, 418Bveritas et vita (Joan. XIV). De quo dicitur: Deus, judicium tuum regi da (Psal. LXXI). Hac incedendum est, et nusquam declinandum, nec in agrum nec in vineam, id est nec ad opera, nec ad sensus diabolicos, si volumus pacifice transire per mundum. Sed hoc magis incitat principem mundi quod dicimus nos nolle manere cum eo, vel morari, vel aliquid ejus contingere: inde irascitur et movet persecutiones et pericula, et cruciatus intentat. Inde dicit: Congregavit Sehon populum suum, etc.

VERS. 23.-- Exercitu: principibus scilicet et judicibus mundi et omnibus nequitiae ministris, qui semper impugnant populum Dei.

In Jasa. In Jasa, Hebraice iahtsah quod interpretatur Mandati impletio. Si enim veniamus ad 418Cimpletionem mandatorum, superamus superbum et elatum: mandatum enim complere, est diabolum et exercitum ejus superare. Nihil enim nobis poterit nocere, si veniamus in Jasa, id est si praecepta Domini nostri Jesu Christi servemus.

VERS. 24.-- In ore gladii. Vivus est sermo Dei, efficax, et penetrabilior omni gladio, etc. (Heb. IV). De quo dicit Apostolus: Et gladium Spiritus quod est verbum Dei (Eph. V). In hujus ore cadit Sehon, id est diabolus.

Terra ejus. Omnis terrena regio terra Sehon dicitur; sed Christus et Ecclesia ejus in omni terra Sehon dominatur.

(RAB.) Jeboc civitas finisque regni Sehon, etc., usque ad vocatum est nomen ejus Israel.

418DVERS. 25.-- Tulit ergo. (AUG., quaest. 44 in Num.) Hic possedit Israel civitates Amorrhaeorum, etc., usque ad sed declinaverunt ab eis.

Israel. (ORIG., hom. 13 in Num.) Qui in Christo Israel est, etc., usque ad et vult ut accendatur.

(ISID.) In tricesima nona mansione, etc., usque ad et intellecta fortiter superatur.

VERS. 26.-- Sehon regis. Sehon, id est loquentis: qui enim sub diabolo agunt, tantum loqui norunt, sed inania loquuntur, ut pote astrologi aliqui et philosophi; fidelium autem regnum, quod a Deo est, non in sermone, sed in virtute Dei (I Cor. IV).

Moab. (RAB.) Moab qui interpretatur, etc., usque ad corruunt et consumuntur.

419AVERS. 27.-- Idcirco dicitur. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Alia littera, etc., usque ad non Amorrhaeorum, sed filiorum Israel.

VERS. 29.-- Vae tibi, Moab. (RAB.) Chamos interpretatur, etc., usque ad contra quas dicitur: Ne tradas bestiis animas confitentes tibi (Psal. LXXIII).

VERS. 32.-- Jazer, id est fortitudinem eorum. Fortissima scilicet haereticorum dogmata dialectica arte constructa. Et possederunt. Ab errore convertendo et praecepta Domini docendo, ut de eorum. correctione et profectu coelestem sibi patriam acquirerent.

VERS. 33.-- Og rex Basan. (ORIG.) Ad Og regem Basan, etc., usque ad in nullo enim potest honestum esse quod turpe est.

CHAPTER XXI.

VERS. 1.-- When he had heard this. The Canaanite heard that Israel had come into the place of the spies, where the people had once given offense; he joined battle, and 415Aled Israel away captive. And again in the same place there is fighting: by reason of a vow the victor is vanquished, and the vanquished prevail. In which it is understood that when enemies have set upon us, destitute of God's help, and have taken us captive, we should not despair: for it can come about that we conquer where we were conquered.

VERS. 3.-- Anathema. (AUG., quaest. 40 in Num.) Another reading, etc., as far as it is the same as "I devote to destruction, I abhor."

VERS. 4.-- They set forward, etc. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam. ) The thirty-fifth station, etc., as far as but with the mouth confession unto salvation (Rom. X).

VERS. 8.-- Make a serpent (ISID. in Num., tom. V.) Those bitten by the serpents were healed when the brazen serpent was lifted up and looked upon, etc., as far as he lives through his Divinity as though of brass.

415BVERS. 10.-- And the children of Israel setting forward encamped, etc. The thirty-seventh station is in Oboth, which is rendered "magicians" or "pythons." Because after the image of God, which is shown forth in the reason of the heart, and after the confession of faith, which is uttered with the mouth, there rise up serpents and maleficent arts; but let us, keeping our heart with all watchfulness, stop our ears, that we may not hear the voices of those who enchant and the songs of the sirens.

VERS. 11.-- Whence going forth. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) Into Jeabarim, etc., as far as but this belongs properly to those who look upon the rising of the Sun of justice.

VERS. 12.-- They came to the torrent Zareth, etc. (RAB.) The thirty-ninth station was in Dibongad, etc., as far as but let us fear the wilderness set before us.

415CVERS. 14.-- In the book of the wars. (AUG., quaest. 42.) He did not say in what book it is written, etc., as far as a war of the Lord should be written in any book of theirs.

VERS. 16.-- From that place, etc. (ORIG., hom. 12 in Num.) What is it that the Lord so greatly commands, etc., as far as because the substance and the nature of the Trinity is one.

(ID., ibid.) Those wells too can be understood, etc., as far as and he removed the earth which the Philistines had thrown in.

But it is to be noted that, while Abraham was living, the Philistines did not dare to fill up the wells or to throw earth into them; but when he was dead they grew strong and lay in wait for his wells, yet they are repaired by Isaac. Abraham's servant too found Rebecca at the wells, who is interpreted Patience: she is made the wife of Isaac, but at 415Dthe wells (Gen. XXIV, XXIX); likewise Jacob, when he came to Mesopotamia having his father's command not to take a wife of another race, found Rachel at the wells; and Moses found Sephora (Exod. II). If therefore thou hast understood what and of what sort the wives of the saints are, if thou wilt take Patience to wife, or Wisdom and the other virtues of the soul, and say what was said of wisdom: Her have I sought to take to me for a wife (Sap. VIII), be assiduous about these wells, and there thou wilt find a spouse of this kind, because it is certain that the virtues dwell at the streams of the living word. There are therefore many wells within the soul, and very many others in the several sayings and senses of the Scriptures: yet there is here a more eminent one which they digged, not just any men, but 416Athe princes, and of these the loftier sort, whom he called kings. Therefore hymns are sung to God at this well, and it is written that this is the well concerning which the Lord spoke to Moses: Gather the people together, and I will give them water out of the well.

Gather the people together, and I will give. The law calls thee to the well, that is, to Christ, the Son of God, subsisting indeed in his own proper substance, yet named together with the Father and the Spirit from one fount of Deity. To this well, then, that is, to the faith of Christ, the law calls thee. Whence he himself says that Moses wrote of me (Joan. IV). And he calls us to drink the waters, and to sing him a song, that is, that we may believe with the heart unto justice and confess with the mouth unto salvation (Rom. X).

VERS. 17.-- Let the well ascend. (ORIG.) Begin for him the 416Bwell, that is, set the well as the beginning of all things, because he himself is the beginning and the firstborn of every creature (Apoc. III).

Or thus, so that the words are directed from the person of Moses to the people: Give a beginning to your heart, that it may begin to understand what the well is, from which spiritual waters are to be drawn and the people of believers refreshed. Begin therefore for him, that is, for Israel, this well, so that if anyone sees the mystical sense from the heart, he may be able to draw God out of the depths. To this well Moses, that is, the law, gathers men, to which someone may seem to come, but unless he be gathered through Moses he is not acceptable to God. Marcion seems to himself to have come, and Basilides, and Valentinus: but because they did not come through Moses, nor did they receive the law and the prophets, they cannot praise 416CGod from the fountains of Israel. Such men therefore do not come to the well, which the princes digged (Gen. XIV) and the kings hammered out. But there is a salt valley in which are wells of bitumen: and every heresy and every sin is in a valley, and in a salt valley. For sin does not ascend upward, but always goes down to the lowest and lower places. Every heretical thought therefore, and every act of sin, is set in a valley, salt and bitter. For what sweetness, what pleasantness can sin have? But if thou comest to a heretical opinion and to the bitterness of sin, thou hast come to the wells of bitumen, which is food and nourishment for fire If thou tastest water from these wells, if thou receivest the heretical sense and the bitterness of sin, thou wilt prepare within thyself fuel for the fire and the burnings of 416Dgehenna. To such it is said: Walk in the light of your fire, etc. (Isa. L.)

VERS. 16.-- The princes digged it and the kings hammered it out. The princes therefore digged, the kings hammered out — which is said from hammering out, not from cutting away. For that is said to be hammered out which is cut or shaped in stone. The princes therefore, as the lesser, digged, that is, sank a certain depth as it were into the softness of the earth: but those whom he calls kings, as the stronger, penetrate not only the depth of the earth but the hardness of the rock, that they may reach the deeper waters and search out the very veins of the abyss, knowing that the judgments of God are a great deep. These are the apostles, one of whom says: But God hath revealed to us by his Spirit; for the Spirit searcheth all things, yea, the deep things of God 417A(I Cor. II). Those therefore who through the Holy Spirit can search out the deep things and penetrate the profound mysteries of the well are said to be kings, who hammered out the well in the rock, because they penetrated the hard and difficult secrets of knowledge. The princes therefore digged this well, and the kings of the nations hammered it out, that is, the Apostles, who gathered the nations to the obedience of faith, and laid open to all the knowledge of Christ, in whom are hidden all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge. For they fulfilled the commandment of God, teaching all nations, and baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Ghost (Matth. XXVIII). And by this they hammered out, that is, laid open, the knowledge of the well. These things too which we have in our hands are a well to us, and all the Scripture of the law and of the prophets: the evangelical also and 417Bthe apostolic together are one well, which none can dig or hammer out but kings and princes, those, namely, who can take away the earth and remove the surface of the letter, and bring forth from the lower rock, where Christ is, the spiritual senses as living water. They are called kings because they have driven out the reign of sin and have set the reign of justice in their own members. For it becomes him to teach others who has first done what he teaches; whence: He that shall do and shall so teach men, he shall be called great in the kingdom of heaven (Matth. V); and to be great in the kingdom is to be a king.

VERS. 18.-- Princes. The same men can be called both princes and kings. Or let us understand by princes the prophets: for they placed the sense and the prophecy concerning Christ buried deep in the profundity of the letter, and therefore it is said of them: 417CUnless you hear it in secret, your soul shall weep. And elsewhere: How great is the multitude of thy sweetness, O Lord, which thou hast hidden for them that fear thee (Psal. XXX). The kings are the apostles, who search out the deep things of God and rule the Churches, whence Christ also is called King of kings.

And the leaders prepared it. (ORIG.) They hammered out, he says, etc., as far as and searching out the secrets of the word of God.

And they set forward. (ORIG.) Setting forward from the well, etc., as far as that our affection and our faith may be proved.

He who drinks of this well profits thereby, that he may have gifts to offer to God, of which it is said in the law: My gifts, my givings. (Levit. II). Of those things therefore which God has given, offering is made to God; and he has given the knowledge of himself, and man offers him his faith and his affection.

417DWhen we have offered the things that are of ourselves, we obtain the things that are of God. For when we have offered our faith and our affection, then he himself bestows the diverse gifts of the Holy Spirit, of which it is said: But all things are of God (Rom. XI).

VERS. 20.-- In Bamoth. Bamoth is the coming of death, by which, namely, we die together with him, that we may live together with him, and mortify our members upon the earth. For we are buried together with him by baptism unto death (Rom. VI). For he who keeps the order of the way of salvation ought to go through these things one by one, and after many things to come to this place.

(ORIG.) And from Bamoth, he says, etc., as far as nay rather, by advances is the soul's journey to heaven made ready.

418AVERS. 21.-- And he sent. (ID.) Israel sends legates to the king of the Amorrhites, etc., as far as he gathers to himself cisterns broken or crushed.

To Sehon the king. Sehon, who is interpreted a barren tree, or one lifted up, is the king of the Amorrhites, who are interpreted those who bring into bitterness, or those who speak. This is the devil, lifted up and barren, of whom it is said: The prince of this world cometh, and in me he hath not anything (Joan. XIV). And elsewhere: Behold, the prince of this world shall be sent forth (Joan. XII), not because he created the world, but because there are sinners in the world, and he himself is the prince of sin, according to what is said: For the whole world is set in wickedness (Joan. V), that is, he himself is the prince of the world.

VERS. 22.-- The king's highway. Which says: I am the way, 418Bthe truth and the life (Joan. XIV). Of whom it is said: Give to the king thy judgment, O God (Psal. LXXI). In this we must walk, and turn aside nowhere, neither into the field nor into the vineyard, that is, neither to diabolical works nor to diabolical thoughts, if we would pass peaceably through the world. But this the more provokes the prince of the world, that we say we will not remain with him, nor tarry, nor touch anything of his: hence he grows angry and stirs up persecutions and perils, and threatens torments. Hence he says: Sehon gathered together his people, etc.

VERS. 23.-- The army: namely the princes and judges of the world and all the ministers of wickedness, who always assail the people of God.

In Jasa. In Jasa, in Hebrew iahtsah, which is interpreted the fulfilling of the commandment. For if we come to the 418Cfulfilling of the commandments, we overcome the proud and lifted-up one: for to fulfil a commandment is to overcome the devil and his army. For nothing will be able to hurt us, if we come into Jasa, that is, if we keep the precepts of our Lord Jesus Christ.

VERS. 24.-- In the edge of the sword. The word of God is living, effectual, and more piercing than any sword, etc. (Heb. IV). Of which the Apostle says: And the sword of the Spirit, which is the word of God (Eph. V). In the edge of this Sehon falls, that is, the devil.

His land. Every earthly region is called the land of Sehon; but Christ and his Church bear rule in every land of Sehon.

(RAB.) Jeboc, a city and the boundary of Sehon's kingdom, etc., as far as his name was called Israel.

418DVERS. 25.-- He took therefore. (AUG., quaest. 44 in Num.) Here Israel took possession of the cities of the Amorrhites, etc., as far as but they turned aside from them.

Israel. (ORIG., hom. 13 in Num.) He who is Israel in Christ, etc., as far as and he wills that it be kindled.

(ISID.) In the thirty-ninth station, etc., as far as and once it is understood it is bravely overcome.

VERS. 26.-- Of Sehon the king. Sehon, that is, of one speaking: for those who act under the devil know only how to speak, but they speak empty things, as for instance certain astrologers and philosophers; but the kingdom of the faithful, which is from God, is not in speech, but in the power of God (I Cor. IV).

Moab. (RAB.) Moab, who is interpreted, etc., as far as they fall down and are consumed.

419AVERS. 27.-- Therefore it is said. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Another reading, etc., as far as not of the Amorrhites, but of the children of Israel.

VERS. 29.-- Woe to thee, Moab. (RAB.) Chamos is interpreted, etc., as far as against whom it is said: Deliver not up to beasts the souls that confess to thee (Psal. LXXIII).

VERS. 32.-- Jazer, that is, their strength. Namely the strongest dogmas of the heretics, framed by the art of dialectic. And they possessed it. By turning them from error and by teaching the precepts of the Lord, that from their. correction and progress they might gain for themselves the heavenly fatherland.

VERS. 33.-- Og the king of Basan. (ORIG.) To Og the king of Basan, etc., as far as for nothing can be honorable which is base.

CAPUT XXII.

  • 419B

VERS. 2.-- Videns autem. (AUG., quaest. 46.) Quod dicit Scriptura, etc., non vero una gens erat, sed duae vicinae.

VERS. 5.-- Misit ergo nuntios ad Balaam filium Beor. (ORIG., Hom. 13.) Bellum tibi imminet, rex Balac, etc., usque ad mittit ergo rex ad eum dicens: Veni nunc, et maledic mihi populum hunc, etc.

VERS. 6.-- Si quo modo possim. Videtur non ex integro confidere in Balaam fama virtutum quae in populo Dei factae sunt perterritus.

Novi enim quod benedictus sit. (ORIG.) Non credo eum scire quod quibuscunque benedixisset Balaam, benedicti essent, sed adulandi gratia haec dicere, ut 419Cartem ejus extollens promptiorem sibi faceret. Ars enim magica nescit benedicere, nec daemones scit benedicere Isaac, et Jacob, et omnes sancti: impiorum nullus benedicere novit.

VERS. 7.-- Divinationis pretium. Alii: Divinacula in artibus, quas curiositas humana composuit. Sunt quaedam quae Scriptura divinacula nominavit. Gentilis autem consuetudo vel tripodas vel cortinas, vel hujusmodi vocabulis appellat, quae quasi ad hoc consecrata, moveri ab eis et contrectari solent. Sed divina Scriptura Ephod vernaculo sermone nominat in prophetis, quod tradunt esse indumentum prophetantium (I Reg. XXX). Aliud tamen in divina Scriptura prophetia, aliud divinatio. Ait enim: Non erit auguratio in Jacob, neque divinatio in Israel. 419DAbdicatur ergo penitus divinatio, opere enim et ministerio daemonum impletur. Igitur Balaam acceptis divinaculis, cum solerent ad se daemones venire, fugatos videt et Deum adesse: quem ideo dicit se interrogare, quia consuetos sibi apparere non videt. Venit ergo Deus ad Balaam, non quia sit dignus, sed ut fugentur illi qui ad maledicendum et malefaciendum adesse consueverant: hinc enim providebat Deus populo suo.

VERS. 8.-- Venit Deus, etc. Non dicitur utrum in somnis hoc factum sit, quamvis per noctem factum esse constet. Postea enim dicit Scriptura: Exsurgens Balaam mane, etc.

Nemo glorietur, si sibi Deus loquitur. Novit enim 420Aquomodo loquendum sit cum talibus, cum loquatur etiam cum reprobis: qui et cum per angelos loquitur, ipse loquitur.
