(ORIG., Hom. 13.) Venit Deus, et ait ad eum, etc. Potest objici: Licet invocet Balaam daemones, etc., usque ad et ad magnae fidei argumentum parvum puerum quasi magnum regem venerati sunt.

VERS. 16.-- Sic dicit Balac filius Sephor, etc. Balac exclusio vel devoratio: in quo significatur mundi hujus aliqua contraria potestas, quae excludere et devorare cupit Israelem spiritualem, nec utitur ministris nisi pontificibus et scribis et Pharisaeis. Ipsos invitat, mercedem promittit; illi vero sicut Balaam cuncta simulant se ad Dominum referre, et zelo Dei agere. Dicunt enim: Scrutare 420BScripturas et vide quia propheta a Galilaea non surgit. Et: Nos legem habemus, et secundum legem debet mori, quia Filium Dei se fecit (Joan. VII, XIX).

VERS. 18.-- Si dederit mihi Balac, etc. (AUG., quaest. 48.) Quod ait Balam iterum ad se missis honoratioribus nuntiis, etc., usque ad unde Secuti viam Balaam filii Beor, qui mercedem iniquitatis dilexit.

VERS. 20.-- Venit ergo Deus. Molestus est Deo Balaam, et prope extorquet permitti sibi, ut eat maledicere filiis Israel, et invocet daemones ad quem jam venerat Deus.

(ORIG., Hom. 13.) Difficile est in explanatione historiae, etc., usque ad quia apud Deum otiosa non sunt nec malorum nec bonorum opera.

Si vocare. (RAB.) Balaam divinus erat, etc., usque 420Cad non tam corde et sensu, quam ore et sermone.

(ORIG.) Si videas contrariam potestatem, etc., usque ad scilicet ex gentibus credentes.

VERS. 22.-- Stetitque angelus Domini. Qui aderat scilicet filiis Israel, de quo scriptum est: Angelus meus ibit tecum (Exod. XXXII).

VERS. 23.-- Cernens asina angelum stantem. Magus daemones videt, asina angelum non quod sit digna videre, sicut nec loqui, sed ut confutetur Balaam, unde mutum animal arguit prophetae dementiam.

(GREG., Past., part. III, adm. 13.) Balaam pervenire ad propositum tendit, etc., usque ad quia ad maledicendum pergens vocem, non mentem, mutavit.

420DVERS. 25.-- Parieti. Id est, maceriae illius vineae, in qua non erat angelus, quoniam ab alia parte erat in sulcis vinearum, juxta Septuaginta.

VERS. 26.-- Ad locum. Non jam in sulcis vinearum, sed inter ipsas macerias, id est in via obstitit, ut non posset ad dexteram neque ad sinistram ire.

VERS. 27.-- Concidit sub, etc. Quia caesa non ivit retro, in partem non declinavit, quia non ab altera parte terrebatur, sed in media via in angusto erat angelus.

VERS. 28.-- Aperuitque. Ut arguatur per eam Balaam, et mutae pecudis vocibus confutetur, qui divinus videbatur et sapiens.

Et locuta est. (ISID). Asina cui Balaam sedet, angelum 421Avidet, et loquitur; id est bruta Gentilitas, quam quondam Balaam, id est, seductor idololatriae, quasi brutum animal et nulla ratione renitens, quo voluit errore substravit; sed ista angelum vidit quem homo videre non potuit, et vidit, et detulit, et locuta est; ut agnosceremus sub adventu magni angeli, illam gentilem plebem mutata duritie stoliditatisque natura solutis Deo linguis locuturam.

(ORIG. hom. 14). Asina cui Balaam sedebat, etc., usque ad major autem his charitas, quae sola nunquam cadit.

VERS. 29.-- Respondit. Nimirum tanta cupiditate plenus ferebatur, ut nec tanti monstri miraculo terreretur, et responderit quasi ad hominem loquens; cum Deus non asinae animam, in rationalem 421Bnaturam vertisset; sed quod illi placuerat ex illa sonare fecisset ad illius vesaniam cohibendam; illud forte praefigurans: Quia stulta mundi elegit Deus, ut confunderet sapientes, pro spirituali et vero Israel.

VERS. 38.-- Ecce adsum: Nunquid aliud loqui potero nisi quod Deus, etc. (ORIG., hom. 15.) Balaam in Scripturis nunc vituperabilis, nunc laudabilis ponitur, etc., usque ad Balaam, illuminatus ab illo Lucifero de quo dicitur: Et quomodo cecidit Lucifer qui mane oriebatur? (Isa. XIV.)

CHAPTER XXII.

  • 419B

VERS. 2.-- And seeing. (AUG., quaest. 46.) What Scripture says, etc., yet they were not one nation, but two neighboring ones.

VERS. 5.-- He sent therefore messengers to Balaam the son of Beor. (ORIG., Hom. 13.) War threatens thee, king Balac, etc., as far as the king therefore sends to him, saying: Come now, and curse me this people, etc.

VERS. 6.-- If by any means I may be able. He seems not to trust wholly in Balaam, being terrified by the report of the mighty works which had been done among the people of God.

For I know that he is blessed. (ORIG.) I do not believe that he knew that whomsoever Balaam had blessed would be blessed, but that he said these things for flattery's sake, that by 419Cextolling his art he might make him readier for himself. For the magic art knows not how to bless, nor does it know how to bless demons, and Isaac, and Jacob, and all the saints: of the impious none has known how to bless.

VERS. 7.-- The price of divination. Others: Divining instruments among the arts, which human curiosity has put together. There are certain things which Scripture has named divining instruments. But Gentile custom calls them either tripods or curtains, or by words of this kind, which, as though consecrated to this end, are wont to be moved and handled by them. But divine Scripture in the prophets names the Ephod in the vernacular speech, which they hand down to be the garment of those who prophesy (I Reg. XXX). Yet in divine Scripture prophecy is one thing, divination another. For he says: There shall be no auguring in Jacob, neither divination in Israel. [Vulg. Non est augurium in Jacob, nec divinatio in Israel, "There is no augury in Jacob, nor divination in Israel"] 419DDivination therefore is utterly renounced, for it is accomplished by the work and ministry of demons. Therefore Balaam, having received his divining instruments, when the demons were wont to come to him, sees them put to flight and God present: whom for that reason he says he consults, because he does not see those who were accustomed to appear to him. God came therefore to Balaam, not because he is worthy, but that those might be put to flight who had been wont to be present for cursing and for doing harm: for hereby God was providing for his people.

VERS. 8.-- God came, etc. It is not said whether this was done in sleep, although it is certain that it was done by night. For afterward Scripture says: Balaam rising in the morning, etc.

Let no man glory if God speaks to him. For he knows 420Ahow he must speak with such men, since he speaks even with the reprobate: and he who speaks through angels, himself speaks.

(ORIG., Hom. 13.) God came, and said to him, etc. It can be objected: Granted that Balaam calls upon demons, etc., as far as and for a proof of great faith they venerated a little child as though a great king.

VERS. 16.-- Thus saith Balac the son of Sephor, etc. Balac is a shutting out or a devouring: in which is signified some power of this world set against us, which desires to shut out and devour the spiritual Israel, and uses no ministers but the chief priests and scribes and Pharisees. These it invites, to these it promises a reward; but they, like Balaam, feign in all things to refer themselves to the Lord, and to act with zeal for God. For they say: Search 420Bthe Scriptures and see that a prophet riseth not out of Galilee. And: We have a law, and according to the law he ought to die, because he made himself the Son of God (Joan. VII, XIX).

VERS. 18.-- If Balac would give me, etc. (AUG., quaest. 48.) That Balam said, when more honorable messengers had been sent to him a second time, etc., as far as whence They have followed the way of Balaam the son of Beor, who loved the wages of iniquity. [Vulg. viam Balaam ex Bosor, qui mercedem iniquitatis amavit]

VERS. 20.-- God came therefore. Balaam is troublesome to God, and all but extorts leave for himself to go and curse the children of Israel, and to call upon demons — he to whom God had already come.

(ORIG., Hom. 13.) It is difficult in the exposition of the history, etc., as far as because with God neither the works of the wicked nor those of the good are idle.

If they have come to call thee. (RAB.) Balaam was a diviner, etc., as far 420Cas not so much in heart and understanding as in mouth and speech.

(ORIG.) If thou shouldst see a power set against thee, etc., as far as namely those of the Gentiles who believe.

VERS. 22.-- And the angel of the Lord stood. He, namely, who was present with the children of Israel, of whom it is written: My angel shall go with thee (Exod. XXXII).

VERS. 23.-- The ass seeing the angel standing. The magician sees demons, the ass an angel, not because she was worthy to see, any more than to speak, but that Balaam might be confuted; whence a dumb beast reproves the prophet's madness.

(GREG., Past., part. III, adm. 13.) Balaam presses on to reach his purpose, etc., as far as because, going on to curse, he changed his voice, not his mind.

420DVERS. 25.-- To the wall. That is, to the fence of that vineyard in which the angel was not, since he was on the other side, in the furrows of the vineyards, according to the Septuagint.

VERS. 26.-- To the place. No longer in the furrows of the vineyards, but between the fences themselves, that is, he stood in the way, so that she could go neither to the right hand nor to the left.

VERS. 27.-- She fell down under, etc. Because, being beaten, she did not go back, nor turn aside to one side, because she was not frightened from the other side, but the angel was in the middle of the way, in a narrow place.

VERS. 28.-- And he opened. That through her Balaam might be reproved, and confuted by the voices of a dumb beast, who seemed to be a diviner and wise.

And she spoke. (ISID). The ass on which Balaam sits sees the angel 421Aand speaks; that is, the brute Gentile world, which of old Balaam — that is, the seducer of idolatry — laid low with what error he would, as a brute animal resisting with no reason; but she saw the angel whom the man could not see, and she saw, and reported, and spoke; that we might know that at the coming of the great angel that Gentile people, its hardness and the nature of its stupidity being changed, would speak with tongues loosed unto God.

(ORIG. hom. 14). The ass on which Balaam was sitting, etc., as far as but the greater of these is charity, which alone never falls.

VERS. 29.-- He answered. Doubtless he was carried along so full of covetousness that he was not even terrified by the miracle of so great a portent, and answered as though speaking to a man; whereas God had not turned the soul of the ass into a rational 421Bnature, but had made that which pleased him to sound forth from her, to restrain that man's frenzy; perhaps prefiguring this: That God hath chosen the foolish things of the world, that he might confound the wise, for the spiritual and true Israel.

VERS. 38.-- Behold I am here: shall I be able to speak any other thing than what God, etc. (ORIG., hom. 15.) Balaam is set down in the Scriptures now as blameworthy, now as praiseworthy, etc., as far as Balaam, enlightened by that Lucifer of whom it is said: And how did Lucifer fall, who rose in the morning? (Isa. XIV.) [Vulg. Quomodo cecidisti de caelo, Lucifer, qui mane oriebaris, "How art thou fallen from heaven, O Lucifer, who didst rise in the morning"]

CAPUT XXIII.

VERS. 7.-- Veni, inquit, et maledic mihi Jacob. (ORIG. hom. 15.) In Latinis sermonibus, etc., usque ad ideoque dicit verbum Dei positum in ore ejus.

421CVERS. 8.-- Quomodo maledicam. (ID.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad et pronuntiandi auctoritas perimatur.

Cui non maledixerit Deus. Qui tantum meritum designat ejus cui maledicitur, et sententiam promit, quem non qualitas peccati, nec fallit affectus peccantis: homo autem neutrum novit, et saepe conviciis aut injuriis provocatus maledicit. Ideo dicit Apostolus: Benedicite et nolite maledicere (Rom. XII), ut conviciandi vitium resecetur.

VERS. 9.-- De summis, etc. Quasi, ideo non debet maledici, quia alta et coelestis ejus vita. Nemo intelligit eam, nisi ad eminentem scientiam ascenderit.

(ORIG.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad consurgentes 421Dcum Christo exercuerint super terram.

Vide, etc. Actus Jacob, id est activorum, videntur: contemplatio Israel, id est contemplativorum, tantum intelligitur. Vel, in futura resurrectione Jacob, videbitur, id est corpus Israel intelligitur, id est, resurgentium anima et spiritus.

Populus solus. Quia non est permistus caeteris hominibus, nec inter caeteras gentes reputatus, certis nobilitatus privilegiis, observationibus et legitimis suis, sicut tribus Levi non est permista caeteris.

Populus solus habitabit, etc. (ORIG.) Spiritualis Jacob et Israel, etc., usque ad inter gentes ultra reputabitur.

Quis numerare. (ID.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad et 422Apatitur illa quae in secundo libro Regum sunt scripta.

VERS. 10.-- Moriatur anima mea. (ID.) De se prophetare videtur: sed secundum litteram nec in Balaam, nec in illo Israel factum est, nec fieri potuit, nec enim intra ipsos, sed ab ipsis mortuus est.

(ID.) Moriatur anima mea morte justorum, etc., usque ad: sed fides quae per dilectionem operatur (Gal. V).

(GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 27.) Moriatur anima mea morte justorum, et fiant novissima mea horum, etc. Multi intra Ecclesiam, etc., usque ad unde, Vultus ejus non sunt amplius in diversa mutati (I Reg. I).

Et fiant novissima mea horum, etc. (ORIG.) Hoc in 422BMagis, qui de Oriente primi venerunt adorare Christum (Matth. II), potest intelligi, qui de genere Balaam erant per generis successionem, et per disciplinae traditionem. Constat enim eos agnovisse stellam quam praedixerat Balaam in Israel orituram

VERS. 11.-- Quid est hoc? Balac contra spem benedictiones pro maledictionibus audiens, et ultra non ferens, prophetantis verba interrupit.

(ID.) Non vult amarus rex benedictionem, sed maledicta quaerit: est enim de cognatione illius cui dicitur: Maledictus tu ab omnibus bestiis terrae (Gen. III).

Dixit ergo Balac. Putat eum perterritum Israelis multitudine, et ideo non ausum maledicere, et mutationem loci sibi prodesse.

422CVERS. 13.-- Videre non possis. Demens putavit Israeliticam gratiam loci abjectione celari, nesciens quia non potest abscondi civitas supra montem posita.

VERS. 14.-- Aedificavit. Res profanis sacrificiis agebatur, et divinatio arte magica quaerebatur. Volens tamen Deus abundare gratiam ubi abundavit delictum (Rom. V), adesse dignabatur, non sacrificiis, sed in occursum venienti. Et ibi dat verbum suum et mysteria, et futura pronuntiat, ubi maxime fides et admiratio Gentilium pendet: ut qui nostris nolunt credere prophetis, credant divinis suis.

VERS. 15.-- Sta hic juxta. Ad holocaustum suum stabat in idololatria sua positus, ideo magis cadebat: surgere ergo videtur, qui magis ceciderat, cum idololatria 422Dstare videretur.

Non est Deus quasi homo, ut mentiatur. (ORIG., hom. 16.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad ne perveniat ad eum vindicta malorum suorum.

VERS. 19.-- Dixit ergo, etc. (ORIG.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad haec vero a perfectioribus secretius advertenda sunt?

VERS. 21.-- Non est idolum in Jacob. Alia translatio: Non erit labor in Jacob, neque videbitur dolor in Israel, in futuro scilicet saeculo.

(ORIG.) Non erit labor in Jacob, neque videbitur dolor in Israel, etc. (II Cor. XI). Futurae vitae status denuntiatur, etc., usque ad et prophetas audiant illos.

423ADominus Deus, etc. (ORIG.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad (qui luctatus est et vicit) accipiet.

Et clangor victoriae regis in illo. Admonitio praedicationis, qua in nobis rex, id est Christus, diabolum triumphat.

VERS. 22.-- Deus eduxit eum. (ID.) Alia littera, usque ad transformabit corpus humilitatis nostrae conforme corpori gloriae suae.

Cujus fortitudo. Cujus? Christi. Cujus quidquid est, unum cornu est, id est unum regnum, cujus gloria est spiritualis Israel. Ipse enim ait: Pater, da illis ut sicut ego et tu unum sumus, ita in nobis unum sint (Joan. XVII).

VERS. 23.-- Non est augurium in Jacob. (ORIG., hom. 16 in Num.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad neque 423Bdivinatio in Israel. Sequitur:

In tempore dicetur Jacob, et Israeli quod perficiet Deus: id est cum oportet, cum expedit, etc., usque ad quae Moyses (his credo de causis) vocavit immunda.

In Israel. Qui per fidei puritatem et munditiam mentis videt Deum, nec ipsum Balaam reciperet, sed verbum quod Deum posuit in ore ejus respuere non audet. Nisi enim verbum a Deo esset, non illud famulo suo Moysi procul absenti, cum Balaam haec diceret, revelasset.

VERS. 24.-- Ecce populus, etc. Alii: Ecce sicut catulus leonis exsurget, et sicut leo exsultabit. Catulus enim est, cum nuper geniti infantes rationabiles 423Csine dolo lac accipiunt: leo autem exsultans, cum vir perfectus deponit quae erant parvuli.

(ORIG.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad dedit in se credentibus nomen leonis, et catuli leonis.

Erigetur. Erigendum dicit populum futurum, de eo enim quae videbat, dixisset, erectus est.

Non accubabit. (ORIG.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad et ut leo exsultans.

Cumque duxisset. (ORIG.) Et assumpsit Balac, etc., usque ad et deserta sunt Deo negotia.

Aedifica mihi hic. (ID.) Quae sacrificant gentes, etc., usque ad qui fidem Christi non recipiunt.

CHAPTER XXIII.

VERS. 7.-- Come, he says, and curse Jacob for me. (ORIG. hom. 15.) In the Latin speech, etc., as far as and therefore he says that the word of God was set in his mouth.

421CVERS. 8.-- How shall I curse. (ID.) Another reading, etc., as far as and the authority of pronouncing be taken away.

Him whom God hath not cursed. He who marks so great a merit in the man who is cursed, and utters sentence, whom neither the quality of the sin nor the disposition of the sinner deceives: but man knows neither of these, and often, provoked by reproaches or injuries, curses. Therefore the Apostle says: Bless, and curse not (Rom. XII), that the vice of reviling may be cut away.

VERS. 9.-- From the highest, etc. As if to say, therefore he ought not to be cursed, because his life is lofty and heavenly. No one understands it unless he has ascended to eminent knowledge.

(ORIG.) Another reading, etc., as far as they shall have practised upon earth, rising 421Dtogether with Christ.

See, etc. The acts of Jacob, that is, of active men, are seen: the contemplation of Israel, that is, of contemplative men, is only understood. Or, in the resurrection to come Jacob shall be seen, that is, the body; Israel is understood, that is, the soul and spirit of those who rise again.

The people alone. Because it is not mingled with the rest of men, nor reckoned among the other nations, being ennobled by fixed privileges, by its observances and its own ordinances, just as the tribe of Levi is not mingled with the rest.

The people shall dwell alone, etc. (ORIG.) The spiritual Jacob and Israel, etc., as far as it shall be reckoned no more among the nations.

Who can number. (ID.) Another reading, etc., as far as and 422Ahe suffers those things which are written in the second book of Kings.

VERS. 10.-- Let my soul die. (ID.) He seems to prophesy of himself: but according to the letter it was fulfilled neither in Balaam nor in that Israel, nor could it be, for he died not among them but by them.

(ID.) Let my soul die the death of the just, etc., as far as: but faith which worketh by charity (Gal. V).

(GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 27.) Let my soul die the death of the just, and let my last end be like theirs, etc. Many within the Church, etc., as far as whence, Their countenances were no more changed to divers looks (I Reg. I).

And let my last end be like theirs, etc. (ORIG.) This can be understood of the 422BMagi, who came first from the East to adore Christ (Matth. II), who were of the stock of Balaam by succession of race and by the handing down of his discipline. For it is certain that they recognized the star which Balaam had foretold would rise in Israel

VERS. 11.-- What is this? Balac, hearing blessings instead of curses against his hope, and bearing it no longer, broke in upon the words of the prophesying man.

(ID.) The bitter king does not want a blessing, but seeks curses: for he is of the kindred of him to whom it is said: Cursed art thou among all the beasts of the earth (Gen. III).

Balac said therefore. He thinks him terrified by the multitude of Israel, and that therefore he did not dare to curse, and that a change of place would profit him.

422CVERS. 13.-- Thou canst not see. The madman thought that the grace of Israel could be hidden by the meanness of the place, not knowing that a city set upon a mountain cannot be hid.

VERS. 14.-- He built. The business was carried on with profane sacrifices, and divination was sought by the magic art. Yet God, willing that grace should abound where sin abounded (Rom. V), deigned to be present, not for the sacrifices, but as coming to meet him. And there he gives his word and his mysteries, and foretells things to come, where the faith and the wonder of the Gentiles chiefly hangs: so that they who will not believe our prophets may believe their own diviners.

VERS. 15.-- Stand here beside. He stood at his own holocaust, set fast in his own idolatry, and therefore he was falling the more: he therefore seems to rise who had fallen the more, when by idolatry 422Dhe seemed to stand.

God is not as a man, that he should lie. (ORIG., hom. 16.) Another reading, etc., as far as lest the vengeance of his own evils come upon him.

VERS. 19.-- He said therefore, etc. (ORIG.) Another reading, etc., as far as but are these things to be observed more secretly by the more perfect?

VERS. 21.-- There is no idol in Jacob. Another translation: There shall be no labor in Jacob, neither shall sorrow be seen in Israel, namely in the age to come.

(ORIG.) There shall be no labor in Jacob, neither shall sorrow be seen in Israel, etc. (II Cor. XI). The state of the life to come is proclaimed, etc., as far as and let them hear those prophets.

423AThe Lord God, etc. (ORIG.) Another reading, etc., as far as he (who wrestled and prevailed) shall receive.

And the sound of the king's victory is in him. The admonition of preaching, whereby in us the king, that is, Christ, triumphs over the devil.

VERS. 22.-- God brought him out. (ID.) Another reading, as far as he will transform the body of our lowliness, made conformable to the body of his glory.

Whose strength. Whose? Christ's. Whatsoever is his, is one horn, that is, one kingdom, whose glory is the spiritual Israel. For he himself says: Father, grant to them that, as I and thou are one, so they may be one in us (Joan. XVII).

VERS. 23.-- There is no augury in Jacob. (ORIG., hom. 16 in Num.) Another reading, etc., as far as neither 423Bdivination in Israel. There follows:

In time it shall be told to Jacob, and to Israel, what God will accomplish: [Vulg. temporibus suis dicetur Jacob et Israeli quid operatus sit Deus, "in their own times it shall be told to Jacob and Israel what God hath wrought"] that is, when it is fitting, when it is expedient, etc., as far as which Moses (for these causes, I believe) called unclean.

In Israel. He who through purity of faith and cleanness of mind sees God would not receive Balaam himself, yet does not dare to reject the word which set God in his mouth. For unless the word were from God, he would not have revealed it to his servant Moses, far away, at the time when Balaam was saying these things.

VERS. 24.-- Behold the people, etc. Others: Behold, he shall rise up as a lion's whelp, and shall leap up as a lion. For he is a whelp when newborn infants, reasonable, 423Ctake milk without guile: but a leaping lion when the perfect man puts away the things that were of a little child.

(ORIG.) Another reading, etc., as far as he gave to those who believe in him the name of a lion, and of a lion's whelp.

He shall be raised up. He says that the people is to be raised up hereafter, for of that which he was seeing he would have said, he is raised up.

He shall not lie down. (ORIG.) Another reading, etc., as far as and as a leaping lion.

And when he had brought him. (ORIG.) And Balac took him, etc., as far as and the affairs of God are forsaken.

Build me here. (ID.) What things the Gentiles sacrifice, etc., as far as who do not receive the faith of Christ.

CAPUT XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- Cumque, etc. (ORIG.) Cum nullum 423Ddaemonum, etc., usque ad et unumquemque in ordine suo resurrectionis gloriam adepturum.

Nequaquam abiit. Id est non stultis sensibus, sicut solebat in multis animalibus et pecudibus, Dei considerabit voluntatem: sed agnoscet quia neque de bobus cura est Deo, neque de ovibus, neque de avibus, aliisque animalibus (I Cor. IX; Rom. XV), sed quae de eis scripta sunt, propter homines intelliget scripta.

VERS. 2.-- Irruente in se. (AUG., quaest. 51 in Num.) Et factus est spiritus Dei super illum, id est super Balaam, etc., usque ad et multa similia.

VERS. 3.-- Dixit Balaam. (ORIG., hom. 17.) Dixit Balaam filius Beor, etc., usque ad et ideo dicit revelatos 424Aoculos ejus esse, quia potuit videre quod vidit.

Cujus obturatus, etc. (RAB.) Quia in somnis clausis corporeis oculis angelico ministerio haec vidit. Oculos vero mentis partim habet apertos, partim clausos, qui mysteria futura agnoscit, sed errorem non corrigit. Sic iniqui aliud scientia, aliud moribus agunt: aliud ore, aliud opere ostendunt.

VERS. 5.-- Quam pulchra. (ORIG., hom. 17.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad ut nemora umbrantia, ut paradisi super flumina, etc.

VERS. 6.-- Ut valles nemorosae. (ORIG.) Alii, etc., usque ad et in doctrina eorum quasi incedentes per opaca nemorum delectantur.

Ut horti juxta fluvios irrigui. (ID.) Alia littera, etc., 424Busque ad in tabernaculo merito dicitur habitare.

Cedri. Dei scilicet, quae suscipiunt palmites vitis de Aegypto translatae: in quibus requiescit ille fructus, cujus umbra operuit montes.

(ORIG.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad et novissimum inimicum destruat mortem.

VERS. 8.-- Eduxit illum de. Deus eduxit eum ex Aegypto post mortem scilicet Herodis, unde: Ex Aegypto vocavi Filium (Osee. XI). Quod ex hoc loco videtur assumptum et Evangelio insertum vel de Osee propheta.

Post mortem Herodis, vel, de saeculo, eduxit illum Pater ad semetipsum, ut viam faceret eis qui de hoc mundo ascensuri erant ad Deum.

Cujus fortitudo similis, etc. Omnia enim daemonum 424Cregna dejiciens, unum suum regnum quasi vere unicornis fundavit in gloria: cornu enim regnum significat.

Devorabunt gentes, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Edet gentes inimicorum suorum, et crassitudines illorum medullabit, etc. Gentes scilicet, etc., usque ad qui intelligibilis et sanctus est, et unus et multiplex dicitur et subtilis.

Et perforabunt sagittis. LXX: Et jaculis suis sagittabunt inimicum. Christus enim verbis suis vicit diabolum (Matth. IV), et omnes contradicentes superat et confringit: omnis enim qui peccat, inimicus ejus est, dum peccat; si autem verbis Dei confixus peccata sua agnoscens compungatur, et ad poenitentiam conversus, jaculis ejus confixus dicitur.

424DVERS. 9.-- Accubans dormivit ut leo, etc. Si intellexisti quantam requiem habeat iter sapientiae, quantum gratiae, quantumque dulcedinis, noli dissimulare, noli negligere, aggredere iter, nec eremi solitudinem perhorrescas; habitanti enim in hujusmodi tabernaculis occurrit manna coeleste, et angelorum panem manducabis (Exod. XVI). Incipe tantum cito, in consortium tuum venient angeli, quos significant cedri.

Quam suscitare nullus audebit; LXX: Quis suscitabit eum? Nunc enim a Patre suscitatus dicitur; nunc ipse templum corporis sui suscitare se dicit (Joan. II), ideo quasi percontantis significatur affectus.

Qui benedixerit. Qui benedicunt, Christo benedicunt 425Ain communionem paternae benedictionis assumpti. Qui autem maledicunt maledicti sunt. Judaei qui maledixerunt Christo, lacrymabiliter maledicti sunt. Quid enim posset illis evenire qui maledicunt sapientiae, et veritati et vitae, nisi ut ab his omnibus exsules jaceant? Haec enim omnia Christus est (Joan. 9).

Qui maledixerit tibi, in maledictione reputabitur: Iratusque Balac contra. (ORIG.) Ego puto quod non solum maledicit Christo, qui sermonem adversus eum profert maledictum; sed etiam qui sub nomine Christiani male agit turpiter vivit, et inhonestis verbis aut factis nomen ejus facit blasphemari. Sicut non solum Deum benedicit, qui sermonibus solis, sed et qui actibus, vita et moribus facit nomen Dei benedici.

425BVERS. 11.-- Dominus privavit te honore disposito. Ostendit Dominus quia etiam Balac intellexit non jam daemonum ministerio decipi Balaam, sed Dei virtute ad meliora transferri.

VERS. 13.-- Non potero praeterire sermonem Domini Dei mei, ut vel boni. Sciens Balaam quod non sibi per ministros solitos responsa deferrentur, sed ab eo qui habet omnium potestatem, recte videtur protestatus se non posse praeterire verbum Domini, vel proferre aliquid boni vel mali ex ore suo. Qui enim loquitur, non potest sacrificiis vel muneribus permutari, apud quem non est transmutatio, et ideo non potest sacerdos mercede mutari, ubi Deus muneribus non movetur. Discessurus tamen Balam incipit 425Citerum prophetare. Et dicit: Consilium do tibi, etc.

VERS. 14.-- Dabo tibi consilium (ORIG., hom. 18.) Veni, consilium do tibi, etc., usque ad tanquam qui vere videns sit et vere audierit.

VERS. 15.-- Dixit Balaam. (ID.) Balaam filius Beor, etc., usque ad quae pro utilitate concessa sunt.

(GREG., lib. XV Moral., cap. 22.) Dixit homo cujus obturatus est oculus. Nescit impius mala quae facit, nisi cum pro eis puniri coeperit. Consilium Balaam contra Israelitas praebuit, sed post in poena vidit quid prius ex culpa commiserit. Electi autem quia ne peccare debeant, praevident: oculi eorum ante casum 425Dpatent. Iniquus vero post casum oculos aperit, quia post culpam jam in poena conspicit, quia malum debuit vitare quod fecit.

(VERS. 16.-- Qui cadens apertos habet oculos. Saepe malus aeterna judicia admiratur, praedicationem coelestis patriae cum audit, diligit, et opera divinae dispositionis obstupescit. Jacens ergo miracula considerat, quia Dei potentiam sciendo pensat, sed vivendo non amat.

VERS. 17.-- Videbo eum, sed non modo. Alia littera: Ostendam ei, et non modo. Beatificabo et non appropinquat. Haec ad illos quorum personam gerit referuntur. Quia doctores legis et scribae ostendunt Christum in lege et prophetis promissum, sed non modo; id est quando venit, sed cum plenitudo gentium 426Aintroierit, tunc ostendent, tunc beatificabunt, modo blasphemant. Sed tempus quo haec futura sunt, longe est et in fine saeculi. Ideo ait: Ostendam illi, id est populo, qui tunc salvabitur, sed non modo.

Non modo, cum ista loquor: Quia ubi venit plenitudo temporis misit Deus Filium suum (Gal. IV).

Orietur stella ex Jacob. (ORIG.) De hac stella, etc., usque ad ut utrumque evidenter prophetatum appareat.

Et percutiet duces, principatus et potestates affigens cruci (Col. II). Aliter non potest salvare Moabitas, nisi prius vastasset duces impietatis.

Vastabitque omnes. (ORIG.) Et praedabitur omnes filios Seth. Iste est Seth filius Adam, etc., usque ad de morte revocavit ad vitam.

426BEt erit Idumaea possessio ejus. (ID.) Et erit Edom haereditas ei, Esau inimicus ejus, etc. Edom idem est qui Esau, etc., usque ad per obedientiam spiritus consors futurae haereditatis existet.

VERS. 18.-- Israel vero fortiter aget. (Id.) Et Israel fecit in virtute. Quia scilicet tunc Edom, etc., usque ad qui potest animam et corpus perdere in gehennam.

Et perdat reliquias civitatis. (ID.) Perdet liberatum de civitate, etc., usque ad cui si commorimur, etiam convivemus (II Tim. II).

VERS. 20.-- Principium. Non hoc ad historialem Amalec referri potest: non enim primus erat antiquitate; sed ad spiritalem, qui declinando populos a Deo et faciendo gentiles ex Dei cultoribus, Amalec 426Cnominatur. Et initium gentium dicitur, quia initium dedit, ut homines fierent gentiles mutando gloriam incorruptibilis Dei in similitudinem imaginis corruptibilis hominis: ut enim in populo Dei Christus est initium, sic in populo qui declinat a Deo, et gentilis efficitur Amalec.

(ORIG.) Initium gentium Amalec, etc., usque ad et omnis secta quae a Deo populum declinat, exponatur.

VERS. 21.-- Vidit quoque, etc. (ID.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad et discant non blasphemare.

Non videtur Cinaeum valde culpabiliter nominare, cui dixit Saul: Discede de medio Amalec et non percutiam te, quia fecisti misericordiam Israel, etc. (I Reg. XV.) Hujus robustum est habitaculum, si ponat in 426Dpetra, quae est Christus, nidum suum.

(RAB.) Cinaeus, etc., usque ad et omne quod praeterit cordis volatu transcendunt.

VERS. 22.-- Electus. Electi de numero sapientum, veritatis inquisitores vita vivunt perpetua; unde: elucidant me, vitam aeternam habebunt (Eccli. XXIV).

Assur. Diabolus scilicet, cui haeretici traduntur dirigendi et corrigendi.

VERS. 23.-- Parabola. Saepe parabolice loqui dicitur, ne quid in his non quasi parabolam, sed secundum litteram accipiamus.

Quis. Tam felix, qui haec videat, sentiat, intelligat, et credat, quod haec ita fecerit Deus.

Quando ista. Cum scilicet orietur stella ex Jacob, surget homo ex Israel, et delebit Amalec, et semen 427Aperibit. Id est, cum veniet Christus et cultum idolorum destruet, et daemonum potestatem subjiciet.

VERS. 24.-- Venient. Videtur significare quod juxta incarnationem Domini veniant Romani, Assyrios, Hebraeos et omnes orientales superaturi, et tandem regnum perdituri, secundum statuam Danielis (Dan., II); vel quod in adventu Christi in fine temporum, quod pars occidentalis significat, omnia regna mundi gladio spiritus, id est, verbo Dei superentur, et in tempore Antichristi cesset praedicatio Evangelii.

(ORIG.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad ei super omnem virtutem inimici.

Hebraeos. Hebraeus est qui de Aegypto transit ad 427Bterram promissionis, de tenebris ad lucem, de morte ad vitam: sed non sine magno certamine potest obtinere; affligit ergo et affligitur, caedit adversarium et caeditur. Affligunt ergo Assyrii Hebraeos, id est, populum Dei, sicut affliguntur ab eis.

Etiam ipsi peribunt. (ID.) Et ipsi pariter peribunt. Non cum Hebraeis scilicet, sed pariter, etc., usque ad quos deceptio diaboli deduxit in mortem.

VERS. 25.-- Surrexitque. (ID., hom. 20.) Quia Balaam Dei virtute constrictus, etc., usque ad rex vero Balac continuo consilio paruit.

CHAPTER XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- And when, etc. (ORIG.) Since none 423Dof the demons, etc., as far as and that each one in his own order will obtain the glory of the resurrection.

He went not. That is, he will not consider the will of God by foolish observations, as he was wont to do in many animals and beasts: but he will acknowledge that God has no care for oxen, nor for sheep, nor for birds and the other animals (I Cor. IX; Rom. XV), but he will understand that the things written about them were written for the sake of men.

VERS. 2.-- The spirit rushing upon him. (AUG., quaest. 51 in Num.) And the spirit of God came upon him, that is, upon Balaam, etc., as far as and many like things.

VERS. 3.-- Balaam said. (ORIG., hom. 17.) Balaam the son of Beor said, etc., as far as and therefore he says that his eyes were 424Auncovered, because he was able to see what he saw.

Whose is stopped, etc. (RAB.) Because in sleep, with his bodily eyes closed, he saw these things by the ministry of an angel. But he has the eyes of the mind partly open, partly closed, who acknowledges the mysteries to come but does not correct his error. So the wicked do one thing in knowledge, another in their manners: they show one thing with the mouth, another in deed.

VERS. 5.-- How beautiful. (ORIG., hom. 17.) Another reading, etc., as far as as shading groves, as paradises above the rivers, etc.

VERS. 6.-- As woody valleys. (ORIG.) Others, etc., as far as and in their teaching they take delight as men walking through the shady places of the groves.

As watered gardens beside the rivers. (ID.) Another reading, etc., 424Bas far as he is deservedly said to dwell in the tabernacle.

Cedars. Of God, namely, which take up the branches of the vine brought over out of Egypt: in which rests that fruit whose shadow covered the mountains.

(ORIG.) Another reading, etc., as far as and that he may destroy death, the last enemy.

VERS. 8.-- He brought him out of. God brought him out of Egypt, that is, after the death of Herod, whence: Out of Egypt have I called my Son (Osee. XI). Which seems to be taken from this place and inserted into the Gospel, or else from the prophet Osee.

After the death of Herod, or out of the world, the Father brought him out to himself, that he might make a way for those who were to ascend from this world to God.

Whose strength is like, etc. For casting down all the kingdoms of the demons, 424Che founded his own one kingdom in glory as a true unicorn: for the horn signifies a kingdom.

They shall devour the nations, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) He shall eat the nations of his enemies, and shall suck the marrow of their fatness, etc. The nations, namely, etc., as far as who is intelligible and holy, and is called both one and manifold and subtle.

And they shall pierce with arrows. The Seventy: And with their darts they shall shoot the enemy. For Christ by his words overcame the devil (Matth. IV), and overcomes and breaks all who contradict him: for everyone who sins is his enemy while he sins; but if, pierced by the words of God and acknowledging his sins, he be pricked to the heart and turned to repentance, he is said to be pierced by his darts.

424DVERS. 9.-- Lying down he hath slept as a lion, etc. If thou hast understood how much rest the way of wisdom has, how much grace, and how much sweetness, do not dissemble, do not neglect it, set out upon the way, and do not shudder at the solitude of the desert; for to him who dwells in tabernacles of this kind the heavenly manna comes, and thou shalt eat the bread of angels (Exod. XVI). Only begin quickly, and angels will come into thy company, whom the cedars signify.

Whom none shall dare to rouse up; the Seventy: Who shall rouse him up? For now he is said to be raised up by the Father; now he himself says that he will raise up the temple of his body (Joan. II), and therefore the feeling of one inquiring, as it were, is signified.

He that blesseth. Those who bless, bless Christ, 425Abeing taken up into the communion of the Father's blessing. But those who curse are accursed. The Jews who cursed Christ are lamentably accursed. For what could befall those who curse wisdom and truth and life, save that they should lie exiled from all these? For all these things Christ is (Joan. 9).

He that curseth thee shall be reckoned as accursed: And Balac being angry against. (ORIG.) I think that not only does he curse Christ who utters a cursing word against him, but also he who under the name of a Christian acts wickedly and lives basely, and by dishonorable words or deeds causes his name to be blasphemed. Just as not only does he bless God who blesses him with words alone, but also he who by his acts, his life and his manners causes the name of God to be blessed.

425BVERS. 11.-- The Lord hath deprived thee of the honor appointed for thee. The Lord shows that Balac too understood that Balaam was no longer being deceived by the ministry of demons, but was being carried over to better things by the power of God.

VERS. 13.-- I shall not be able to go beyond the word of the Lord my God, to utter either good. Balaam, knowing that the answers were not brought to him by his accustomed ministers, but from him who has power over all, is rightly seen to have protested that he could not go beyond the word of the Lord, nor bring forth anything of good or of evil out of his own mouth. For he who speaks cannot be bought over by sacrifices or by gifts, with whom there is no change; and therefore the priest cannot be changed for a price where God is not moved by gifts. Yet Balam, about to depart, begins 425Cto prophesy again. And he says: I give thee counsel, etc.

VERS. 14.-- I will give thee counsel (ORIG., hom. 18.) Come, I give thee counsel, etc., as far as as one who truly sees and has truly heard.

VERS. 15.-- Balaam said. (ID.) Balaam the son of Beor, etc., as far as which were granted for our profit.

(GREG., lib. XV Moral., cap. 22.) The man hath said, whose eye is stopped. The wicked man does not know the evils he does, except when he has begun to be punished for them. Balaam furnished counsel against the Israelites, but afterward in his punishment he saw what he had first committed by his fault. But the elect, because they ought not to sin, see beforehand: their eyes are open before 425Dthe fall. The unjust man, however, opens his eyes after the fall, because after the fault, already in punishment, he perceives that he ought to have avoided the evil which he did.

(VERS. 16.-- Who falling hath his eyes opened. Often the evil man marvels at the eternal judgments, and when he hears the preaching of the heavenly country he loves it, and is astonished at the works of the divine ordering. Lying down, therefore, he considers the miracles, because he weighs the power of God by knowing it, but does not love it by living.

VERS. 17.-- I shall see him, but not now. Another reading: I will show to him, and not now. I will pronounce blessed, and he draweth not nigh. These things are referred to those whose person he bears. Because the doctors of the law and the scribes show that Christ was promised in the law and the prophets, but not now; that is, when he came, but when the fulness of the Gentiles 426Ashall have come in, then they will show, then they will pronounce blessed, now they blaspheme. But the time in which these things shall be is far off, and at the end of the world. Therefore he says: I will show to him, that is, to the people which shall then be saved, but not now.

Not now, while I speak these things: Because when the fulness of time came, God sent his Son (Gal. IV).

A star shall rise out of Jacob. (ORIG.) Concerning this star, etc., as far as that both may appear to have been plainly prophesied.

And he shall strike the leaders, nailing the principalities and powers to the cross (Col. II). Otherwise he cannot save the Moabites, unless he had first laid waste the leaders of impiety.

And he shall waste all. (ORIG.) And he shall spoil all the children of Seth. This is Seth the son of Adam, etc., as far as he called back from death to life.

426BAnd Idumea shall be his possession. (ID.) And Edom shall be an inheritance to him, Esau his enemy, etc. Edom is the same as Esau, etc., as far as through obedience he will stand forth a partaker in spirit of the inheritance to come.

VERS. 18.-- But Israel shall do valiantly. (Id.) And Israel hath acted with strength. Because, namely, then Edom, etc., as far as who can destroy both soul and body in hell.

And that he may destroy the remnants of the city. (ID.) He will destroy the man delivered out of the city, etc., as far as with whom if we die together, we shall also live together (II Tim. II).

VERS. 20.-- The beginning. This cannot be referred to the historical Amalec: for he was not the first in antiquity; but to the spiritual Amalec, who by turning peoples away from God and by making Gentiles out of the worshippers of God is 426Cnamed Amalec. And he is called the beginning of the Gentiles, because he gave the beginning whereby men became Gentiles, changing the glory of the incorruptible God into the likeness of the image of corruptible man: for as in the people of God Christ is the beginning, so in the people which turns away from God and becomes Gentile the beginning is Amalec.

(ORIG.) Amalec is the beginning of the Gentiles, etc., as far as and let every sect which turns the people away from God be set forth.

VERS. 21.-- He saw also, etc. (ID.) Another reading, etc., as far as and let them learn not to blaspheme.

He does not seem to name the Cinite as one greatly blameworthy, to whom Saul said: Depart from the midst of Amalec, and I will not strike thee, because thou hast shown mercy to Israel, etc. (I Reg. XV.) His dwelling place is strong, if he sets his nest in 426Dthe rock, which is Christ.

(RAB.) The Cinite, etc., as far as and they pass beyond by the flight of the heart everything that passes away.

VERS. 22.-- Chosen. The chosen, of the number of the wise, the searchers out of truth, live with everlasting life; whence: they that explain me shall have life everlasting (Eccli. XXIV).

Assur. The devil, namely, to whom heretics are handed over to be set straight and corrected.

VERS. 23.-- A parable. He is often said to speak in parables, lest we should take anything in these things not as a parable, but according to the letter.

Who. So happy is he who may see these things, perceive them, understand them, and believe that God has so done them.

When these things. When, namely, a star shall rise out of Jacob, a man shall spring up out of Israel, and shall destroy Amalec, and the seed 427Ashall perish. That is, when Christ shall come and shall destroy the worship of idols, and shall subdue the power of the demons.

VERS. 24.-- They shall come. He seems to signify that about the time of the Lord's incarnation the Romans should come, about to overcome the Assyrians, the Hebrews and all the men of the East, and at last to lose their kingdom, according to the statue of Daniel (Dan., II); or that at the coming of Christ at the end of the times, which the western part signifies, all the kingdoms of the world should be overcome by the sword of the spirit, that is, by the word of God, and that in the time of Antichrist the preaching of the Gospel should cease.

(ORIG.) Another reading, etc., as far as to him over all the power of the enemy.

The Hebrews. A Hebrew is one who passes over from Egypt to 427Bthe land of promise, from darkness to light, from death to life: but he cannot obtain it without a great struggle; he afflicts therefore and is afflicted, he strikes down his adversary and is struck down. The Assyrians therefore afflict the Hebrews, that is, the people of God, just as they are afflicted by them.

They also shall perish. (ID.) And they likewise shall perish. Not together with the Hebrews, namely, but likewise, etc., as far as whom the deceit of the devil led down into death.

VERS. 25.-- And he rose up. (ID., hom. 20.) Because Balaam, constrained by the power of God, etc., as far as but king Balac at once obeyed the counsel.

CAPUT XXV.

VERS. 1.-- Orabatur, etc., in Setim. Interpretatione 427CHebraicorum nominum, Sittim invenimus in lingua nostra responsionem vel refutationem dici. Applicuit ergo Israel ad responsionem vel refutationem, non bene applicuit.

(ORIG., hom. 20 in Num.) Ipsa historia nos aedificat, etc., usque ad mendacio et omnibus malis, cum quibus unum corpus efficitur.

VERS. 2.-- Comederunt. Quasi, etiam de his quae immolata sunt idolis comedunt, quae exsecrabilia sunt apud Deum. Quis enim consensus templo Dei cum idolis?

(ORIG.) Ait Apostolus, etc., usque ad et qui eum recipit, idolis immolata manducat.

Et adoraverunt deos earum. Nota ordinem: malos servos primo concupiscentia decipit, inde ingluvies, 427Dpostremo captivat impietas, cujus merces exsolvitur fornicatio.

(ORIG.) Salomon cum esset sapientissimus, etc., usque ad, Adolescentularum non est numerus, una tamen est columba mea.

VERS. 3.-- Initiatus est Israel, etc. Consecratus est, scilicet mysteriis idoli, quod apud Madianitas praecipue a mulieribus colitur, et est species turpitudinis, sed scriptum vix reperitur quae, vel qualis sit, vel cujus formae, forte ne auditorem polluat. Cum ergo multae sint turpitudinis species, una Beelphegor dicitur. Omnis ergo qui turpe aliquid committit, in aliqua turpitudinis specie Beelphegor consecratur.

(ORIG.) Et initiatus est Beelphegor, etc. Per omnia 428Apeccata quae committimus, etc., usque ad praedicens quia vincula et carceres maneant eum ibi.

(ORIG.) Fortassis referuntur haec ad angelos, etc., usque ad rationem reddituri pro animabus vestris.

(ID.) Populus peccat, etc., usque ad ante quem nihil potest abscondi, nihil obscurari.

(AUG., quaest. 52 in Num.) Et dixit Dominus, etc., usque ad prudentium fidei satis evidenter ostendit.

VERS. 7.-- Quod cum, etc. (ORIG.) Haec aedificaverunt priorem populum, etc., usque ad et propitius nobis fiat Deus per verum Phineem Dominum nostrum Jesum Christum.

Arrepto pugione. Significat per crucem Christi non solum idololatriam, sed et omnem carnis affectum vel concupiscentiam perimi, et sic Deum placari.

428BVERS. 9.-- Occisi sunt viginti quatuor millia. Quia lumen veritatis et justitiae relinquentes, caecitatem idololatriae et fornicationis amaverunt, solis aeterni lumine privati, mortis tenebras incurrunt. Visibilis enim sol viginti quatuor horis orbem illustrat, et suo ambitu noctis tenebras fugat; sic et Christus ad fugandas peccati tenebras per apostolos et prophetas mundum illustrat.

VERS. 13.-- Pactum. Quisquis zelo Dei compunctus carnis stimulos contemnit, comprimit; vel subjectos, ne lasciviant, corrigit, sempiternum sacerdotium acquirit, pertinens ad eumdem, de quo dicitur: Tu es sacerdos in aeternum secundum ordinem Melchisedech (Psal. CIX), cum quo aeternum regnum possidebit. 428CInterpretatur autem Phinees ore parcens, vel ore requievit, vel oris augurium: Eleazar, Deus meus adjutor, vel Dei adjutorium; Aaron mons fortitudinis, vel mons fortis. Quae nomina congrue praedictum sensum exprimunt: qui enim se ore continet ne prava loquatur, et mysteria coelestia exponit auditoribus, per Dei adjutorium pium laborem explens, ad montem fortitudinis, id est Christum, feliciter ascendit.

Erat autem, etc. (RAB. in Num.) Israelita qui fornicatus est cum Madianitide, etc., usque ad ubi cruciatur die ac nocte usque ad novissimum quadrantem.

CHAPTER XXV.

VERS. 1.-- He was entreated, etc., in Setim. [Vulg. Nm 25:1 Morabatur autem eo tempore Israel in Settim, "was abiding in Settim"] By the interpretation 427Cof the Hebrew names, [Migne's plate sets the Hebrew word for Sittim at this point, on both printings; our digitized source has lost it, and it is not supplied here.] we find that Sittim is said in our tongue to be answer or refutation. Israel therefore encamped at answer or refutation; he did not encamp well.

(ORIG., hom. 20 in Num.) The history itself edifies us, etc., as far as with lying and with all the evils with which one body is made up.

VERS. 2.-- They ate. As if to say, they eat even of those things which have been sacrificed to idols, which are execrable before God. For what agreement has the temple of God with idols?

(ORIG.) The Apostle says, etc., as far as and he that receiveth him eateth the things sacrificed to idols.

And they adored their gods. Note the order: concupiscence first deceives evil servants, then gluttony, 427Dlast impiety takes them captive, whose wage is paid out as fornication.

(ORIG.) Solomon, though he was most wise, etc., as far as, Of the maidens there is no number, yet one is my dove.

VERS. 3.-- Israel was initiated, etc. He was consecrated, namely, to the mysteries of the idol which among the Madianites is worshipped chiefly by women; and it is a kind of foulness, but it is scarcely found written what it is, or of what sort, or of what shape, perhaps lest it defile the hearer. Since therefore there are many kinds of foulness, one is called Beelphegor. Everyone therefore who commits anything foul is consecrated to Beelphegor in some kind of foulness.

(ORIG.) And he was initiated to Beelphegor, etc. Through all 428Athe sins which we commit, etc., as far as foretelling that bonds and prisons await him there.

(ORIG.) Perhaps these things are referred to the angels, etc., as far as being about to render an account for your souls.

(ID.) The people sins, etc., as far as before whom nothing can be hidden, nothing darkened.

(AUG., quaest. 52 in Num.) And the Lord said, etc., as far as he shows plainly enough to the faith of the prudent.

VERS. 7.-- And when, etc. (ORIG.) These things edified the former people, etc., as far as and may God be made propitious to us through the true Phinees, our Lord Jesus Christ.

Having taken a dagger. It signifies that through the cross of Christ not only idolatry, but also every affection or concupiscence of the flesh is destroyed, and that God is thus appeased.

428BVERS. 9.-- There were slain four and twenty thousand. Because, leaving the light of truth and of justice, they loved the blindness of idolatry and of fornication, and, deprived of the light of the eternal sun, they run into the darkness of death. For the visible sun lights the world in four and twenty hours, and by its circuit puts to flight the darkness of night; so also Christ lights the world through the apostles and prophets to put to flight the darkness of sin.

VERS. 13.-- A covenant. Whosoever, pricked by zeal for God, despises and represses the goads of the flesh, or corrects those subject to him lest they run wanton, acquires an everlasting priesthood, pertaining to him of whom it is said: Thou art a priest for ever according to the order of Melchisedech (Psal. CIX), with whom he shall possess an eternal kingdom. 428CBut Phinees is interpreted sparing with the mouth, or he rested with the mouth, or an omen of the mouth; Eleazar, my God a helper, or the help of God; Aaron, a mountain of strength, or a strong mountain. Which names fitly express the aforesaid sense: for he who restrains himself with his mouth that he speak not perverse things, and expounds the heavenly mysteries to his hearers, fulfilling a godly labour by the help of God, happily ascends to the mountain of strength, that is, to Christ.

And there was, etc. (RAB. in Num.) The Israelite who fornicated with the Madianite woman, etc., as far as where he is tormented day and night even to the last farthing.

CAPUT XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- Postquam noxiorum, etc. (ORIG., hom. 428D21 in Num). Quia illi qui primo asciti fuerant pro peccato ceciderunt, etc., usque ad his autem cuncta quae promittuntur impleta sunt.

VERS. 2.-- Numerate omnem. Iterum populus numeratur et Levitae, ut interfectis primis carnalibus, novus populus Dei censeatur, qui per Jordanis baptismum transeat, et coelestis haereditatis repromissionem accipiat.

VERS. 53.-- Istis dividetur terra. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Reprobatur prior populus qui est in circumcisione, etc., usque ad et merae divinitatis capaces per puritatem effecti.

(ID.) Terrae hujus divisio, etc., usque ad sed illius quae urbibus contigua est.

429AVERS. 54.-- Pluribus majorem. Ut scilicet numerosior tribus majora spatia terrae sortiatur; quae minor autem fuerit, hominum numero minorem.

Unus justus, secundum quod Deo acceptus, pro pluribus habetur; unde: Per unum sapientem habitabitur civitas, tribus autem iniquorum desolabitur; et unus justus pro toto mundo. Iniqui autem, etsi multi sint, exigui et pro nihilo ducuntur apud Deum (Eccli. XVI). Est ergo multitudo laudabilis; unde Abrahae quem eduxit foras, ait: Respice coelum si potes dinumerare stellas, ita erit semen tuum, etc. (Gen. XV). In quo notandum quia justus in interioribus semper consistit, quia in abscondito orat Patrem, et omnis gloria filiae regis, id est, regalis animae, intrinsecus est: sed Deus educit eum foras, cum res postulat et rerum visibilium ratio.

429BVERS. 55.-- Ut sors terram. Per sortem praecipitur dividi haereditas, sed video ipsummet Moysen cui ista mandantur, non sorte dividere haereditatem Ruben, Gad, et dimidiae tribui Manasse (Josue XV): Jesus quoque Nave, extra sortem dat haereditatem tribui Judae et Caleb et tribui Ephraim, et dimidiae tribui Manasse. In caeteris sors mittitur: unde puto, quod in coelesti haereditate aliqui non venient ad sortem, neque cum caeteris (quamvis sancti sint) numerabuntur, sed erit egregia eorum haereditas, sicut fuit Caleb, et tribui Judae, et Jesu filio Nave. Sicut enim cum adepta victoria dividuntur spolia, egregii bellatores cum caeteris non ducuntur ad sortem, sed optima quaeque virtutum merito percipiunt, 429Ccaeteri sorte utuntur jure victoriae: ita videtur mihi Christus Dominus meus facturus. Illis enim praecipuos et sublimes decernet honores, quorum facta magnifica et sublimes cognoscit virtutes, unde ait: Volo, Pater, ut ubi ego sum, illic et isti mecum sint. (Joan. XVII). Et alibi, Sedebitis et vos super duodecim thronos, judicantes duodecim tribus, etc. (Matth. XIX). Et alibi, Sicut tu in me, Pater, et ego in te, ita et isti in nobis unum sint (Joan. XVII). Haec omnia non sorte descendunt, sed dilectionis praerogativa.

VERS. 65.-- Nisi Caleb filius Jephone, et Josue filius Nun. (ISID.) Sexcenta millia armatorum de Aegypto dicuntur egressa, et duo tantum in terram promissionis ingressi. Multi enim per baptismum ad fidem transeunt, sed ad coelestem patriam paucissimi 429Dperveniunt, secundum illud: Multi enim sunt vocati, pauci vero electi (Matth. XX). Duo tantum ingrediuntur, propter eos qui ex utroque populo coeleste regnum adipiscuntur, vel qui per actionem et contemplationem ad aeternam beatitudinem praedestinantur.

CHAPTER XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- After the blood of the guilty, etc. (ORIG., hom. 428D21 in Num). Because those who had first been enrolled fell for sin, etc., as far as but to these all the things which are promised have been fulfilled.

VERS. 2.-- Number all. Again the people and the Levites are numbered, so that, the first and carnal ones being slain, a new people of God may be reckoned, which may pass through the baptism of the Jordan and receive the promise of the heavenly inheritance.

VERS. 53.-- To these shall the land be divided. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) The former people, which is in circumcision, is reproved, etc., as far as and made capable of unmixed divinity through purity.

(ID.) The division of this land, etc., as far as but of that which is contiguous to cities.

429AVERS. 54.-- To the greater number the greater part. That is, that the more numerous tribe may obtain by lot greater spaces of land; but whichever shall be the lesser, a lesser, by the number of men.

One just man, in respect of that whereby he is acceptable to God, is reckoned for many; whence: By one wise man shall a city be inhabited, but a tribe of the unrighteous shall be made desolate; and one just man for the whole world. But the unrighteous, although they be many, are counted as few and as nothing before God (Eccli. XVI) [Vulg. Sir 16:5 Ab uno sensato inhabitabitur patria: tribus impiorum deseretur, "by one man of sense shall a country be inhabited"; the received text has nothing answering to the two clauses that follow]. There is therefore a praiseworthy multitude; whence to Abraham, whom he led forth out of doors, he says: Look upon heaven, if thou canst number the stars: so shall thy seed be, etc. (Gen. XV) [Vulg. Gn 15:5 Suspice caelum, et numera stellas, si potes … Sic erit semen tuum, "look up to heaven and number the stars"]. Wherein it is to be noted that the just man always abides in things inward, because he prays the Father in secret, and all the glory of the king's daughter, that is, of the royal soul, is within: but God leads him forth out of doors, when the matter and the reason of visible things demands it.

429BVERS. 55.-- That the lot may divide the land. By lot the inheritance is commanded to be divided; but I see that Moses himself, to whom these things are commanded, did not divide by lot the inheritance of Ruben, of Gad, and of the half tribe of Manasse (Josue XV): Josue the son of Nun also gives an inheritance outside the lot to the tribe of Juda and to Caleb and to the tribe of Ephraim, and to the half tribe of Manasse. In the rest the lot is cast: whence I think that in the heavenly inheritance some will not come to the lot, nor be numbered with the rest (although they be holy), but their inheritance shall be excellent, as was that of Caleb, and of the tribe of Juda, and of Josue the son of Nun. For just as, when the victory has been won and the spoils are divided, the excellent warriors are not led with the rest to the lot, but receive by merit whatsoever things of the several kinds are best, 429Cwhile the rest use the lot by the law of victory: so it seems to me that Christ my Lord will do. For to those whose deeds he knows to be great and their virtues lofty he will decree special and lofty honours, whence he says: I will, Father, that where I am, there these also may be with me. (Joan. XVII). And elsewhere, You also shall sit upon twelve thrones, judging the twelve tribes, etc. (Matth. XIX) [Vulg. Mt 19:28 sedebitis et vos super sedes duodecim, "upon twelve seats"]. And elsewhere, As thou, Father, in me, and I in thee, so let these also be one in us (Joan. XVII). All these things come not down by lot, but by the prerogative of love.

VERS. 65.-- Except Caleb the son of Jephone, and Josue the son of Nun. (ISID.) Six hundred thousand armed men are said to have gone out of Egypt, and only two to have entered into the land of promise. For many pass over to the faith through baptism, but very few 429Dcome to the heavenly country, according to that: For many are called, but few chosen (Matth. XX). Only two enter in, because of those who from either people obtain the heavenly kingdom, or who through action and contemplation are predestined to eternal blessedness.

CAPUT XXVII.

VERS. 6.-- Da eis. (ORIG., hom. 22 in Num.) Dabis ei possessionem, etc. Haec secundum historiam etc., usque ad recte ergo locutae sunt filiae Saphaad.

VERS. 8.-- Homo cum mortuus fuerit absque filio, etc. (ID.) Supponitur lex de successione, etc., usque ad non perdet mercedem suam.

430AVERS. 12.-- Ascende in montem, etc. (ID., hom. 22.) Vide quomodo qui perfectus et beatus est, etc., usque ad Ille ergo solus debet requiri, qui peccatum non fecit.

(ID.) Mors Moysi finis legis, etc., usque ad id est, sub velamento Patris et Filii et Spiritus sancti requiescet anima nostra.

VERS. 14.-- Quia offendistis, etc. (AUG., quaest. 53 in Num.) Eam causam mortis Moysi dicit Dominus, quam et fratris ejus, quia scilicet non sanctificaverunt eum coram populo ad aquam contradictionis, id est quia dubitaverunt quod dono ejus posset aqua de petra profluere.

Mystice autem significatur, quia nec vetus sacerdotium, cujus personam Aaron gerebat, nec ipsa lex, cujus personam gerebat Moyses, introducunt 430Bpopulum Dei in terram aeternae haereditatis: sed Jesus, in quo significatur Christus, id est gratia per fidem. Et Aaron quidem ante defunctus est quam Israel in aliquam partem terrae promissionis intraret: Moyse autem adhuc vivente capta est terra Amorrhaeorum et possessa, sed Jordanem cum eis non transiit. Ex aliqua enim parte lex observatur in fide Christiana. Ibi enim sunt etiam praecepta quae usque hodie Christiani observare jubentur. Sacerdotium vero illud et sacrificium nullam partem tenent hodie fidei Christianae, nisi quod in umbris futurorum acta et transacta. Cum vero ambobus fratribus, ut apponantur ad populum suum dicitur, manifestum est non esse in illis iram Dei, quae separat a pace 430Caeternae societatis. Unde patet non solum officia, sed et mortes eorum signa fuisse futurorum, non supplicia indignationis Dei.

Provideat. Nota, non elegit filios, non nepotes, nec rogat ut constituantur duces: Dei judicio electionem committit.

VERS. 16.-- Provideat Dominus Deus. (ORIG.) Recessurus de saeculo, etc., usque ad ut possint eum audire filii Israel.

Spiritus. Sanctus scilicet. Non enim de spiritu hominis hoc diceret, quem nullus erat qui non haberet. Jubetur tamen manus ei imponere, ne quisquam quantalibet pollens gratia sacramenta consecrationis audeat recusare.

VERS. 20.-- Et dabis. (ISID. in Num. ) Jesus succedit 430DMoysi, etc., usque ad et remissionem peccatorum, etc.

Et partem gloriae. Quasi, facies eum socium gloriae tuae: non enim res hujusmodi quasi partiliter divisae minuuntur, sed totae sunt omnibus, totae singulis, qui earum habent societatem.

Ut audiat, etc. (ORIG., hom. 22) Si sacrificia et instituta legalia usque in praesens mansissent, Evangelii fidem exclusissent. Erat enim in illis magnitudo et reverentiae plena religio, quae primo aspectu stupefaceret intuentem. Quis enim videns sanctuarium, altare, sacerdotes sacrificium consummantes, et omnem illius rei ordinem, non putaret plenissimum hunc esse ritum quo Deus coleretur creator omnium? 431ASed gratias adventui Christi, qui illa destruit quae magna videbantur in terris, et cultu Dei a visibilibus ad invisibilia transtulit, et a temporalibus ad aeterna. Sed ipse Christus aures requirit quae haec audiant, et oculos qui haec videant.

VERS. 21.-- Pro hoc, etc. Moraliter instruimur, ut quisquis accipiat potestatem in populo secundum magisterium divinae legis (quae in sacerdotis officio maxime commendatur) vitam suam et subditorum ordinet atque regat.

CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS. 6.-- Give to them. (ORIG., hom. 22 in Num.) Thou shalt give him a possession, etc. These things according to the history etc., as far as rightly therefore have the daughters of Saphaad spoken.

VERS. 8.-- A man when he shall die without a son, etc. (ID.) The law of succession is subjoined, etc., as far as he shall not lose his reward.

430AVERS. 12.-- Go up into the mountain, etc. (ID., hom. 22.) See how he that is perfect and blessed, etc., as far as He alone therefore must be sought, who did no sin.

(ID.) The death of Moses is the end of the law, etc., as far as that is, under the veil of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit our soul shall rest.

VERS. 14.-- Because you offended, etc. (AUG., quaest. 53 in Num.) The Lord names that cause of the death of Moses which he names also of his brother's, namely because they did not sanctify him before the people at the water of contradiction, that is, because they doubted that by his gift water could flow forth from the rock.

But mystically it is signified that neither the old priesthood, whose person Aaron bore, nor the law itself, whose person Moses bore, brings in 430Bthe people of God into the land of the eternal inheritance: but Jesus, in whom Christ is signified, that is, grace through faith. And Aaron indeed died before Israel entered into any part of the land of promise: but while Moses was yet living the land of the Amorrhites was taken and possessed, though he did not cross the Jordan with them. For in some part the law is observed in the Christian faith. For there are also precepts there which Christians are bidden to observe even to this day. But that priesthood and that sacrifice hold no part today of the Christian faith, save that they were done and finished in the shadows of things to come. But when it is said of both brothers that they should be gathered to their own people, it is manifest that there was not in them the wrath of God, which separates from the peace 430Cof the eternal fellowship. Whence it appears that not only their offices, but their deaths also, were signs of things to come, not punishments of God's indignation.

Let him provide. Note, he did not choose sons, he did not choose grandsons, nor does he ask that they be appointed leaders: he commits the choice to the judgment of God.

VERS. 16.-- May the Lord God provide. (ORIG.) Being about to depart from the world, etc., as far as that the children of Israel may be able to hear him.

A spirit. The Holy Spirit, namely. For he would not say this of the spirit of man, which there was none who had not. Yet he is bidden to lay his hand upon him, lest anyone, however endowed with grace, dare to refuse the sacraments of consecration.

VERS. 20.-- And thou shalt give. (ISID. in Num. ) Jesus succeeds 430DMoses, etc., as far as and the remission of sins, etc.

And part of thy glory. As if to say, thou shalt make him a partner of thy glory: for things of this kind are not diminished as though divided out in parts, but are whole to all, whole to each, who have fellowship in them.

That he may hear, etc. (ORIG., hom. 22) If the sacrifices and the legal institutions had remained until the present, they would have shut out the faith of the Gospel. For there was in them a greatness and a religion full of reverence, which at the first sight would astound the beholder. For who, seeing the sanctuary, the altar, the priests completing the sacrifice, and the whole order of that matter, would not think this to be the fullest rite whereby God the creator of all things was worshipped? 431ABut thanks be to the coming of Christ, who destroys those things which seemed great on earth, and in the worship of God has carried men over from things visible to things invisible, and from things temporal to things eternal. But Christ himself requires ears which may hear these things, and eyes which may see these things.

VERS. 21.-- For this, etc. Morally we are instructed that whosoever receives power over a people according to the mastership of the divine law (which is chiefly commended in the office of the priest) should order and rule his own life and the life of those subject to him.

CAPUT XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Dixit quoque. (ORIG., hom. 23 in Num.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad qui putat aliquid se Deo praestare velut indigenti.

431B(ID.) In diebus festis meis. Habet Deus dies festos suos, etc., usque ad ubi adhuc nulla sunt peccata, dies festos meos dicit.

VERS. 3.-- Haec sunt sacrificia. Quaeramus ergo diligenter, etc., usque ad sed sunt alii dies festi his qui non possunt indesinenter immolare sacrificium castitatis.

VERS. 9.-- Die autem Sabbati. (RAB. in Num. ) Secunda ergo festivitas est, etc., usque ad qui sacerdos est in aeternum secundum ordinem Melchisedech (Hebr. V).

Et duas decimas similae oleo conspersae. (RAB.) Haec offert qui spirituali gratia repletus scientiam duorum testamentorum, docente Spiritu sancto, verbis et exemplis docet auditores suos, non pro temporali 431Clucro, sed pro vitae aeternae praemio.

VERS. 11.-- In Kalendis. Id est in neomeniis, cum Ecclesia vel quaelibet anima soli justitiae sic propinquat, ut unus spiritus cum eo fiat, et per ipsum innovata veterem hominem abjiciat.

(ID.) Tertia festivitas neomeniae dies, etc., usque ad innovata jugulat.

Vitulos de armento. Potest in duobus vitulis jucunditas boni operis in anima vel in corpore signari: quae secundum duo Testamenta offertur Domino in duobus praeceptis charitatis.

Arietem unum. In ariete regimen disciplinae forte et immobile secundum unitatem fidei catholicae.

Agnos anniculos septem. Simplicitatem et mansuetudinem morum pro coelesti requie per gratiam septiformis 431DSpiritus.

VERS. 12.-- Et tres decimas similae. Haec offert qui scientiam spiritualem in sanctae Trinitatis fide, oleo laetitiae illuminatus exhibet cum bono opere.

Et duas decimas. Hoc facit, qui decalogum legis in duobus praeceptis charitatis ad suam et proximorum utilitatem studet convertere, et sic totam vitam suam in suavem odorem Domino dedicare.

VERS. 13.-- Et decimam decimae, etc., per agnos singulos. Hoc fit cum simplicitas morum secundum Scripturarum regulam gratia Spiritus sancti cooperante instituitur et ornatur. Bene autem haec omnia in holocaustum Domino jubentur offerri, quia haec agere debemus, non ut humanam laudem, vel praesentem 432Aretributionem quaeramus, sed omnium bonorum datori Deo per omnia placeamus, et aeternae vitae retributionem mereamur.

VERS. 16.-- Mense. (ORIG., hom. 23 in Num.) Quarto loco ponitur inter festivitates, etc., usque ad et diem festum agit cum Deo et angelis ejus.

VERS. 17.-- Quintadecima die. (ID.) Sequitur huic continua festivitas, etc., usque ad efficitur nequior et deterior.

(ID.) Omnes qui imbuendi sunt altioribus disciplinis, etc., usque ad sacrificiis spiritalibus in purificatione mentis oblatis.

VERS. 26.-- Dies etiam primitivorum quando offeretis novas fruges, etc. (ORIG.) Sexta festivitas dicitur novorum, etc., usque ad bonitatem, mansuetudinem 432Bet similia.

CHAPTER XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- The Lord also said. (ORIG., hom. 23 in Num.) Another reading, etc., as far as who thinks that he bestows something upon God as upon one in need.

431B(ID.) In my festival days. God has his own festival days, etc., as far as where as yet there are no sins, he calls them my festival days.

VERS. 3.-- These are the sacrifices. Let us therefore seek diligently, etc., as far as but there are other festival days for those who cannot unceasingly offer the sacrifice of chastity.

VERS. 9.-- But on the sabbath day. (RAB. in Num. ) The second festival therefore is, etc., as far as who is a priest for ever according to the order of Melchisedech (Hebr. V).

And two tenths of flour tempered with oil. (RAB.) He offers these who, filled with spiritual grace, teaches his hearers by words and by examples the knowledge of the two testaments, the Holy Spirit teaching, not for temporal 431Cgain, but for the reward of eternal life.

VERS. 11.-- On the calends. That is, in the new moons, when the Church or any soul so draws near to the sun of justice that it becomes one spirit with him, and, renewed through him, casts off the old man.

(ID.) The third festival is the day of the new moon, etc., as far as being renewed she slays.

Calves of the herd. In the two calves can be signified the gladness of good work in the soul or in the body: which is offered to the Lord according to the two Testaments in the two precepts of charity.

One ram. In the ram, the governance of discipline, strong and unmoved according to the unity of the catholic faith.

Seven lambs of a year old. Simplicity and gentleness of manners for heavenly rest through the grace of the sevenfold 431DSpirit.

VERS. 12.-- And three tenths of flour. He offers these who, enlightened with the oil of gladness, sets forth spiritual knowledge in the faith of the holy Trinity together with good work.

And two tenths. This he does who studies to turn the decalogue of the law into the two precepts of charity for his own profit and his neighbours', and so to dedicate his whole life to the Lord for a sweet odour.

VERS. 13.-- And the tenth of a tenth, etc., for every lamb. This is done when simplicity of manners is established and adorned according to the rule of the Scriptures, the grace of the Holy Spirit working with it. And rightly are all these things commanded to be offered to the Lord in holocaust, because we ought to do these things not that we may seek human praise or a present 432Arecompense, but that we may in all things please God the giver of all good things, and may merit the recompense of eternal life.

VERS. 16.-- The month. (ORIG., hom. 23 in Num.) It is set in the fourth place among the festivals, etc., as far as and he keeps a festival day with God and with his angels.

VERS. 17.-- On the fifteenth day. (ID.) There follows upon this a continuous festival, etc., as far as he is made more wicked and worse.

(ID.) All who are to be imbued with higher disciplines, etc., as far as with spiritual sacrifices offered in the purification of the mind.

VERS. 26.-- The day also of the firstfruits, when you shall offer new fruits, etc. (ORIG.) The sixth festival is called that of the new things, etc., as far as goodness, gentleness 432Band the like.

CAPUT XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- Mensis etiam septimi. (ORIG., hom. 23 in Num.) Sequitur festivitas septimorum, etc., usque ad quem posuit Deus propitiatorem in sanguine suo, per fidem.

(ORIG.) Ultimus dies festus, etc., usque ad, Cum autem venerit quod perfectum est, evacuabitur quod ex parte est (I Cor. XIII).

CHAPTER XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- The seventh month also. (ORIG., hom. 23 in Num.) There follows the festival of the sevenths, etc., as far as whom God hath set forth as a propitiator in his own blood, through faith.

(ORIG.) The last festival day, etc., as far as, But when that which is perfect is come, that which is in part shall be done away (I Cor. XIII).

CAPUT XXX.

VERS. 3.-- Si quis. Animae quae in perfectum virum occurrit, nemo dominatur in votis, sed libertatem suam habet in illis.

(ORIG., hom. 24 in Num.) Votorum lex ponitur, etc., 432Cusque ad ut facias tantum quod pertinet ad divinum cultum.

(ID.) Animarum, quae in Ecclesia Dei sunt, etc., usque ad nec necesse sit nobis sub tutoribus et procuratoribus derelinqui a Patre.

Juramento constrinxerit, etc. (AUG., quaest. 56 in Num.) Non hoc ad omnem, etc., usque ad sed per votum ipse sibi efficit non licere.

VERS. 4.-- Mulier si, etc. Id est, si femina, et adhuc tenera fuerit anima, cui vir, vel pater dominatur in votis: non semper in ipsa est culpa, sed interdum redit ad viros vel parentes.

Et juramentum; LXX: Et definitiones quas definivit adversus animam suam. Non quod talibus votis noceant animae suae: sed adversus animam, dicitur 432Dadversus animalem delectationem, sicut ibi: Affligetis animas vestras.

(AUG., quaest. 57) Merito quaeritur in hoc loco etiam de voto virginitatis: mulieres enim, etiam virgines, in Scriptura solent appellari; et videtur Apostolus de patre loqui, cum dicit: Servet virginem suam, et det virginem suam, etc. (I Cor. VII). Hoc modo, nuptum ubi nonnulli intellexerunt virginem suam, id est virginitatem suam: nulla tamen hoc simili Scripturarum locutione demonstrant, cum sit inusitatissima.

VERS. 6.-- Nec obnoxia tenebitur. LXX: Dominus mundabit eam, quia abnuit pater ejus. Id est mundam habebit, et judicabit, sicut dicitur: Mundabit eum sacerdos, et mundatione non mundabis reum (Levit. 433AXIII). Id est non dices mundum eum qui immundus est.

VERS. 7.-- Si maritum. Id est, si adulta et viripotens fuerit ad concipiendum semen verbi Dei, et doctrinae spiritalis capienda secreta; unde: Volo autem omnes vos uni viro virginem castam exhibere Christo (II Cor. XI).

(AUG. quaest. 59) Quia Dominus mandavit, etc., usque ad cum ipse Deus hoc praeceperit, et hoc voluerit.

VERS. 11.-- Uxor in domo. (AUG.) De illa prius dixit, quae in domo patris sui vovit, etc., usque ad sic tamen, ut non nisi rationi motio illa consentiat.

CHAPTER XXX.

VERS. 3.-- If any man. Over the soul which comes unto the perfect man no one has dominion in vows, but she has her own liberty in them.

(ORIG., hom. 24 in Num.) The law of vows is laid down, etc., 432Cas far as that thou do only that which pertains to the divine worship.

(ID.) Of the souls which are in the Church of God, etc., as far as nor need we be left by the Father under tutors and procurators.

He shall have bound himself by an oath, etc. (AUG., quaest. 56 in Num.) This does not belong to every, etc., as far as but by his vow he himself makes it not lawful for himself.

VERS. 4.-- A woman if, etc. That is, if it be a female, and a soul as yet tender, over which a husband or a father has dominion in vows: the fault is not always in her, but sometimes it returns upon the husbands or the parents.

And an oath; LXX: And the definitions which she has defined against her own soul. Not that they harm their own soul by such vows: but "against the soul" is said 432Dagainst the animal delight, as there: You shall afflict your souls.

(AUG., quaest. 57) With reason is it asked in this place also concerning the vow of virginity: for women, even virgins, are wont to be so called in Scripture; and the Apostle seems to speak of the father, when he says: Let him keep his virgin, and let him give his virgin, etc. (I Cor. VII) [Vulg. 1 Cor 7:37-38 servare virginem suam … qui matrimonio jungit virginem suam, "to keep his virgin" … "he that giveth his virgin in marriage"]. In this way, nuptum, where some have understood his virgin, that is, his own virginity: yet they show this by no like locution of the Scriptures, since it is most unusual.

VERS. 6.-- Neither shall she be held liable. LXX: The Lord shall cleanse her, because her father refused. That is, he shall hold her clean, and shall so judge, as it is said: The priest shall cleanse him, and by cleansing thou shalt not cleanse the guilty (Levit. 433AXIII). That is, thou shalt not call him clean who is unclean.

VERS. 7.-- If she have a husband.
