That is, if she be grown up and marriageable, to conceive the seed of the word of God and to take in the secrets of spiritual teaching; whence: But I will that you all present a chaste virgin to one husband, Christ (II Cor. XI) [Vulg. 2 Cor 11:2 Despondi enim vos uni viro, virginem castam exhibere Christo, "For I have espoused you to one husband"].

(AUG. quaest. 59) Because the Lord commanded, etc., as far as since God himself commanded this, and willed this.

VERS. 11.-- A wife in the house. (AUG.) Of her he spoke first, who vowed in the house of her father, etc., as far as yet so, that that motion consent to nothing but reason.

CAPUT XXXI

433BVERS. 2.-- Ulciscere prius. (ORIG.) Scandala filiis Israel Madianitarum tergiversatione acciderant, etc., usque ad ubi recte ambulanti deceptio ad peccandum subjicitur.

Et sic colligeris. Novissime inquit: Apponeris ad populum tuum, etc. Hoc contra Samaritas, qui negant resurrectionem mortuorum, nec fidem futuri saeculi recipiunt. Nemo enim apponitur ad eos qui non sunt: constat ergo esse aliquem populum cui Moysen dicit esse apponendum.

VERS. 4.-- Mille viri de singulis, etc. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Adverte divinae virtutis magnificentiam, etc., usque ad talibus enim armis instructi diabolica castra fugabimus.

VERS. 8.-- Et reges eorum, etc. (ORIG., hom. 25 433Cin Num.) Interficiuntur reges Madianitarum, etc., usque ad factus est nobis a Deo justitia, et pax, et redemptio.

Balaam quoque. Patet consilio Balaam subornatas esse mulieres Madianitarum quae deciperant Israel; unde hic velut auctor sceleris interficitur.

(ORIG.) Dictum est superius, quod Balaam rediit in locum suum. Sed forte reversus est, quamvis Scriptura non dixerit: vel forte in locum suum rediit ab eo loco ubi sacrificia faciebat, in eum scilicet locum, ubi tanquam peregrinus habebat hospitium; non enim dictum est in domum suam, aut in patriam suam. De Balac autem dictum est, ad semetipsum. Id est, ubi tanquam dominus habitabat.

Cur feminas. (RAB.) Mystice feminam in praedam 433Dde bello revertens servat, etc., usque ad, ut quae Deo placita sunt desideremus, et perficere studeamus.

VERS. 16.-- Ad suggestionem. Hic evidentius ostenditur ipsius Balaam suggestione mulieres subornatas fuisse.

(ISID.) Non dubitavit Balaam, oblata copia feminarum, fornicationis ruina Israelitas collapsuros, quia concupiscibiles animae eorum partes sciebat esse corruptas: ita daemones unumquemque pertentant, illis affectibus animae laqueos tradentes quibus nos senserint aegrotare.

VERS. 21.-- Eleazar. Nota quod Eleazar non ad omnem populum, sed ad viros virtutis loquitur, 434Aqui revertebantur de praelio: viri autem virtutis sunt, qui ad bellum procedunt, qui in agone contendunt, et ab omnibus se abstinent; alii vero non viri sunt virtutis.

VERS. 22.-- Aurum. Tempus belli est in hoc mundo contra nequitias spirituales et concupiscentias carnales. Spectat nos angelorum chorus, et virtutum coelestium pia erga nos pendet exspectatio, quando vel quomodo de praelio revertamur, quis plus afferat auri vel argenti, quis lapides pretiosos curiose intuetur. Requirunt etiam qui deferunt aes, qui ferrum, qui plumbum, vel vas ligneum, vel fictile aut aliquid hujusmodi magnae domus usibus necessarium. Secundum ergo ea quae quisque detulerit, mansionis ei meritum deputabitur. Probantur tamen 434Bhaec omnia, alia per ignem, alia per aquam uniuscujusque opus quale sit ignis probabit.

Et lavabitis, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Vides quomodo purgatione indiget omnis qui exit de praelio hujus vitae, etc., usque ad ut mundus ingrediatur civitatem Dei.

VERS. 27.-- Qui pugnaverunt. (ORIG., hom. 26 in Num.) Sunt quidam in populo Dei, etc., usque ad tanto praestantior est numerus quingentorum quam quinquaginta.

(ORIG.) Differentias esse profectus et meritorum in populo fidelium, etc., usque ad Catenulae vero verbi et doctrinae connexiones significant.

VERS. 50.-- Depreceris. (ORIG.) Ad propitiandum Deum pro nobis, etc., usque ad pro quibus solis decet 434Ceum propitiari.

VERS. 51.-- Susceperuntque. Alia littera: Et accepit Moyses et Eleazar sacerdos aurum ab omnibus tribunis et centurionibus, et intulit illud in tabernaculum testimonii memoriale filiis Israel coram Domino. Nota quia quae dicuntur, non ad conspectum visibilem, sed ad mentis memoriam referuntur. Beatus enim est qui recordatur se aliquid boni operis fecisse coram Domino et obtulisse munera beneplacita Deo, virtutes scilicet animi et ornamenta pietatis.

CHAPTER XXXI

433BVERS. 2.-- Revenge first. (ORIG.) Scandals had befallen the children of Israel by the shuffling of the Madianites, etc., as far as where deceit unto sinning is laid under one that walks rightly.

And so thou shalt be gathered. At the last he says: Thou shalt be added to thy people, etc. This is against the Samaritans, who deny the resurrection of the dead, nor receive the faith of the world to come. For no man is added to those who are not: it is certain therefore that there is some people to which he says that Moses is to be added.

VERS. 4.-- A thousand men of every, etc. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Mark the magnificence of the divine power, etc., as far as for armed with such weapons we shall put to flight the camps of the devil.

VERS. 8.-- And their kings, etc. (ORIG., hom. 25 433Cin Num.) The kings of the Madianites are killed, etc., as far as he was made unto us of God justice, and peace, and redemption.

Balaam also. It is plain that by the counsel of Balaam the women of the Madianites were suborned who had deceived Israel; whence he is here killed as the author of the crime.

(ORIG.) It was said above that Balaam returned unto his own place. But perhaps he did return, although Scripture has not said so: or perhaps he returned unto his own place from that place where he was offering sacrifices, namely unto that place where he had his lodging as a stranger; for it is not said unto his own house, or unto his own country. But of Balac it was said, unto himself. That is, where he dwelt as a lord.

Why the women. (RAB.) Mystically, returning from the war he keeps a woman for spoil 433D, etc., as far as, that we may desire the things which are pleasing to God, and study to accomplish them.

VERS. 16.-- At the suggestion. Here it is shown more evidently that the women were suborned at the suggestion of Balaam himself.

(ISID.) Balaam did not doubt, a store of women having been offered, that the Israelites would fall by the ruin of fornication, because he knew that the concupiscible parts of their souls were corrupted: so the demons make trial of every man, delivering snares to those affections of the soul in which they have perceived us to be sick.

VERS. 21.-- Eleazar. Note that Eleazar speaks not to all the people, but to the men of valour, 434Awho were returning from the battle: but they are men of valour who go forth to war, who strive in the contest, and abstain from all things; but the others are not men of valour.

VERS. 22.-- Gold. It is the time of war in this world against spiritual wickednesses and carnal concupiscences. The choir of angels beholds us, and the loving expectation of the heavenly virtues hangs upon us, when or how we shall return from the battle, who brings back more gold or silver, who looks curiously upon precious stones. They inquire also who carry brass, who iron, who lead, or a vessel of wood, or of earthenware, or anything of this kind necessary for the uses of a great house. According therefore to those things which each one shall have carried back, the merit of his dwelling shall be assigned to him. Yet all 434Bthese things are tried, some by fire, some by water the fire shall try of what sort each man's work is.

And you shall wash, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Thou seest how everyone who goes out of the battle of this life needs cleansing, etc., as far as that he may enter clean into the city of God.

VERS. 27.-- Who fought. (ORIG., hom. 26 in Num.) There are certain men in the people of God, etc., as far as by so much is the number of five hundred more excellent than fifty.

(ORIG.) That there are differences of advancement and of merits in the people of the faithful, etc., as far as But the little chains signify the connections of the word and of doctrine.

VERS. 50.-- That thou mayest pray. (ORIG.) To make God propitious for us, etc., as far as for whom alone it is fitting 434Cthat he be made propitious.

VERS. 51.-- And they received. Another reading: And Moses and Eleazar the priest took the gold from all the tribunes and centurions, and brought it into the tabernacle of the testimony, a memorial of the children of Israel before the Lord. Note that the things which are said are referred not to visible sight, but to the memory of the mind. For blessed is he who remembers that he has done some good work before the Lord, and has offered gifts well-pleasing to God, namely the virtues of the mind and the ornaments of piety.

CAPUT XXXII.

VERS. 1.-- Filii autem Ruben, etc. (ORIG., hom. 26 in Num.) Omnia quae dicuntur non solum ex sermone, 434Dqui dicitur, pensanda sunt, sed ex persona dicentis, etc., usque ad sed per Moysen haereditatem extra Jordanem accipiunt.

VERS. 4.-- Terra quam percussit Dominus. (GREG., lib. XXVII Moral., cap. 10.) Sunt multi in Ecclesia qui parvuli esse despiciunt, etc., usque ad dum transitoriis excaecantur.

Jumenta plurima. (ORIG.) Jumenta et pecora multa sunt, etc. Prior populus, etc., usque ad qui est terra promissionis intrare.

VERS. 29.-- Si transierint filii Gad et filii Ruben, etc. (ORIG., hom. 26.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad sed quae sursum est, et libera, et mater omnium nostrum est.

CHAPTER XXXII.

VERS. 1.-- And the sons of Ruben, etc. (ORIG., hom. 26 in Num.) All the things which are said are to be weighed not only from the discourse 434Dwhich is spoken, but from the person of the speaker, etc., as far as but through Moses they receive an inheritance beyond the Jordan.

VERS. 4.-- The land which the Lord struck. (GREG., lib. XXVII Moral., cap. 10.) There are many in the Church who despise to be little ones, etc., as far as while they are blinded by things transitory.

Very many cattle. (ORIG.) There are cattle and much flock, etc. The former people, etc., as far as who is the land of promise to enter.

VERS. 29.-- If the sons of Gad and the sons of Ruben shall pass over, etc. (ORIG., hom. 26.) Another reading, etc., as far as but that which is above, and free, and is the mother of us all.

CAPUT XXXIII.

  • 435A

VERS. 1.-- Hae sunt mansiones, etc. (ORIG., hom. 27 in Num.) Diversas creavit Deus ciborum differentias, etc., usque ad aliam vero, qua post resurrectionem ascensura ad coelos, non subito nec importune condescendit, sed per multas mansiones, in quibus singulis lumine sapientiae illustrata ad ipsum patrem luminum perveniat.

Per turmas suas. Septuaginta sic: Cum virtute sua, Christo, scilicet, qui est Dei virtus et Dei potentia. Cum ipso ergo ascenditur, qui ad nos descendit ut ascenderemus. Qui enim descendit, ipse est qui ascendit, etc. (Ephes. IV). Unde: Tecum descendam in Aegyptum, etc. (Genes. XLVI). Ideo, non erat in 435Btribubus eorum infirmus.

In manu Moysi et Aaron quas descripsit, etc. (ORIG., ibid. ) Non enim tantum scientiam legis et fidei, etc., usque ad unum enim opus utriusque manus est, et una perfectionis expletio.

VERS. 2.-- Moyses juxta castrorum loca, quae Domini jussione, etc. Et scripsit Moyses profectiones eorum et mansiones per verbum Domini, ut scilicet legentes quantae nobis immineant profectiones et mansiones, praeparemus nos ad hanc viam, nec segniter tempus nostrum consumamus: ne dum vanitatibus immoramur, et singulis quae ad visum, auditum, tactum, odoratum, gustumque veniunt, delectamur, praetereat tempus, nec spatium viae expleamus, sed in medio deficiamus quasi in deserto. 435CIdcirco enim venimus in hunc mundum, ut transeamus de virtute in virtutem, neque permaneamus pro terrenis in terra, sicut ille qui dicebat: Anima, habes multa bona: manduca, bibe et laetare (Luc. XII). Ait enim illi Dominus: Stulte, auferetur a te hac nocte anima tua (Ibid.). Non dixit in hac nocte, nec in hac die, sed hac nocte: nocte enim perimitur, sicut primogenita Aegyptiorum, quia dilexit mundum et tenebras ejus, socius rectorum mundi hujus, tenebrarum harum. Tenebrae autem et nox mundus iste dicitur, pro his qui in ignorantia vivunt, nec lumen veritatis recipiunt, et ideo de Ramesse ad Soccoth non transeunt.

VERS. 3.-- Profecti igitur, etc. Filii Israel adhuc in Aegypto positi quartadecima die fecerunt pascha, 435Det initium quoddam festivitatis. Sequenti ergo die, qui est primus azymorum, quinto decimo, scilicet primi mensis, profiscuntur de Ramesse, et veniunt in Soccoth, ut ibi faciant festivitates azymorum, vel diem. Quis haec intelligat? Quis vel ex parte cognoscat? sicut Apostolus dicit: Ex parte scimus, et ex parte prophetamus (I Cor. XIII). Quis enim intelligit quomodo ex parte dies festos agimus, ut nemo nos judicet in parte diei festi, aut neomeniae, aut sabbati? (Coloss. II.) Omnis enim dies festus, qui hic agitur, in parte geritur, non in integro: sed cum exieris de Aegypto, tunc erit tibi perfecta festivitas. Scito tamen quia post illud pascha, quod in Aegypto factum est semel, invenitur in deserto aliud 436Acelebratum cum lex data est: et post haec nusquam geri nisi in terra promissionis.

(ORIG.) Hic ordo et distinctio mansionum valde necessaria est, etc., usque ad proficiscitur ex Soccoth, et applicat in Ethan.

Manu excelsa. Ubi non est humanum opus, neque terrenum, sed divinum, ibi excelsa manus nominatur: per manum enim opus intelligitur.

VERS. 4.-- Et in diis eorum. Omnes dii gentium daemonia. In quibus faciet Dominus vindictam in die judicii. Facit et nunc, cum qui ab illis deceptus fuerat, ut idola coleret, per verbum Domini conversus Deum colit; vel cum fornicator ad pudicitiam convertitur, et se errasse deplorat, ipsius poenitentiae lacrymis uritur daemon; vel de superbia ad humilitatem, 436Bde luxuria ad parcimoniam. Quantis eos putas agi tormentis, si quem videant vendere omnia sua, et dare pauperibus, et tollere crucem suam, et sequi Christum: vel cum viderint fraudis suae nebulas per agnitionem divinae legis reserari? Non enim sine ipsis consummatur peccatum. Nobis ergo summopere agendum est, ne Aegyptiorum primogenita, vel deos eorum, quos Dominus exstinxit, resuscitemus in nobis, si dederimus eis locum operandi in nobis quae Dominus odit. Sic enim Dominus puniet deos Aegyptiorum de emendatione nostra et conversatione.

VERS. 5.-- In Soccoth. In tabernaculis. Dum enim peregrinamur in corpore, in tabernaculis habitamus. Cum exierimus de Aegypto, id est, de saeculo, 436Cprimum tabernacula figimus, scientes quia ad ulteriora, et ad terram sanctam, coelestem scilicet, properamus.

VERS. 6.-- De Soccoth. (ORIG., hom. 27.) Proficiscuntur ex Soccoth, etc., usque ad ut non ibi moretur, sed ut victoriam consequatur.

In Etham. Etham sana fortitudo, vel professio, in qua fortitudinem et robur assumimus, ne in via deficiamus.

(HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam. ) Quarta mansio est Phihahiroth, etc., usque ad falso sibi assumens vocabulum virtutis et dexterae, cum totus sit in sinistra.

VERS. 8.-- In Mara, etc. (ID., ibid.) Quinta mansio est Mara, etc., usque ad ne refugias ergo applicare 436Dad amaritudinem.

VERS. 9.-- In Elim ubi erant. Profectique de amaritudine venerunt in Elim, ubi sunt duodecim fontes aquarum, et septuaginta palmae erant. Vides post amaritudines et tentationum asperitates quam amoena te suscipiant loca! Non venisses ad palmas, nisi tentationum amaritudines pertulisses; nec ad dulcedinem fontium, nisi tristia et aspera superasses. Non quod in his sit finis itineris et perfectio cunctorum, sed dispensator animarum Deus interserit laboribus quaedam refrigeria, quibus recreata anima promptior reddatur ad aliquos labores. Elim interpretatur arietes: arietes sunt duces gregis, hi sunt apostoli duces gregis Christi, qui sunt etiam duodecim 437Afontes. Sed quia non solum illos elegit Christus, sed et alios septuaginta, ideo septuaginta arbores scribuntur esse palmarum, et ipsi enim apostoli nominantur; unde Paulus: Deinde apostolis omnibus. Haec te amoenitas post amaritudinem, haec requies post laborem, haec gratia suscipiat tentationem.

VERS. 10.-- Sed et inde egressi, etc., super mare Rubrum. Quia post apostolicam doctrinam et dulces fructus triumphorum, interdum apparent praeterita discrimina.

Profectique de Elim applicuerunt juxta mare Rubrum. Nota quia jam non intrant in mare, sufficit semel intrasse; sed applicant juxta, et mare quidem vident et undas, sed motus ejus et impetus non 437Btiment.

Mari Rubro, etc. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) Hebraice etc., usque ad postquam egressi sunt de Ramesse.

VERS. 11.-- In deserto Sin. (HIERON., ibid.) Profectique de mari Rubro castrametati sunt in deserto Sin, etc., usque ad quia plures desertae filii, quam ejus quae habet virum.

Profectique de mari Rubro applicaverunt in desertum Sin. Sin, rubus interpretatur vel tentatio: ibi jam incipit arridere bonorum spes, ubi de rubo apparuit Dominus, et responsa dedit Moysi, et initium visitationis filiis Israel. Solet enim in visionibus esse tentatio; solet enim angelus iniquitatis transfigurare se in angelum lucis, et ideo sollicite agendum est ut discernas visiones. Unde Jesus Nave, 437Ccum visiones videret, interrogat eum qui apparuit, Noster es, an adversariorum? Ita ergo proficiscens anima cum invenerit discretionem visionum probabitur spiritalis esse. Est enim inter dona Spiritus sancti discretio spirituum.

VERS. 12.-- In Daphca. Hebraice Daphquah, id est pulsatio. Post responsa Domini, post octavum numerum resurrectionis Christi, incipimus sacramenta pulsare.

(ORIG.) Profecti de deserto Sin, venerunt in Daphca, quae interpretatur sanitas. Nota ordinem profectuum. Ubi spiritalis efficitur anima, et discretionem habet visionum, pervenit ad sanitatem, ut merito dicat: Benedic, anima mea, Domino, qui sanat omnes languores tuos. Multi sunt languores animae, 437Davaritia scilicet, superbia, jactantia, formido, inconstantia et similia.

Castrametati sunt. (HIERON., ibid.) Decima mansio in Exodo non habetur, etc., usque ad et impleri Scripturam: Panem angelorum manducabit homo (Psal. LXXVII).

Profectique de Daphca veniunt in Alus, id est, labores. Nec mireris si sanitatem sequuntur labores, quia ideo sanatur anima, ut delectabiliter laboret, et dicatur ei: Labores manuum tuarum quia manducabis, beatus es et bene tibi erit (Psal. CXXVII).

VERS. 14.-- Raphidim. (HIERON., ibid.) Castrametati sunt in Raphidim, etc., usque ad solent daemonum tentamenta consurgere.

438A(ORIG.) Post haec veniunt in Raphidim, quae interpretatur laus judicii. Bene laus sequitur post labores, sed laus judicii: fit ergo digna laude anima, quae recte judicat et discernit, quae scilicet spiritualiter dijudicat omnia, et a nemine dijudicatur.

VERS. 15.-- In deserto Sinai. (HIER.) Sinai duodecima mansio. Statim tibi veniat in mentem apostolorum numerus. Una de pluribus, sed major omnibus, non separatur in ordine, praecellit in merito. Ad hanc quadragesima septima die perveniunt dicente Scriptura: Mense tertio egressionis filiorum Israel de Aegypto, in hac die transierunt in solitudinem Sinai (Exod. XIX), post pervenitur in desertum Sina.

Sina quidem locus deserti est, quae supra Sin nominavit: 438Bsed hic magis locus montis, qui in ipso deserto est, appellatur, qui ex vocabulo deserti Sina dicitur. Postquam ergo laudabilis judicii facta est anima, et rectum coepit habere judicium, datur ei lex a Deo, quia capax est divinorum secretorum, et coelestium visionum.

VERS. 16.-- Ad sepulcra concupiscentiae, etc. (HIERON.) Tertia decima mansio sepulcra concupiscentiae. Est autem sensus ille de Evangelio, quod Dominus Jesus baptizatus, statim a Spiritu deductus est in desertum, et tentabatur a diabolo (Matth. IV). Itaque Israel post familiarem cum Deo sermonem, postquam juxta montem Sinai commoratus est, anno uno et diebus quatuor, mira dispositione castrorum egressus est in solitudinem Pharan, quae interpretatur 438Conager, vel feritas; ibique succumbit malae bestiae fastidiens coelestem panem.

(ORIG.) Profecti de Sina, veniunt ad sepulcra concupiscentiae, ubi scilicet sepultae sunt et obrutae concupiscentiae, et exstincta omnis cupiditas, ne ultra concupiscat caro adversus spiritum, mortificata scilicet morte Christi.

VERS. 17.-- In Haseroth. (HIERON.) Quarta decima mansio in Haseroth, quae interpretatur atria. Ibi Aaron et Maria propter Aethiopissam contra Moysen murmurant, et in typum murmurantis contra Ecclesiam de gentibus congregatam populus Judaeorum lepra perfunditur, nec redit ad tabernaculum cum pristina sanitate, donec statutum plenitudinis gentium tempus compleatur.

438D(ORIG.) Post haec venitur in Haseroth, quod interpretatur atria perfecta vel beatitudo. Intuere, viator, diligentius quis sit ordo profectuum: postquam sepelieris et mortificaveris concupiscentias carnis, venies ad amplitudines atriorum, et venies ad beatitudinem. Beata est enim anima quae jam nullis vitiis carnis urgetur.

VERS. 18.-- In Rethma. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) Castrametati sunt in Rethma, etc., usque ad et claro sonitu Evangelium praedicemus.

(ORIG.) Inde venitur in Rethma sive Pharan. Rethma visio consummata interpretatur; Pharan vero os visibile. Quid, nisi ita crescat anima, ut cum desierit molestiis carnis urgeri, visiones habeat 439Aconsummatas, et rerum perfectam capiat intelligentiam? causas scilicet incarnationis verbi Dei, et rationes dispensationum ejus altius cognoscens.

VERS. 19.-- In Remmomphares. (HIERON.) Mali Punici divisio, in quo significatur Ecclesia quasi multa grana uno cortice contegi: dum omnem turbam credentium in fidei unitatem concludit. Vel varietas et consonantia virtutum, unde: Multitudinis credentium erat cor unum et anima una (Act. IV); sicque divisi sunt singuli gradus, ut omnes eadem compage teneantur.

(ORIG.) Inde venitur in Remmomphares, ubi scilicet divitiarum et coelestium rerum a terrenis et infimis discretio fit, crescente enim intellectu animae, notitia ei excelsorum praebetur, et judicium 439Bdatur quo sciat a temporalibus aeterna, et a perpetuis caduca separare.

VERS. 20.-- In Lebna, etc. Laterem scilicet. In hoc enim transitu nunc crescimus, nunc decrescimus, et post multos profectus saepe ad laterem, id est carnalia opera redimus. Post haec venitur in Lebna, quod interpretatur dealbatio. Scio in aliis dealbationem culpabiliter poni, ut cum dicitur paries dealbatus, et monumenta dealbata. Hic autem dealbatio est de qua dicitur: Lavabis me et super nivem dealbabor (Psal. L); et, si fuerint peccata vestra sicut Phoenicium, ut nivem dealbabo. Et alibi, Nive dealbabuntur in Selmon. Et vetusti dierum capilli dicuntur esse candidi, id est, albi sicut lana. Haec igitur dealbatio ex splendore verae lucis intelligitur 439Cprovenire, et ex visionum coelestium claritate descendere.

VERS. 21.-- In Ressa. Frena scilicet. Si enim ad lutulenta opera descendimus, infrenandi sumus, et cursu vagi atque praecipites Scripturarum retinaculis dirigendi.

(ORIG.) Post haec fit mansio in Ressa, etc., usque ad ergo est visibilis vel laudabilis tentatio.

VERS. 23.-- In monte Sepher. (HIERON.) Pulchritudinis. Mons autem pulchritudinis est Christus. Vicesima mansio in monte pulchritudinis constituta est, de qua dicitur: Domine, quis habitabit in tabernaculo tuo? etc. (Psal. XIV). Vides quid prosint frena: a vitiis nos retrahunt, ad virtutum choros introducunt, 439Det in Christo monte pulcherrimo habitare faciunt.

(ORIG.) Alia littera. Inde venitur in montem Sepher, quod tubicinatio dicitur. Tuba signum belli est, ubi enim se tantis ac talibus virtutibus armatam sentit anima, necessario procedit ad bellum, quod est contra principatus et potestates, et hujus mundi rectores. Vel tuba canit in verbo Dei doctrinae scilicet et praedicationis: ut qui audierit tubam, praeparare se possit ad bellum.

VERS. 24.-- In Arada. (HIERON.) Miraculum. Et nota ordinem, post frenos in Ecclesiam intromittimur, inde ad montem Christi ascendimus, in quo positi miramur quae nec oculus vidit, nec auris audivit.

440A(ORIG.) De monte Sepher venerunt in Arada, quod interpretatur idoneus effectus, ut scilicet dicat: Idoneos nos fecit ministros Novi Testamenti (I Cor. II).

VERS. 25.-- Maceloth. Coetus; haec est Ecclesia de qua dicitur: Ecce quam bonum et quam jucundum, habitare, fratres, in unum (I Cor. III). Vel consonantia omnium virtutum.

(ORIG.) Inde venitur ad Maceloth, quod interpretatur ab initio. Contemplatur enim qui ad perfectionem tendit initium rerum, imo cuncta ad eum refert qui erat in principio apud Deum, nec ab isto unquam recedit initio.

(HIER.) Ex hac venitur in Maceloth, quod est principatus vel virgae, utroque potestas indicatur, et quod anima eousque profecerit, ut dominetur corpori 440Bquasi tenens virgam potestatis, imo toti mundo, cum dicit: Mihi mundus crucifixus est, etc. (Gal. VI).

VERS. 26.-- Thahath. Pavor, qui est custos beatitudinis, ut qui tantum ascendit, non superbiat; quasi: Venisti ad ecclesiam, ascendisti ad montem pulcherrimum, stupore et miraculo tuo Christi magnitudinem confiteris. Vides ibi multos virtutis socios: Noli altum sapere, sed sapere ad sobrietatem [Rom. XII]. Timor virtutum custos, securitas labilis, unde: Virga tua et baculus tuus, ipsa me consolata sunt (Psal. XII). Hoc est dum tormenta formido, gratiam acceptam servo.

(Orig.) Post haec fit mansio in Thabath, quod est confirmatio vel patientia. Necesse est enim eum qui vult aliis prodesse, multa pati, et cuncta patienter 440Cferre; unde: Ego enim ostendam ei quanta oporteat eum pati pro nomine meo (Act. IX).

VERS. 27.-- Castrametati sunt in Thare. (HIERON.) Id est malitia, etc., usque ad et Elias a corvis pascitur.

(ORIG.) Inde venitur ad Thare, quod est contemplatio stuporis, id est extasis cum alicujus magnae rei stupescit animus admiratione.

(HIERON.) Thare malitia vel pastura, quod ad praepositos Ecclesiae, vel ad custodiam animae refertur, ut sollicitus sit qui pascit, ne diabolus in caulas ovium, id est ecclesias aliquo vitiorum foramine se ingerat.

VERS. 28.-- Methcha. (ID.) Dulcedo. Quasi: ascendisti 440Din excelsum montem patris, et miratus es virtutum choros, timuisti ruinam, abjecisti insidiatores, ideo dulcis te fructus laboris insequitur, ut dicat Psalmista: Quam dulcia faucibus meis eloquia tua (Psal. CXVIII)! Samson, qui abegerat a fructibus suis aves, et vulpes quae exterminant vineas colligaverat, leonem quoque interfecerat rugientem, favum invenit in ore mortui.

Post haec venerunt ad Methcha, quod interpretatur mors nova: mors autem nova est, quando Christo commorimur et consepelimur, ut convivamus ei.

VERS. 29.-- Hesmona. Quae interpretatur festinatio, quia post dulces fructus laboris non debemus 441Aesse otiosi, sed obliti praeteritorum in futura nos extendamus.

(ORIG.) Inde ad Seman venitur, quod os, vel ossa significare dicitur, in quo virtus et robur patientiae declaratur.

VERS. 30.-- Moseroth. (HIERON.) Vincula, vel disciplina. Ut magistrorum teramus limina; et praecepta virtutum, ac mysteria Scripturarum, vincula putemus esse aeterna contra diaboli potestatem et vincula: quibus diruptis vicit hostes Samson. De quibus dicitur: Dirupisti vincula mea (Psal. CXV). Qui vinculo Christi fuerit ligatus, dicet: Sinistra ejus sub capite meo et dextera ejus amplexabitur me (Cantic. II).

(ORIG.) Inde ad Moseroth, quod est excludens, ut 441Bjam excludat suggestiones contrarii spiritus de cogitationibus suis; unde: Si spiritus potestatem habenti ascenderit super te, locum tuum ne dimittas (Eccli. X). Tenendus est ergo locus, et excludendus adversarius, ne inveniat locum in corde nostro, unde: Nolite locum dare diabolo (Ephes. IV).

VERS. 32.-- Benejaacan venerunt, etc. (HIERON.) Filii necessitatis vel stridoris vicesima octava mansio. Nota numerum, quia si ab uno incipias, et paulatim addens ad septimum usque pervenias, vicesimus octavus efficitur.

(ID.) Benejaacan interpretatur filii necessitatis vel stridoris. De his filiis dicitur: Afferte Domino filios arietum (Psal. XXVIII). Cum autem divinis Scripturis fueris eruditus, et leges earum et testimonia 441Cscieris esse vincula charitatis, et contendens cum adversariis ligabis eos, et vinctos duces in captivitatem, et de hostibus et captivis efficies liberos Dei; et filii stridoris timore supplicii, ubi est fletus et stridor dentium, deserentes vincula diaboli, Christo colla submittent.

(ORIG.) Post haec venitur ad Benejaacan, quod significat fontes vel excolationes, ubi scilicet divinorum verborum fontes haurit, usquequo excolet eos bibendo, ut nec minimum quidem mandatum praetereat, imo nec unum iota, aut unus apex de verbo Dei in intellectu ejus habeatur otiosus.

Montem. (HIERON.) Gadgad vel Galgath, interpretatur nuntius, vel actio, vel concisio. Non enim possumus facere filios necessitatis, nisi praeceptores 441Deorum interfecerimus, nec parcat manus nostra armum aut extremum auriculae extrahere de ore leonis, et nuntiaverimus praemia futura, et accinctos esse in rebellando docuerimus; unde: Maledictus qui facit opus Dei negligenter et prohibet gladium suum a sanguine (Jerem. XLVIII). Et David: In matutino interficiebam omnes peccatores terrae (Psal. C).

(ORIG.) Post haec venit in Gadgad, quod interpretatur tentamentum, vel constipatio. Fortitudo quaedam in Deo et munimen, est animae tentamentum. Ita enim virtutibus admiscetur, ut videantur usque eo nec decorae esse nec plenae, et ideo proficiscentibus ad virtutem variae et frequentes mansiones in tentationibus fiunt. Quas cum transieris, applicabis 442Ain Jetebatha: Jetebatha interpretatur bona; ad bonum ergo non nisi post tentationum experimenta venitur.

Jetebathae. (HIERON.) Bonitas, quae est Christus: ut cum venerimus ad perfectum virum, sacerdotalem gradum, et aetatem plenitudinis Christi, in qua Ezechiel erat juxta fluvium Chobar (Ezech. I), possimus cum David in psalmo canere: Domine, in te speravi non confundar in aeternum (Psal. XXX). Pastor enim, bonus ponit animam suam pro ovibus suis. (Joan. X.)

VERS. 34.-- Hebrona. (ID.) Transitus, id est mundus. Praeterit enim figura hujus mundi, in quem venientes sancti cupiunt ad meliora transire. Ad hanc mansionem venit verus Hebraeus, id est transitor, qui dicere potest: Transiens, videbo visionem hanc 442Bmagnam (Exod. III); unde: Et non dixerunt qui praeteribant, Benedictio Domini super vos. (Psal. CXXVIII.)

(ORIG.) Inde applicuerunt in Hebrona, quod est transitus: transeunda enim sunt omnia, quod etiam si ad bona venias, oportet te ad meliora transire, usquequo ad illud bonum venias, in quo semper debeas permanere.

VERS. 35.-- In Asiongaber. (HIER.) Asiongaber ligna viri. Quia scilicet saltuum et omnium arborum genera possunt multitudinem gentium figurare. Hucusque solitudo Pharan decimas octavas continet mansiones, quae descriptae in catalogo in superiori itinere non ponuntur.

(ORIG.) Post haec venitur ad Asiongaber, quod interpretatur 442Cconsilia viri. Si quis desiit puer esse sensibus, pervenit ad consilia viri, sicut ille qui dicebat: Quando autem factus sum vir, evacuavi quae erant parvuli (I Cor. XIII). Sunt ergo magna consilia viri; unde: Aqua alta consilium in corde viri. (Prov. XX.)

VERS. 36.-- In desertum, etc. (HIERON.). Quaeritur, etc., usque ad de hac mansione dicitur: Commovebit Dominus desertum Cades.

(ORIG.) Hinc iterum Sin, id est tentatio, quia nec aliter expedit hoc iter agere. Aurifex enim vas necessarium facere volens frequenter admovet igni, subdit malleis, rasoriis perstringit, ut purgatius fiat, et ad speciem, quam prospicit artifex, perveniat.

(ID.) Post haec venerunt ad Pharan Cades, etc., 442Dusque ad Alius enim in valle, alius in campis, alius moritur in monte, alius in monte montis.

(ID.) Inde applicatur in monte Hor, quod interpretatur montanus. Venit enim ad montem Dei, ut fiat mons uber, mons coagulatus. Vel ab eo quod semper in monte Dei habitet, dicatur montanus.

VERS. 38.-- Aaron sacerdos in, etc. (ISID.) Eodem anno mortuus est Aaron, quo novus populus terram promissionis intraturus erat. Et quanquam in monte, Eleazaro sacerdotium dereliquerit, et lex eos qui eam impleverint perducat ad summum; tamen ipsa sublimitas non est trans fluenta Jordanis, sed in extremis terrenorum operum finibus. Et plangit eum populus triginta diebus: Aaron, plangitur, 443AJesus non plangitur; in lege, descensus ad inferos; in Evangelio, ad paradisum ascensio.

VERS. 40.-- Audivitque. (ISID.) Audivit quoque Chananaeus quod venisset Israel, et in loco exploratorum, ubi quondam populum offendisse noverat, iniit praelium et captivum duxit Israel. Rursum in eodem loco expugnatur, ex voto victor vincitur, superant victi. Per quod intelligimus, ut cum nos auxilio Dei destitutos hostes captivarint, non desperemus salutem, sed iterum pugnemus. Potest fieri ut vincamus, ubi victi sumus. Appellaturque nomen loci illius Horma, id est anathema.

VERS. 41.-- In salmona. (HIERON.) Castrametati sunt in Salmona, etc., usque ad manna fastidiunt, a serpentibus vulnerantur.

443B(ORIG.) Sequitur, etc., usque ad Christus est, et Spiritus sanctus.

(HIERON.) Salmona imaguncula, quia ibi expressa est imago Salvatoris per serpentem aeneum qui in ligno pependit.

VERS. 42.-- Phunon. Os, quia cum passionem Filii Dei cognoscimus, quod corde credimus, ore pronuntiamus, secundum illud: Corde creditur ad justitiam, etc. (Rom. X)

(ORIG.) Hinc venitur ad Phunon, quod putamus esse oris parcimoniam; qui enim potuerit mysterium Christi, et Spiritus sancti intueri, et viderit vel audierit quae non licet homini loqui, necessario habebit oris parcimoniam, sciens quibus, quando vel quomodo de mysteriis divinis oporteat loqui.

443CVERS. 43.-- Oboth. (HIERON.) Magi vel pythones, quia post imaginem Dei quae in corde ratione monstratur, et confessionem fidei quae ore profertur, consurgunt serpentes, et maleficae artes ad bella nos provocant. Sed omni custodia servantes cor nostrum, obturemus aures nostras ne audiamus voces incantantium et carmina sirenarum.

(ORIG.) Post hoc venitur in Oboth. Cujus nominis quamvis non invenerimus interpretationem, non dubitamus sicut in caeteris, et in hoc consequentiam profectuum conservari.

VERS. 44.-- In finibus. Significat secundum litteram, quod hucusque fuerunt in finibus Idumaeorum, et nunc veniunt ad terminos Moab.

(ORIG.) Sequitur post haec mansio Gai, id est 443DChaos: appropriat enim per hoc profectus ad sinum Abrahae, qui ait: Inter nos et vos magnum chaos firmatum est (Luc. XVI), ut cum Lazaro requiescat.

In Dibongad. (HIERON.) Dibongad, etc., usque ad sed econtrario solitudinem nobis propositam noverimus.

(ORIG.) Inde venitur iterum ad Dibongad, etc., usque ad quam perfecte et pie de Deo sentiat cognoscatur.

VERS. 46.-- Helmondeblathaim. (HIERON.) Quod interpretatur, etc., usque ad vix populi precibus liberatum.

(ORIG.) Post haec venitur in Helmondeblathaim, quod interpretatur contemptus ficuum, id est ubi 444Acontemnantur penitus terrena: nisi enim contempta fuerint, quae delectare videntur in terris, ad coelestia transire non possumus.

VERS. 47.-- Venerunt ad montem Abarim contra Nabo, etc. (HIERON.) Quadragesima prima mansio vertitur in montes transeuntium, et est contra faciem montis Nabo.

(ORIG.) Sequitur mansio Abarim, quod est transitus contra Naban, quod est abscessio. Ubi enim per has omnes virtutes anima transierit, et ad summum perfectionis ascenderit, transit jam de saeculo, et abscedit sicut Henoch, qui non inveniebatur, quia transtulit illum Deus. Quod si videatur adhuc in saeculo esse, et in carne habitare, tamen non invenitur in ullo saeculari actu, quia transtulit illum Deus 444Bin regionem virtutum.

(HIERON.) Hic moritur Moyses, terra promissionis ante conspecta. Nabo interpretatur conclusio, in qua finitur lex, et non invenitur ejus memoria, sed gratia Evangelii sine fine perseverat. Et notandum quod mansio transeuntium in montibus sita est, et adhuc profectu indiget: post montana enim ad campestria Moab et Jordanis fluenta descendimus, qui interpretatur descensio. Nihil enim tam periculosum quam gloriae cupiditas et jactantia, et animus conscientia virtutum tumidus.

VERS. 48.-- Ad campestria Moab. (ID.) In quadragesima secunda mansione, etc., usque ad et spinas quae suffocaverunt sementem verbi Dei de quibus dicitur: Versatus sum in miseria, dum mihi configitur 444Cspina (Psal. XXXI).

(ORIG.) Ultima mansio est, etc., usque ad profectum mentis indicant et incrementa virtutum.

VERS. 51.-- Transieritis Jordanem, id est cum baptizati fueritis, quasi, cum deposito veteri homine cum actibus suis, indueritis novum (Col. III), qui renovatur in agnitione Dei.

VERS. 52.-- Disperdite cunctos. Cum terram sanctam repromissionis per fidem ingredimur, simul cum idololatria etiam omnia vitia exstinguamus.

Habitatores. Malignos spiritus, phantasmata vitiorum, cum suggestionibus suis; unde: Beatus qui tenebit et allidet parvulos suos ad petram (Psal. CXXXVI).

VERS. 54.-- Pluribus dabitis latiorem. Quia qui 444Dhabent copiam virtutum, latitudinem sperant praemiorum: qui autem paucitate virtutum contenti sunt, secundum meritum suum retributionem percipiunt; unde: Qui parce seminat, parce et metet; et qui seminat in benedictionibus, de benedictionibus, etc. (II Cor. IX).

CHAPTER XXXIII.

  • 435A

VERS. 1.-- These are the stations, etc. (ORIG., hom. 27 in Num.) God created divers differences of foods, etc., as far as but another, by which, being about to ascend to the heavens after the resurrection, she does not come down suddenly nor unseasonably, but through many stations, in each of which, enlightened by the light of wisdom, she may come to the Father of lights himself.

By their troops. The Septuagint thus: With his own power, Christ, namely, who is the power of God and the might of God. With him therefore is the ascent made, who came down to us that we might ascend. For he that descended, the same is he that ascended, etc. (Ephes. IV). Whence: I will go down with thee into Egypt, etc. (Genes. XLVI). Therefore, there was not among 435Btheir tribes one that was feeble.

In the hand of Moses and Aaron, which he described, etc. (ORIG., ibid. ) For not only the knowledge of the law and of faith, etc., as far as for the work of both hands is one, and the fulfilling of perfection is one.

VERS. 2.-- Moses according to the places of the camps, which by the Lord's command, etc. And Moses wrote their goings forth and their stations by the word of the Lord, so that we, reading how many goings forth and stations hang over us, may prepare ourselves for this way, and may not sluggishly waste our time: lest, while we linger among vanities and are delighted with the several things which come to sight, hearing, touch, smell and taste, the time pass by, and we fulfil not the space of the way, but fail in the middle as it were in the desert. 435CFor to this end we came into this world, that we might pass from virtue to virtue, and not remain on earth for the sake of earthly things, like him who said: Soul, thou hast many goods: eat, drink and be merry (Luc. XII) [Vulg. Lc 12:19 Anima, habes multa bona posita in annos plurimos: requiesce, comede, bibe, epulare, "laid up for many years: take thy rest, eat, drink, make good cheer"]. For the Lord said to him: Fool, this night shall thy soul be taken away from thee (Ibid.) [Vulg. Lc 12:20 Stulte, hac nocte animam tuam repetunt a te, "this night do they require thy soul of thee"]. He did not say in this night, nor in this day, but this night: for it is by night that a man is destroyed, like the firstborn of the Egyptians, because he loved the world and its darkness, a companion of the rulers of this world, of these darknesses. But this world is called darkness and night, for those who live in ignorance, and receive not the light of truth, and therefore pass not over from Rameses to Soccoth.

VERS. 3.-- They set forward therefore, etc. The children of Israel, being as yet placed in Egypt, kept the pasch on the fourteenth day, 435Dand a certain beginning of the festival. On the following day therefore, which is the first of the unleavened bread, on the fifteenth, namely, of the first month, they set forward from Rameses, and come into Soccoth, that there they may keep the festivals of unleavened bread, or the day. Who may understand these things? Who may know them even in part? as the Apostle says: We know in part, and we prophesy in part (I Cor. XIII) [Vulg. 1 Cor 13:9 Ex parte enim cognoscimus, "for we know in part"]. For who understands how we keep festival days in part, so that no man judge us in part of a festival day, or of the new moon, or of the sabbath? (Coloss. II.) [Vulg. Col 2:16 Nemo ergo vos judicet in cibo, aut in potu, aut in parte diei festi, aut neomeniae, aut sabbatorum, "let no man therefore judge you in meat or in drink … or of the sabbaths"] For every festival day which is kept here is carried on in part, not in the whole: but when thou shalt have gone out of Egypt, then shall there be for thee a perfect festival. Yet know that after that pasch which was made in Egypt once, another is found 436Acelebrated in the desert when the law was given: and after these things, that it was nowhere kept save in the land of promise.

(ORIG.) This order and distinction of the stations is very necessary, etc., as far as he sets forward from Soccoth, and comes to Ethan.

With a high hand. Where there is no human work, nor earthly, but divine, there a high hand is named: for by the hand the work is understood.

VERS. 4.-- And on their gods. All the gods of the Gentiles are demons. On whom the Lord will take vengeance in the day of judgment. He does so even now, when he who had been deceived by them, so as to worship idols, being converted through the word of the Lord worships God; or when a fornicator is converted to chastity, and bewails that he has erred, the demon is burned by the tears of that very penitence; or from pride to humility, 436Bfrom luxury to frugality. With how great torments dost thou think they are driven, if they should see anyone sell all that he has, and give to the poor, and take up his cross, and follow Christ: or when they shall have seen the clouds of their fraud unlocked by the acknowledgment of the divine law? For sin is not accomplished without them. We must therefore act with all our might, lest we raise up again in ourselves the firstborn of the Egyptians, or their gods, whom the Lord destroyed, if we give them place to work in us the things which the Lord hates. For so the Lord will punish the gods of the Egyptians by our amendment and conversation.

VERS. 5.-- At Soccoth. In tabernacles. For while we are pilgrims in the body, we dwell in tabernacles. When we shall have gone out of Egypt, that is, out of the world, 436Cwe first pitch tabernacles, knowing that we hasten to things beyond, and to the holy land, the heavenly, namely.

VERS. 6.-- From Soccoth. (ORIG., hom. 27.) They set forward from Soccoth, etc., as far as that he tarry not there, but that he obtain the victory.

At Etham. Etham, sound strength, or profession, in which we take up strength and vigour, lest we fail in the way.

(HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam. ) The fourth station is Phihahiroth, etc., as far as falsely taking to himself the name of strength and of the right hand, when he is wholly on the left.

VERS. 8.-- At Mara, etc. (ID., ibid.) The fifth station is Mara, etc., as far as do not therefore refuse to draw near 436Dto bitterness.

VERS. 9.-- At Elim where there were. And setting forward from bitterness they came into Elim, where there are twelve fountains of waters, and seventy palm trees were. Thou seest after bitternesses and the harshnesses of temptations what pleasant places receive thee! Thou wouldst not have come to the palms, hadst thou not endured the bitternesses of temptations; nor to the sweetness of the fountains, hadst thou not overcome things sad and harsh. Not that in these is the end of the journey and the perfection of all things, but God the dispenser of souls interposes among the labours certain refreshments, by which the soul, being restored, may be rendered readier for some labours. Elim is interpreted rams: rams are the leaders of the flock, these are the apostles, leaders of the flock of Christ, who are also the twelve 437Afountains. But because Christ chose not only them, but also other seventy, therefore seventy trees are written to be of palms, for they too are called apostles; whence Paul: Then to all the apostles. May this pleasantness after bitterness receive thee, this rest after labour, this grace the temptation.

VERS. 10.-- But going out from thence also, etc., upon the Red Sea. Because after the apostolic teaching and the sweet fruits of the triumphs, past perils sometimes appear.

And setting forward from Elim they came up beside the Red Sea. Note that they now enter not into the sea; it is enough to have entered once; but they come up beside it, and they see indeed the sea and the waves, but they fear not its motions and its assault. 437B

From the Red Sea, etc. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) In Hebrew etc., as far as after they were gone out from Rameses.

VERS. 11.-- In the desert of Sin. (HIERON., ibid.) And setting forward from the Red Sea they pitched their camp in the desert of Sin, etc., as far as because the children of the desolate are more than of her that hath a husband.

And setting forward from the Red Sea they came to the desert of Sin. Sin is interpreted a bush, or a temptation: there already the hope of good things begins to smile, where the Lord appeared out of the bush, and gave answers to Moses, and the beginning of the visitation to the children of Israel. For in visions there is wont to be temptation; for the angel of iniquity is wont to transfigure himself into an angel of light, and therefore it must be carefully seen to that thou discern the visions. Whence Josue the son of Nun, 437Cwhen he saw visions, asks him who appeared, Art thou one of ours, or of our adversaries? Thus therefore the soul, setting forward, when it shall have found the discernment of visions, will be proved to be spiritual. For among the gifts of the Holy Spirit is the discerning of spirits.

VERS. 12.-- At Daphca. In Hebrew Daphquah, [Migne's plate sets the Hebrew word for Daphca at this point, on both printings; our digitized source has lost it, and it is not supplied here.] that is, a knocking. After the answers of the Lord, after the eighth number of the resurrection of Christ, we begin to knock at the sacraments.

(ORIG.) Setting forward from the desert of Sin, they came into Daphca, which is interpreted health. Note the order of the goings forward. Where the soul is made spiritual, and has the discernment of visions, it comes to health, so that it may deservedly say: Bless the Lord, O my soul, who healeth all thy diseases. [Vulg. Ps 102:3 qui sanat omnes infirmitates tuas, "who healeth all thy infirmities"] Many are the diseases of the soul, 437Dnamely covetousness, pride, boasting, dread, inconstancy and the like.

They pitched their camp. (HIERON., ibid.) The tenth station is not had in Exodus, etc., as far as and that the Scripture be fulfilled: Man shall eat the bread of angels (Psal. LXXVII) [Vulg. Ps 77:25 manducavit, "man ate"].

And setting forward from Daphca they come into Alus, that is, labours. And wonder not if labours follow health, because the soul is therefore healed that it may labour delightfully, and that it may be said to it: For thou shalt eat the labours of thy hands, blessed art thou and it shall be well with thee (Psal. CXXVII).

VERS. 14.-- Raphidim. (HIERON., ibid.) They pitched their camp at Raphidim, etc., as far as the temptations of demons are wont to rise up.

438A(ORIG.) After these things they come into Raphidim, which is interpreted the praise of judgment. Well does praise follow after labours, but the praise of judgment: the soul therefore becomes worthy of praise which judges rightly and discerns, which namely judges all things spiritually, and is judged of no man.

VERS. 15.-- In the desert of Sinai. (HIER.) Sinai is the twelfth station. Let the number of the apostles come at once into thy mind. One out of many, but greater than all, it is not separated in order, it excels in merit. To this they come on the forty-seventh day, the Scripture saying: In the third month of the going out of the children of Israel from Egypt, on this day they passed over into the wilderness of Sinai (Exod. XIX) [Vulg. Ex 19:1 Mense tertio egressionis Israel de terra Aegypti, in die hac venerunt in solitudinem Sinai, "of the going out of Israel from the land of Egypt … they came"], and afterwards one comes into the desert of Sina.

Sina indeed is a place of the desert, which above he named Sin: 438Bbut here rather the place of the mountain, which is in that very desert, is so called, which from the name of the desert is called Sina. After therefore the soul has been made of praiseworthy judgment, and has begun to have a right judgment, a law is given her by God, because she is capable of divine secrets and of heavenly visions.

VERS. 16.-- To the graves of lust, etc. (HIERON.) The thirteenth station is the graves of lust. But that sense is from the Gospel, that the Lord Jesus, being baptized, was straightway led by the Spirit into the desert, and was tempted by the devil (Matth. IV). And so Israel, after the familiar discourse with God, after it had stayed beside mount Sinai for one year and four days, with a wonderful ordering of the camps went out into the wilderness of Pharan, which is interpreted 438Ca wild ass, or wildness; and there, loathing the heavenly bread, it succumbs to the evil beast.

(ORIG.) Setting forward from Sina, they come to the graves of lust, where namely lusts are buried and overwhelmed, and all cupidity extinguished, that the flesh may no more lust against the spirit, being mortified, namely, by the death of Christ.

VERS. 17.-- At Haseroth. (HIERON.) The fourteenth station is in Haseroth, which is interpreted courts. There Aaron and Mary murmur against Moses because of the Ethiopian woman, and, as a type of one murmuring against the Church gathered out of the Gentiles, the people of the Jews is overspread with leprosy, and returns not to the tabernacle with its former soundness, until the appointed time of the fulness of the Gentiles be accomplished.

438D(ORIG.) After these things one comes into Haseroth, which is interpreted perfect courts or blessedness. Look, O traveller, more diligently what is the order of the goings forward: after thou shalt have buried and mortified the concupiscences of the flesh, thou shalt come to the breadths of the courts, and thou shalt come to blessedness. For blessed is the soul which is now urged by no vices of the flesh.

VERS. 18.-- At Rethma. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) They pitched their camp at Rethma, etc., as far as and let us preach the Gospel with a clear sound.

(ORIG.) Thence one comes to Rethma or Pharan. Rethma is interpreted a vision completed; Pharan, a visible mouth. What is this, unless that the soul so grow that, when it has ceased to be pressed by the troubles of the flesh, it may have visions 439Acompleted, and receive a perfect understanding of things? knowing, that is, more deeply the causes of the incarnation of the Word of God, and the reasons of his dispensations.

VERS. 19.-- At Remmomphares. (HIERON.) The division of a pomegranate, in which is signified that the Church, like many grains, is covered by one rind: while it shuts up the whole crowd of believers into the unity of faith. Or the variety and concord of the virtues, whence: The multitude of believers had one heart and one soul (Act. IV); and thus the several ranks are divided, that all may be held together in the same framework.

(ORIG.) Thence one comes to Remmomphares, where, that is, the discernment of riches and of heavenly things from earthly and lowest things is made, for as the understanding of the soul grows, knowledge of high things is afforded it, and judgment 439Bis given whereby it may know how to separate eternal things from temporal, and things that fall from things that endure.

VERS. 20.-- At Lebna, etc. A brick, namely. For in this passage we now grow, now decrease, and after many advances we often return to the brick, that is, to carnal works. After these things one comes to Lebna, which is interpreted a whitening. I know that in other places a whitening is set down blameworthily, as when a wall is called whited, and sepulchres whited. But here the whitening is that of which it is said: Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow (Psal. L); and, if your sins be as Phoenician purple, I will whiten them as snow. [Vulg. Is. 1:18 ut coccinum, quasi nix dealbabuntur, "as scarlet, they shall be made white as snow"] And elsewhere, They shall be whitened with snow in Selmon. And the hairs of the Ancient of days are said to be gleaming white, that is, white as wool. This whitening therefore is understood 439Cto come forth from the splendour of the true light, and to descend from the brightness of heavenly visions.

VERS. 21.-- At Ressa. Bridles, namely. For if we go down to muddy works, we must be bridled, and, wandering and headlong in our course, must be guided by the reins of the Scriptures.

(ORIG.) After these things the station is made at Ressa, etc., as far as it is therefore a visible or a praiseworthy temptation.

VERS. 23.-- At mount Sepher. (HIERON.) Of beauty. But the mountain of beauty is Christ. The twentieth station is set upon the mountain of beauty, of which it is said: Lord, who shall dwell in thy tabernacle? etc. (Psal. XIV). Thou seest what bridles avail: they draw us back from vices, they bring us in to the choirs of the virtues, 439Dand they make us dwell in Christ, the most beautiful mountain.

(ORIG.) Another reading. Thence one comes to mount Sepher, which is called a trumpet-blowing. The trumpet is the sign of war, for when the soul feels itself armed with virtues so many and so great, it necessarily goes forth to the war which is against principalities and powers, and the rulers of this world. Or the trumpet sounds in the word of God, that is, of teaching and of preaching: that he who has heard the trumpet may be able to prepare himself for war.

VERS. 24.-- At Arada. (HIERON.) A wonder. And note the order, after the bridles we are brought into the Church, thence we ascend to the mountain of Christ, and set upon it we wonder at the things which neither eye has seen nor ear has heard.

440A(ORIG.) From mount Sepher they came to Arada, which is interpreted a fit result, so that he says, namely: He hath made us fit ministers of the New Testament (I Cor. II).

VERS. 25.-- Maceloth. An assembly; this is the Church of which it is said: Behold how good and how pleasant it is, brethren, to dwell in one (I Cor. III). Or the concord of all the virtues.

(ORIG.) Thence one comes to Maceloth, which is interpreted from the beginning. For he who tends toward perfection contemplates the beginning of things, nay rather he refers all things to him who was in the beginning with God, nor does he ever depart from that beginning.

(HIER.) From this one comes to Maceloth, which is a principality or rods, by either of them power is signified, and that the soul has so far advanced that it rules over the body 440Bas though holding the rod of power, nay rather over the whole world, when it says: The world is crucified to me, etc. (Gal. VI).

VERS. 26.-- Thahath. Dread, which is the guardian of blessedness, that he who has ascended so far may not be proud; as if to say: Thou hast come to the church, thou hast ascended to the most beautiful mountain, by thy amazement and wonder thou confessest the greatness of Christ. Thou seest there many companions of virtue: Be not high-minded, but be wise unto sobriety [Rom. XII]. Fear is the guardian of the virtues, security is slippery, whence: Thy rod and thy staff, they have comforted me (Psal. XII). That is, while I dread torments, I keep the grace I have received.

(Orig.) After these things the station is made at Thabath, which is confirmation or patience. For it is necessary that he who wishes to profit others should suffer many things, and patiently 440Cbear all things; whence: For I will show him how great things he must suffer for my name (Act. IX).

VERS. 27.-- They pitched their camp at Thare. (HIERON.) That is, malice, etc., as far as and Elias is fed by ravens.

(ORIG.) Thence one comes to Thare, which is the contemplation of amazement, that is, ecstasy, when the mind is stupefied with wonder at some great thing.

(HIERON.) Thare is malice or pasturage, which is referred to the prelates of the Church, or to the guarding of the soul, that he who feeds may be watchful, lest the devil thrust himself into the sheepfolds, that is, the churches, through some hole of the vices.

VERS. 28.-- Methcha. (ID.) Sweetness. As if to say: thou hast ascended 440Dinto the high mountain of the father, and hast wondered at the choirs of the virtues, thou hast feared ruin, thou hast cast off the liers in wait, therefore the sweet fruit of thy labour follows thee, so that the Psalmist says: How sweet are thy words to my palate (Psal. CXVIII)! Samson, who had driven the birds away from his fruits, and had bound together the foxes that lay waste the vineyards, and had also slain the roaring lion, found a honeycomb in the mouth of the dead one.

After these things they came to Methcha, which is interpreted a new death: but a new death is when we die together with Christ and are buried together with him, that we may live together with him.

VERS. 29.-- Hesmona. Which is interpreted haste, because after the sweet fruits of labour we ought not 441Ato be idle, but, forgetting the things that are past, let us stretch ourselves forth to the things that are to come.

(ORIG.) Thence one comes to Seman, which is said to signify a bone, or bones, in which the strength and vigour of patience is declared.

VERS. 30.-- Moseroth. (HIERON.) Bonds, or discipline. That we may wear the thresholds of our masters; and may think the precepts of the virtues, and the mysteries of the Scriptures, to be eternal bonds against the power and the bonds of the devil: which being burst, Samson overcame his enemies. Of which it is said: Thou hast broken my bonds (Psal. CXV). He who has been bound with the bond of Christ will say: His left hand is under my head, and his right hand shall embrace me (Cantic. II).

(ORIG.) Thence to Moseroth, which is that which shuts out, that 441Bit may now shut out from its own thoughts the suggestions of the adverse spirit; whence: If a spirit shall rise up over thee for him that hath power, forsake not thy place (Eccli. X). [Vulg. Eccle. 10:4 has the genitive habentis, "of him that hath power", and demiseris, "leave not"] The place therefore must be held, and the adversary shut out, lest he find a place in our heart, whence: Give not place to the devil (Ephes. IV).

VERS. 32.-- From Benejaacan they came, etc. (HIERON.) The sons of necessity or of gnashing, the twenty-eighth station. Note the number, because if thou begin from one, and adding little by little come as far as the seventh, the twenty-eighth is made.

(ID.) Benejaacan is interpreted the sons of necessity or of gnashing. Of these sons it is said: Bring to the Lord the sons of rams (Psal. XXVIII). But when thou hast been instructed in the divine Scriptures, and hast known that their laws and testimonies 441Care the bonds of charity, and contending with adversaries thou shalt bind them, and lead them bound into captivity, and out of enemies and captives shalt make free men of God; and the sons of gnashing, for fear of the punishment where there is weeping and gnashing of teeth, forsaking the bonds of the devil, will bow their necks to Christ.

(ORIG.) After these things one comes to Benejaacan, which signifies fountains or strainings, where, that is, he draws the fountains of the divine words, until he shall have strained them by drinking, so that he may not pass over even the least commandment, nay rather that not one iota, or one tittle of the word of God be held idle in his understanding.

The mountain. (HIERON.) Gadgad or Galgath is interpreted a messenger, or an action, or a cutting. For we cannot make sons of necessity, unless we have slain 441Dtheir teachers, nor let our hand spare to draw out a shoulder or the tip of an ear from the mouth of the lion, and have announced the rewards to come, and have taught them to be girded for rebelling; whence: Cursed is he that doth the work of God negligently and withholdeth his sword from blood (Jerem. XLVIII). [Vulg. Jer. 48:10 opus Domini fraudulenter, "the work of the Lord deceitfully"] And David: In the morning I put to death all the sinners of the land (Psal. C).

(ORIG.) After these things he comes to Gadgad, which is interpreted a trial, or a thronging. A certain strength in God and a defence is the trial of the soul. For it is so mingled with the virtues that up to that point they seem neither comely nor full, and therefore for those who set out toward virtue various and frequent stations are made among temptations. And when thou hast passed through them, thou shalt come 442Ato Jetebatha: Jetebatha is interpreted good things; to the good therefore one comes not save after the trials of temptations.

Jetebatha. (HIERON.) Goodness, which is Christ: that when we have come to the perfect man, to the priestly degree, and to the age of the fulness of Christ, in which Ezechiel was beside the river Chobar (Ezech. I), we may be able to sing with David in the psalm: O Lord, in thee have I hoped, let me not be confounded for ever (Psal. XXX). For a shepherd, a good one, layeth down his life for his sheep. (Joan. X.) [Vulg. Jo. 10:11 Bonus pastor animam suam dat pro ovibus suis, "The good shepherd giveth his life for his sheep"]

VERS. 34.-- Hebrona. (ID.) A passing over, that is, the world. For the figure of this world passeth away, and the saints coming into it desire to pass over to better things. To this station comes the true Hebrew, that is, a passer over, who can say: Passing over, I shall see this 442Bgreat vision (Exod. III); [Vulg. Ex. 3:3 Vadam, et videbo, "I will go and see"] whence: And they that passed by said not, The blessing of the Lord be upon you. (Psal. CXXVIII.)

(ORIG.) Thence they came to Hebrona, which is a passing over: for all things must be passed over, because even if thou come to good things, thou must pass over to better, until thou come to that good in which thou oughtest always to abide.

VERS. 35.-- At Asiongaber. (HIER.) Asiongaber, the wood of a man. Because, namely, the kinds of woodlands and of all trees can figure the multitude of the nations. Thus far the wilderness of Pharan contains the eighteenth stations, which, described in the catalogue, are not set down in the journey above.

(ORIG.) After these things one comes to Asiongaber, which is interpreted 442Cthe counsels of a man. If anyone has ceased to be a boy in his senses, he arrives at the counsels of a man, like him who said: But when I was made a man, I did away the things that were of a little one (I Cor. XIII). Great therefore are the counsels of a man; whence: Deep water is counsel in the heart of a man. (Prov. XX.)

VERS. 36.-- Into the desert, etc. (HIERON.). It is asked, etc., as far as of this station it is said: The Lord shall shake the desert of Cades.

(ORIG.) Hence again Sin, that is, temptation, because it is not expedient to make this journey otherwise. For the goldsmith, wishing to make a necessary vessel, often puts it to the fire, sets it under the hammers, scrapes it with files, that it may become the more purified, and may come to the form which the craftsman foresees.

(ID.) After these things they came to Pharan Cades, etc., 442Das far as For one dies in the valley, another in the plains, another on the mountain, another on the mountain of the mountain.

(ID.) Thence he arrives at mount Hor, which is interpreted mountainous. For he comes to the mountain of God, that he may become a rich mountain [Vulg. Ps 67:16 mons pinguis, "a fat mountain"], a curdled mountain. Or from this, that he ever dwells on the mountain of God, he may be called mountainous.

VERS. 38.-- Aaron the priest went up into, etc. (ISID.) Aaron died in the same year in which the new people were about to enter the land of promise. And although upon the mountain he left the priesthood to Eleazar, and the law brings those who have fulfilled it to the height; yet that height itself is not beyond the streams of the Jordan, but in the utmost bounds of earthly works. And the people mourn for him thirty days: Aaron is mourned, 443AJesus is not mourned; in the law, a descent to hell; in the Gospel, an ascent to paradise.

VERS. 40.-- And he heard. (ISID.) The Chanaanite also heard that Israel had come, and in the place of the spies, where he had known that the people once gave offence, he joined battle and led Israel away captive. Again in the same place he is stormed, according to the vow the victor is conquered, the conquered prevail. By which we understand that when enemies have taken us captive, destitute of the help of God, we should not despair of salvation, but should fight again. It may come to pass that we conquer where we have been conquered. And the name of that place is called Horma, that is, anathema.

VERS. 41.-- At Salmona. (HIERON.) They pitched their camp at Salmona, etc., as far as they loathe the manna, they are wounded by serpents.

443B(ORIG.) There follows, etc., as far as is Christ, and the Holy Spirit.

(HIERON.) Salmona, a little image, because there the image of the Saviour was expressed by the brazen serpent which hung upon the wood.

VERS. 42.-- Phunon. A mouth, because when we know the passion of the Son of God, what we believe with the heart we utter with the mouth, according to that: With the heart we believe unto justice, etc. (Rom. X)

(ORIG.) Hence one comes to Phunon, which we think to be a sparingness of the mouth; for he who has been able to behold the mystery of Christ and of the Holy Spirit, and has seen or heard the things which it is not lawful for a man to speak, will necessarily have a sparingness of the mouth, knowing to whom, when, or how it behoves him to speak of the divine mysteries.

443CVERS. 43.-- Oboth. (HIERON.) Magicians or pythons, because after the image of God which is shown in the heart by reason, and the confession of faith which is uttered by the mouth, serpents rise up, and evil arts provoke us to wars. But keeping our heart with all watchfulness, let us stop our ears, that we may not hear the voices of enchanters and the songs of the sirens.

(ORIG.) After this one comes to Oboth. And although we have not found the interpretation of its name, we do not doubt that in this also, as in the rest, the sequence of advances is preserved.

VERS. 44.-- In the borders. It signifies according to the letter, that thus far they had been in the borders of the Idumaeans, and now they come to the confines of Moab.

(ORIG.) There follows after these things the station of Gai, that is, 443DChaos: for by this the advance draws near to the bosom of Abraham, who says: Between us and you a great chaos is fixed (Luc. XVI), that he may rest with Lazarus.

At Dibongad. (HIERON.) Dibongad, etc., as far as but on the contrary let us know that a wilderness is set before us.

(ORIG.) Thence one comes again to Dibongad, etc., as far as it may be known how perfectly and piously he thinks concerning God.

VERS. 46.-- Helmondeblathaim. (HIERON.) Which is interpreted, etc., as far as scarcely set free by the prayers of the people.

(ORIG.) After these things one comes to Helmondeblathaim, which is interpreted the contempt of figs, that is, where 444Aearthly things are utterly despised: for unless the things which seem to delight on earth have been despised, we cannot pass over to heavenly things.

VERS. 47.-- They came to mount Abarim over against Nabo, etc. (HIERON.) [Vulg. Num. 33:47 montes Abarim, "the mountains of Abarim"] The forty-first station is turned into the mountains of those who pass over, and it is over against the face of mount Nabo.

(ORIG.) There follows the station of Abarim, which is a passing over, over against Naban, which is a departing. For when the soul has passed through all these virtues, and has ascended to the height of perfection, it now passes over out of the world, and departs like Henoch, who was not found, because God took him. But if it seem to be still in the world, and to dwell in the flesh, yet it is not found in any worldly act, because God has taken it 444Binto the region of the virtues.

(HIERON.) Here Moses dies, the land of promise having first been beheld. Nabo is interpreted a conclusion, in which the law is ended, and the memory of it is not found, but the grace of the Gospel endures without end. And it is to be noted that the station of those who pass over is set among the mountains, and still needs advance: for after the mountain country we go down to the plains of Moab and the streams of the Jordan, which is interpreted a going down. For nothing is so dangerous as the desire of glory and boasting, and a mind puffed up with the consciousness of its virtues.

VERS. 48.-- To the plains of Moab. (ID.) In the forty-second station, etc., as far as and the thorns which choked the sowing of the word of God, of which it is said: I have been turned about in wretchedness, while the 444Cthorn is fastened in me (Psal. XXXI). [Vulg. Ps. 31:4 conversus sum in aerumna mea, dum configitur spina, "I am turned in my anguish, whilst the thorn is fastened"]

(ORIG.) It is the last station, etc., as far as they show the advance of the mind and the increases of the virtues.

VERS. 51.-- You shall have passed over the Jordan, that is, when you shall have been baptized, as it were, when, the old man with his acts being laid aside, you shall have put on the new (Col. III), who is renewed in the knowledge of God.

VERS. 52.-- Destroy ye all. When we enter the holy land of promise through faith, let us extinguish together with idolatry all vices also.

The inhabitants. The malignant spirits, the phantasms of the vices, with their suggestions; whence: Blessed is he that shall take and dash his little ones against the rock (Psal. CXXXVI). [Vulg. Ps. 136:9 parvulos tuos, "thy little ones"]

VERS. 54.-- To the more you shall give the larger portion. Because they who 444Dhave abundance of virtues hope for a breadth of rewards: but they who are content with fewness of virtues receive their recompense according to their merit; whence: He that soweth sparingly shall also reap sparingly; and he that soweth in blessings, of blessings, etc. (II Cor. IX).

CAPUT XXXIV.

Locutusque est Dominus, etc. (ORIG., homil. 1 in Num.) Divinis numeris non omnes digni sunt, etc., usque ad tanquam de capite apostolorum procedebant.

Locutus est ergo Dominus ad Moysen in deserto Sina: illa scilicet quae superius breviter comprehendimus, 445Aubi praecipitur numerari a viginti annis et supra omnis qui procedit in virtute Israel, etc. Si quis ergo procedit in virtute, ille numeratur: nec in qualicunque virtute vel Aegyptiorum, vel Assyriorum, vel Graecorum; sed in virtute Israel, quae a Deo per Scripturas docetur, et per fidem apostolicam et evangelicam traditur.

(ORIG.) Ratio quoque tribuum distinctio ordinum, etc., usque ad ut possit ad Israeliticum numerum pertinere.

VERS. 7.-- Ad septentrionalem, etc. Dicunt Hebraei septentrionalem plagam incipere a mari magno quod Palaestinae, Phoenicis et Syriae (quae appellatur Coele) Ciliciaeque praetendit littoribus, et per Aegyptum tendit ad Libyam. Quod autem dicitur, pervenientes 445Busque ad montem altissimum, autumant significari montem Armanum, vel Taurum, quod verius videtur.

VERS. 9.-- Ibuntque confinia, etc. A fine septentrionalis plagae, id est atrio Hena, tendunt fines usque ad Sephama, quam Hebraei Aphamiam vocant, et de Aphamia descendunt in Reblatha, quae est Antiochia Syriae. Unde addit, contra fontem, quem perspicuum est esse Danem, de quo Antiochia abundantissimis fruitur aquis.

VERS. 13.-- Haec erit terra, etc. (ORIG.) In priori divisione trans Jordanem per Moysen duabus tribubus Ruben et Gad, et dimidiae tribu Manasse terra divisa est, etc., usque ad et ipsis moenibus juncta segregantur.

445C(ID.) Videtur quoque quibusdam, etc., usque ad Hi sunt vere sacris numeris apud Deum numerati, imo quorum omnes numerati sunt capilli (Matth. X).

CHAPTER XXXIV.

And the Lord spoke, etc. (ORIG., homil. 1 in Num.) Not all are worthy of the divine numbers, etc., as far as they proceeded as it were from the head of the apostles.

The Lord therefore spoke to Moses in the desert of Sina: those things, namely, which we have briefly comprehended above, 445Awhere it is commanded that there be numbered, from twenty years and upward, everyone that goeth forth in the strength of Israel, etc. If anyone therefore goes forth in strength, he is numbered: and not in any strength whatsoever, whether of the Egyptians, or of the Assyrians, or of the Greeks; but in the strength of Israel, which is taught by God through the Scriptures, and delivered through the apostolic and evangelical faith.

(ORIG.) The account also of the tribes, the distinction of the orders, etc., as far as that he may be able to belong to the number of Israel.

VERS. 7.-- Toward the northern side, etc. The Hebrews say that the northern quarter begins from the great sea, which stretches before the shores of Palestine, of Phoenice and of Syria (which is called Coele) and of Cilicia, and reaches through Egypt to Libya. But as to what is said, reaching 445Bas far as the highest mountain, they suppose that mount Armanus, or Taurus, is signified, which seems the truer.

VERS. 9.-- And the borders shall go, etc. From the end of the northern quarter, that is, from the court of Hena, the borders stretch as far as Sephama, which the Hebrews call Aphamia, and from Aphamia they go down into Reblatha, which is Antioch of Syria. Whence he adds, over against the fountain, which it is plain is Dan, from which Antioch enjoys most abundant waters.

VERS. 13.-- This shall be the land, etc. (ORIG.) In the former division beyond the Jordan the land was divided by Moses to the two tribes of Ruben and Gad, and to the half tribe of Manasse, etc., as far as and the things joined to the very walls are set apart.

445C(ID.) It seems also to some, etc., as far as These are they who are truly numbered with sacred numbers before God, nay whose hairs are all numbered (Matth. X).

CAPUT XXXV.

VERS. 11.-- Decernite quae urbes. (AUG., quaest. 64 in Num.) Quid est quod ait, etc., usque ad si manifestum factum fuerit in judicio, quod nolens occiderit.

(RAB. in Num. ) Si forte pro conscientia peccatorum civitates terrae promissionis, etc., usque ad quia Sichem ab alienigenis comparata est centum agnis.

VERS. 16.-- Reus, id est aeternae morti obnoxius; unde, Si quis scandalizaverit unum ex pusillis istis qui 445Din me credunt, etc. (Matth. XVIII). Et alibi: Omnis qui oderit fratrem suum, homicida est, nec habet vitam aeternam (I Joan. III) Hic secundum Exodum ab altari jubetur avelli (Exod. XXI), quia indignus sacramentis Dominicis a participatione sacri altaris removetur, 446Anec revocatur ad veniam nisi per condignam poenitentiam.

VERS. 28.-- Postquam autem ille obierit. (GREG., homil. 6 in Ezech.) Post mortem summi pontificis, homicida jubetur reverti in terram suam, quia humanum genus peccando sibi mortem intulit post mortem veri sacerdotis, id est Christi, absolutionem reatus accepit.

VERS. 26.-- Ne polluatis terram habitationis vestrae. Polluit terram habitationis suae qui cruenta et nefaria facit opera, proximos scandalizando et spiritualiter interficiendo. Et nisi per confessionem peccatorum et condignam poenitentiam desideria carnis occidantur, terra corporis illius nullo modo mundabitur, nec Creator omnium in ejus anima habitabit, 446Bubi squalorem sordidae conversationis dominari videbit.

CHAPTER XXXV.

VERS. 11.-- Determine what cities. (AUG., quaest. 64 in Num.) What is it that he says, etc., as far as if it shall have been made manifest in judgment that he killed unwillingly.

(RAB. in Num. ) If perchance, for conscience of sins, the cities of the land of promise, etc., as far as because Sichem was bought from strangers for a hundred lambs.

VERS. 16.-- Guilty, that is, liable to eternal death; whence, If anyone shall scandalize one of these little ones that 445Dbelieve in me, etc. (Matth. XVIII). And elsewhere: Everyone that hateth his brother is a murderer, and hath not eternal life (I Joan. III). Such a one, according to Exodus, is commanded to be torn away from the altar (Exod. XXI), because, being unworthy of the sacraments of the Lord, he is removed from the participation of the sacred altar, 446Aand is not called back to pardon save through worthy penance.

VERS. 28.-- But after he shall be dead. (GREG., homil. 6 in Ezech.) After the death of the high priest the manslayer is commanded to return into his own land, because the human race by sinning brought death upon itself, after the death of the true priest, that is, of Christ, received the absolution of its guilt.

VERS. 26.-- Defile not the land of your habitation. He defiles the land of his habitation who does bloody and wicked works, by scandalizing his neighbours and slaying them spiritually. And unless the desires of the flesh be slain through the confession of sins and worthy penance, the land of his body will in no way be cleansed, nor will the Creator of all dwell in his soul, 446Bwhere he will see the squalor of a filthy manner of life bearing rule.

CAPUT XXXVI.

VERS. 1.-- Accesserunt autem et principes, etc. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Providere debent piae cogitationes, etc., usque ad ne si prava intentione gerantur, ab immundis spiritibus pervadantur; unde: Attendite ne justitiam vestram faciatis coram hominibus (Matth. VI).

VERS. 6.-- Nubant. Simile dicit Apostolus: Cui vult nubat, tantum in Domino (I Cor. VII), id est, viro catholico, ne possessio scilicet bonorum operum et rectae fidei perdatur, si gentili aut Judaeo nupserit.

Tropologice vero mulieri, id est, piae intentioni, 446Cper bonam actionem, cui voluerit, licet se conjungere labori, sic tamen ut pietatem et religionem in omnibus conservet, ne operis sui pro quo aeternae haereditatis possessionem sperat, detrimentum patiatur, si alieno, id est terreno se conjunxerit.

(RAB.) Hoc fit cum quilibet de numero credentium Judaeorum doctori de gentibus credenti se jungit, ut ejus magisterio filios gignat, id est pietatis opera. Nomine etiam patrui dici possunt de gentibus electi, quia fratres sunt sanctorum praedicatorum, qui fuerunt in Judaea constituti. Non debet esse schisma in spiritali cognatione, ubi una est fides in professione. Et sicut est unus pastor, debet esse unum ovile: Omnes enim fratres sumus (Joan. X; Matth. XXIII; II Cor. XI).

446DQui humilitatem habere studet, et in infirmitatibus suis complacet; sibi solemnitatem agit, et grata munera Deo tribuit, et rex suae conversationis existit, et progressu virtutum movetur, ambulans de virtute in virtutem.

CHAPTER XXXVI.

VERS. 1.-- And there came also the princes, etc. (RAB. in Num., tom. II.) Pious thoughts ought to provide, etc., as far as lest, if they be carried on with a perverse intention, they be invaded by unclean spirits; whence: Take heed that you do not your justice before men (Matth. VI).

VERS. 6.-- Let them marry. The Apostle says the like: Let her marry whom she will, only in the Lord (I Cor. VII), that is, a catholic husband, lest, namely, the possession of good works and of right faith be lost, if she marry a Gentile or a Jew.

But tropologically it is lawful for the woman, that is, for the pious intention, 446Cto join herself through a good action to whatever labour she will, yet so that she keep piety and religion in all things, lest she suffer the loss of her work, for which she hopes for the possession of the eternal inheritance, if she join herself to a stranger, that is, to an earthly thing.

(RAB.) This is done when anyone out of the number of believing Jews joins himself to a believing teacher from among the Gentiles, that by his teaching he may beget sons, that is, works of piety. Under the name of an uncle also the elect from among the Gentiles may be called, because they are brothers of the holy preachers who were established in Judaea. There ought to be no schism in spiritual kinship, where there is one faith in profession. And as there is one shepherd, there ought to be one sheepfold: For we are all brethren (Joan. X; Matth. XXIII; II Cor. XI).

446DHe who studies to have humility, and is well pleased in his own infirmities; keeps a solemnity for himself, and renders acceptable gifts to God, and stands forth as king of his own manner of life, and is moved by the advance of the virtues, walking from virtue to virtue.

Cruces for this work — where the plate is defective or the reading uncertain →

The Latin is Migne's printing of Liber Numeri (PL 113, coll. 379B–446D), by Anselm of Laon and his school, from the Corpus Corporum transcription. Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself. Every gilt column mark is an address: migne.app/pl/113/379b resolves to the first.

Du bon, à bon marché — carried to its limit: the good, free.
